# Changelog

All notable changes to RustConn will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [0.26.4] - 2026-11-25

### Added
- **Remmina SSH tunnels become Jump Hosts on import (#449, #450)** — an RDP, VNC or SPICE profile with *Enable SSH tunnel* is imported with a saved SSH connection built from `ssh_tunnel_server`, `ssh_tunnel_username` and `ssh_tunnel_privatekey` (key file, password or agent by `ssh_tunnel_auth`), linked as its Jump Host. Profiles that share server, user and port reuse one SSH connection, also across files. The tunnel password is not imported. A tunnel that cannot be mapped is imported without it and listed as a warning in the import summary. When **Skip Duplicates** drops a bastion that already exists, the kept profiles are linked to the existing connection instead of a missing one. The import count includes the created SSH connections. (`rustconn-core/src/import/remmina.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/import/traits.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/import/mod.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **SecureCRT-inspired script examples** — eleven new bundled `.rsc` scripts: TDR cable test, the destructive erase-device script, command capture, rename-tab from the CLI prompt, three read-only BGP peer captures (Cisco IOS, Huawei VRP, Juniper Junos), offline OUI lookup, and three guarded write scripts (global config, DHCP relay, interface description) that stay in check mode until the literal `APPLY` is typed. `example-cisco-erase-device` needs both the hostname and the word `ERASE`: `if_goto` now substitutes a braced `${name}` in its regex (regex-escaped), so the typed hostname is compared with the device's own. The new `caption` and `oui` verbs (the latter resolves against a built-in offline IEEE MA-L table of 40 559 assignments, generated by `scripts/gen-oui-table.py`; 28/36-bit MA-M/MA-S/IAB blocks are not matched), their in-app reference text and tests are included. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/script_examples.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/oui.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Device reports in the CLI** — `rustconn-cli report <kind>` collects one read-only `show` command across selected SSH connections and prints all parsed rows as table, JSON or CSV. It finishes the previously orphaned report command, adds 17 further report kinds (interface stats, next-hop, EIGRP, switchport, merged ARP, MAC-by-VLAN, document-device, six AireOS WLC collectors and BGP peers for Cisco IOS, Huawei VRP and Juniper Junos), and keeps the original 8 parser tests plus new width checks. Real AireOS vWLC 8.7 output is pinned as fixtures for the AireOS collectors; the IOS parsers are tested against hand-written, sanitised samples. Every device runs non-interactively (`ssh -n -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=10 -- <host>`), so a password prompt or an unknown host key fails that host instead of blocking the batch; a failed host is named on stderr (`failed: <host>` with `--quiet`) and the exit code is non-zero, and a saved host starting with `-` is refused. (`rustconn-cli/src/commands/report.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/exec.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)

### Fixed

- **VNC ignored its Jump Host and connected directly (#450)** — a VNC connection started from the launch path that already had its credentials never opened the SSH tunnel, so an address that only exists behind the bastion (such as `127.0.0.1:9336` on the jump host) failed with "Connection refused". The embedded and the external viewer now go through the connection's Jump Host, including a group-inherited one and a fallback, like RDP. The tunnel is opened off the GTK thread, closes with the tab; a failure shows a toast and a failed entry in the history instead of a silent "failed" status, and a jump host that cannot be read stops the launch instead of connecting directly. (`rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`)
- **Editing a cluster left the cluster list empty (#451)** — after pressing Save in the cluster editor the list was refreshed while the application state was still mutably borrowed, so the list provider could not read it and showed the empty state. The borrow is now released before the list refreshes, as delete already did. (`rustconn/src/window/clusters.rs`)
- **Script logging aborted the app with "RefCell already borrowed"** — every script that used `log` (the save-config examples, command capture, the BGP captures) asked the engine for its held-back last log line from inside a host callback, while `pump` still held the engine's mutable borrow, so the process aborted. The engine now flushes that line itself and passes the bytes to `set_log`, which no longer touches the engine. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/script_runner.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **clap 4.6.9 → 4.6.10**, **constant_time_eq 0.6.1 → 0.6.2**, **crc32fast 1.5.5 → 1.5.6**, **h2 0.4.20 → 0.4.21**, **toml 1.2.2 → 1.2.3**, **zerocopy 0.8.64 → 0.8.65**, **zlib-rs 0.6.8 → 0.6.9**, **zstd 0.14.4 → 0.14.5**, **zstd-safe 8.3.0 → 8.4.0**, **zstd-sys 2.1.3 → 2.2.0** (bundles zstd 1.6.0)

## [0.26.3] - 2026-11-24

### Fixed
- **Sidebar status stuck on "connecting" when a client is missing or login is refused** — a Telnet, Mosh, Serial or other terminal connection whose client program is not installed (`command not found`) showed the "not installed" banner but left the sidebar row on the connecting icon for good, because a command that never started never exits to mark the row failed. The same happened to an embedded VNC attempt refused with `Authentication failure`. Both now turn the row to the failed icon.
- **Embedded RDP to a Windows host with NLA never asked for the password** — a connection with no stored password tries without one first, so that a server with its own login screen (xrdp) can show it. A Windows host that requires Network Level Authentication refuses such an attempt with `server requires Enhanced RDP Security with CredSSP`, which ended the connection as failed with no prompt. That refusal now opens the password dialog.
- **Endless auto-reconnect against a host that accepts and closes the session** — auto-reconnect waits for the host to answer a port probe, and the 5-second rapid-crash guard measured from the tab's creation, not from the reconnect. A server that accepted every connection and closed it a few seconds later (seen with Telnet) was reconnected at once, forever, and the sidebar flipped between connected and failed. Auto-reconnect now stops after the connection's retry limit of consecutive reconnects that each ended within 30 seconds; **Reconnect** still works by hand.
- **macOS: passwords saved in the Keychain could not be read back** — the Keychain backend stores the username, password and domain as separate items and treated a missing item as "not found" only when the error text contained `-25300` or `not found`. macOS words it "The specified item could not be found in the keychain.", so for every connection without a domain the lookup of the absent domain item failed and the whole read returned an error. Missing items are now recognised by the `errSecItemNotFound` status code.
- **CLI on macOS: `secret status` reported the keyring as unavailable** — it checked for the Linux `secret-tool` and printed "Not available", while the macOS backend is the Keychain. It now reports **Keyring (Keychain): Available**, and `secret get` points to Keychain Access instead of `secret-tool`.
- **macOS: a connection with no KeePassXC entry reported the database as unreadable** — `keepassxc-cli`'s messages were pinned to English through `LC_MESSAGES`, which Qt on macOS ignores in favour of the system language, so a missing entry came back translated (for example "Неможливо знайти запис…"), was not recognised, and the connection showed "Could not read the password from KeePassXC" after three retries instead of asking for the password. When `show` fails with an unrecognised message, a `db-info` with the same unlock factors now decides: if the database opens, the entry is missing. A YubiKey unlock is not probed, since it would ask for a second touch.
- **"Open Settings" from the password-store dialog opened the first page** — the dialog tells the user to look under Secrets, but Settings opened on Terminal. It now opens on Secrets, and the connection's sidebar row no longer stays on the connecting icon after that choice.
- **Mosh, Serial and Kubernetes connections stayed on the connecting icon** — their connect paths armed the "established" detector only for session recording, never for the sidebar status, so a working session kept the yellow connecting icon until it closed. They now turn the row connected the way Telnet and every in-place reconnect already did.
- **WinRM reconnected in a loop after a refused sign-in** — evil-winrm exits 1 with `WinRM::WinRMAuthorizationError` when the service rejects the account, and the host still answers the port probe, so automatic reconnection retried the same refused sign-in every 20 seconds. That message within the first minute of a session now counts as an authentication failure, the way a refused ssh login already did: no automatic reconnect, and the cached password is dropped so **Reconnect** reads it again.
- **Client version probes hung for their full timeout** — the probes that ask an installed client for its version (`telnet`, `remote-viewer`, `xfreerdp`, `sdl-freerdp` and the rest) inherited RustConn's stdin. Started from a terminal, `telnet` (which has no version flag and opens its own prompt) and GTK/X11 clients blocked reading it, so each probe ran into its 6-second limit, the client list was slow to fill, and the test suite stalled once those clients were installed. The probes now run with stdin closed and answer in well under a second.
- **Terminal search GUI test still failed on CI** — the 0.26.2 fix waited for VTE to parse the fed text, but the test kept failing in the Xvfb job (VTE 0.84.0, GTK 4.22): it searched after a fixed 600 ms pause, while the search entry runs the search from a debounce timer. The test now waits up to 10 s for the selection, and on failure prints the bar's labels (the status among them) and the terminal text, so the next failure says whether the search ran. The search code is unchanged. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/terminal_search.rs`)

### Documentation
- **macOS: protocol client tools documented** — `docs/MACOS_BUILD.md` lists the Homebrew packages for Telnet, FreeRDP, Mosh, TigerVNC and picocom, the `vncviewer` link the TigerVNC cask needs, `evil-winrm` for WinRM through Homebrew's Ruby, and how to build the SPICE viewer `remote-viewer` from the `jeffreywildman/virt-manager` tap, whose upstream download URL is dead.
- **WinRM: refused local administrator explained** — the user guide says why Windows answers a local administrator other than the built-in one with `WinRMAuthorizationError` (UAC remote restrictions) and how to allow it with `LocalAccountTokenFilterPolicy`. The CLI reference names the Keychain as the `keyring` backend on macOS.
### Dependencies
- **hyper 1.12.0 → 1.13.0**
## [0.26.2] - 2026-11-23

### Added

- **Proton Pass secret backend (issue #444)** — Settings → Secrets gains **Proton Pass**, which stores and reads connection credentials through the official Proton Pass CLI (`pass-cli`). Each connection is one login item titled `rustconn/<name>` in a vault named **RustConn**, created on the first save; the Windows domain goes in a `domain` text field. Sign in once with `pass-cli login` (the **Sign In** button opens it in a terminal); RustConn never sees a Proton credential and reuses the CLI's own session, and a missing session is reported as such. Passwords reach `pass-cli` only over stdin, as a login template: `item update` takes values on the command line only, so a save writes a fresh item and removes the one it replaces only after the new one exists, and a field the save leaves out is carried over. **Read-only mode** and **Search all vaults** (which also matches items titled by host or connection name) work as on the other CLI backends. `rustconn-cli secret get|set|delete --backend proton-pass` and `secret status` cover it too. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/proton_pass.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs`)
- **Proton Pass CLI in Flatpak Components** — Menu → **Components…** offers the Proton Pass CLI under Password Manager CLIs, so the Proton Pass backend works in the Flatpak (the Snap lists it too, untested there). The installer reads Proton's release manifest (`versions.json`, format 1), downloads the Linux build for the machine's architecture from `proton.me` and refuses it unless its SHA-256 matches the manifest. In the Flatpak, RustConn runs `pass-cli` with `PROTON_PASS_LINUX_KEYRING=dbus`, because the sandbox blocks the kernel keyring `pass-cli` keeps its session key in by default; the key goes to the Secret Service instead. Settings → Secrets detects the downloaded CLI. In a sandbox the **Sign In** button no longer looks for a host terminal (there is none in reach, and its `pass-cli` would keep a separate session): it opens a dialog that runs `pass-cli login` in a terminal inside RustConn, with a clickable sign-in link, refreshes the status when the login exits and closes on success. (`rustconn-core/src/cli_download/install_custom.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/proton_pass.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/detection.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/proton_login.rs`)

### Fixed

- **Proton Pass hardening** — a vault name, or a vault or item id printed by `pass-cli`, that starts with `-` (it would be read as a flag) or holds a line break is refused or dropped instead of being passed as an argument, and the Sign In dialog opens a clicked link only when it is `https://` on `proton.me`. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/proton_pass.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/proton_login.rs`)
- **Review fixes for Proton Pass and the wizard** — `pass-cli` that exits before reading the password template now reports its own error (for example "Not logged in") instead of a broken-pipe message, and the stdin write counts toward the 30 s limit. A whole Proton Pass save, read or delete is capped at 90 s. A failed `item create` no longer repeats `pass-cli`'s stderr, which can quote the password. A save tries to remove every replaced item even when one removal fails. The password template is built in a pre-sized wiped buffer. The Sign In dialog refuses links with a backslash or user info, which browsers read as another host. In the connection wizard, **Next** follows the typed host again after the protocol changes, and a SPICE socket connection no longer keeps a jump host. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/proton_pass.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/proton_login.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/`)
- **Connection wizard: a SPICE connection with a unix socket path no longer asks for a host** — **Next** is enabled when the **Socket Path** row is filled, and the connection editor accepts such a connection on save (it used to answer "Host is required"). Other protocols still need a host. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/connection_page.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/builders.rs`)
- **Flaky terminal search test** — the GUI test of the search bar fed text to the terminal and waited a fixed 300 ms, but VTE parses it later, so on a slow runner the search saw an empty buffer. The test now waits until all lines are parsed. The search code is unchanged. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/terminal_search.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **yuv 0.8.20 → 0.8.21**, **zip 9.8.1 → 9.8.2**

## [0.26.1] - 2026-11-23

### Added

- **Quick terminal theme picker** — right-click a terminal tab and choose **Terminal Theme…**, or run **Terminal Theme…** from the command palette, then click a theme: the terminal repaints at once. It changes only that session (the focused pane in a split) and is not saved: reconnecting or saving Settings > Terminal brings back the configured theme. (`rustconn/src/window/theme_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`)
- **Drag a whole split out into its own window** — a tab that hosts a split, dragged outside every window or onto another RustConn window, now takes the whole split along: the new window shows the layout with every pane, and all sessions keep running (no reconnect). The pane buttons are hidden there, because they act on the main window; move the split back with the window's **Move to Main Window** button or by dragging its tab to the main window to add, close or pop out panes. Closing the window (or its tab) ends every session of the split. Before, such a drop was refused with "Remove the split layout from this tab…" and an empty window opened and closed. The tab title is not refreshed while the split is in the other window. (`rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/detach.rs`)
- **Drag tabs between windows, or out into a new one — [PR #443](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/443), contributed by @roms2000** — a tab dropped where no RustConn window is opens a new tabbed window holding that session, and a tab dropped anywhere on another RustConn window — its tab bar, its title, or its content — moves there: at the drop position on a tab bar, last otherwise. It works from the main window to a tabbed or cluster window, back, and between two tabbed windows, and the only tab of a window can be dragged too: the window it leaves closes. The session moves through the same paths as **Move to New Window** and **Move Tab to Main Window**, so nothing reconnects, and a tab that cannot leave the main window (an external viewer) stays and says why. A tab dropped into a window with **Tab Broadcast** on turns that broadcast off, so keystrokes never reach a session the user did not pick; cluster membership is kept wherever the tab goes. A tab let go by mistake outside every window also opens a window: drag it back onto the window it came from. On Wayland GTK reports every drop nothing accepted as an error rather than as a drop outside every window, so libadwaita never opened the window there; RustConn now tracks which of its windows the drag is over and opens the window itself. The tab bar of a tabbed window no longer sits in the window's drag handle, so dragging a tab no longer moves the window with it. (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_transfer.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`)
- **Drag a tab onto an empty split panel to put it there** — a tab from the tab bar dropped on an empty pane of the split you are looking at moves its session into that pane, as **Select Tab** does, instead of opening a new window. A pane that already shows a session, and a tab that holds a split itself, refuse the drop: the tab stays and a warning appears. Tabs dragged from another window are not handled. (`rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_transfer.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`)
- **Template editor: printer redirection and session toolbar** — the RDP page of the template editor gains **Printer Redirection** and **Session Toolbar**, and the VNC page gains **Session Toolbar**, worded as in the connection editor. All three are saved with the template, loaded back when it is edited, and carried into a connection created from it; before, a template always saved printing as off and the toolbar as on. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/template.rs`)
- **Connection wizard: SPICE unix socket path** — the wizard shows a **Socket Path** row for SPICE connections. Leave it empty for a network connection. **Duplicate via Wizard…** keeps the socket path of the source connection, which the wizard used to drop. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/connection_page.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/mod.rs`)

### Changed

- **Settings > Interface > System Tray: the on/off switch is on the row itself** — it used to sit inside the collapsed row under **Show icon**, so it was hard to find. Expanding the row now shows only **Minimize to tray**, which is greyed out while the tray is off. Stored values are unchanged. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)
- **Monitoring says which protocols it supports** — the hint in **Settings > Monitoring** and in the connection editor's **Remote Monitoring** group now states that metrics work for SSH sessions only, not for RDP, VNC, Telnet or other protocols. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/monitoring_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/advanced_tab.rs`)
- **Connection list menu has no "Tools" heading** — the three management entries no longer carry a title the other sections lack. (`rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`)
- **The window no longer waits for the keyring, ssh-agent or the example scripts at start** — the KeePass, 1Password, Passbolt and portable-store secrets kept in the system keyring were read before the window existed, up to 5 s each when the Secret Service was locked or slow. They are now read in parallel on a thread once the window is shown. A connection started before they arrive waits for them, so it still resolves its credentials; other screens see the secret as not loaded, as after a failed read. `ssh-agent` (started when `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` is unset) and the bundled example scripts also load off the main thread, and every launch that uses the agent waits for it. `RUST_LOG=rustconn::perf=info` now prints the `op="startup"` lines with `elapsed_ms` per phase. (`rustconn/src/state/startup_secrets.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/startup_gate.rs`)
- **Terminal theme picker shows the current theme** — the session's theme is checked, **Follow System** is translated, and the picker also works from group and detached windows. (`rustconn/src/window/theme_actions.rs`)
- **Removed unused `rustconn-core` API** — `SessionManager`, `Session`, `SessionState`, the `HealthCheck*` types, the `Connection::with_*/has_*/get_*/set_*` helpers, the RouterOS re-exports in `monitoring` and several `rdp_client` builders had no callers and are gone. Library users of `rustconn-core` must drop them.

### Fixed

- **Closing the tab of a split that was moved to its own window asks first** — the tab's close button (and **Ctrl+Shift+W**) in that window ended every session of the split with no question, and when it was the window's only tab, the window closed with it. It now asks, as the main window does: **Cancel** keeps everything running, **Close All Sessions** ends the owner and the guests once. Tabs with one session close as before. (`rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`)
- **A split moved to its own window asks before it closes, and its tab follows the focused pane** — closing that window counted one tab, so a split with several sessions ended them without asking; the question now counts every pane. The tab was named once, when the split moved; it now follows the focused pane as it does in the main window. (`rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`)
- **A connection through a saved jump host, started right after launch, uses the secrets read from the keyring** — the jump host's password is looked up on the main thread at launch and could run before the startup keyring reads finished, so it fell back to asking. Such a connection (SSH, SFTP, RDP, VNC) now waits for the reads on a worker thread first, even with a key or cached target login. (`rustconn/src/window/credentials.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`)
- **Terminal search is a bar at the top of the terminal, not a modal sheet — issue [#442](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/442), reported by @thiagoojack** — the search no longer blocks the session: the terminal stays scrollable and selectable while it is open. **Enter** goes to the next match, **Shift+Enter** to the previous one, **Esc** closes the bar and returns focus to the terminal. The bar pushes the terminal down by its own height, so it never covers a match. Case sensitive, Regex and Highlight All work as before, and the bar works in tabs, split panes and detached or session-group windows. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/terminal_search.rs`)
- **Save & Connect in the wizard no longer asks for the password you just typed** — the password was written to the vault on a worker thread while the connect started at once and read the vault, so a slow backend (KeePassXC took about 3 s) answered "not found" and a password prompt opened. The wizard now hands the typed password to the session cache for that first connect. (`rustconn/src/window/wizard.rs`)
- **Changing the user name of a connection now takes effect** — a connection with a saved password kept logging in as the name stored in the vault entry, so after you edited the name (for example `user-01` to `user-00`) every protocol still used the old one. The connection's own user name now wins over the vault entry's; an empty user name still uses the vault's, and a group's user name on an inherited password still wins. A vault entry saved as `DOMAIN\same-user` still supplies the domain. The vault entry itself is not rewritten. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`)
- **An edited user name is used even while the session credential cache is live** — reconnecting within 5 minutes of editing the name still logged in as the cached one, and the cached domain of the old user followed it. The connection's own user name now wins in every place the cache is read. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`)
- **Another user's vault domain no longer applies to an edited user name** — a vault entry saved for a different user no longer lends its domain to the connection. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`)
- **Keyring secrets of a backend you switched away from during start-up are not kept** — only the secrets of the configured backends are applied. (`rustconn/src/state/startup_secrets.rs`)
- **Terminal search bar no longer leaks the terminal after it closes** — closing the bar left a reference cycle that kept the terminal and its PTY alive; closing also restores the overlay's previous top margin instead of resetting it to 0. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/terminal_search.rs`)
- **Esc while dragging a tab no longer opens a window** — cancelling a drag of a lone tab over the desktop used to open a new window as if it had been dropped. (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_transfer.rs`)
- **A connect right after launch no longer freezes the window for a slow `ssh-agent`** — the GTK thread waits for the agent only when no socket is known at all; worker threads still wait. (`rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`)
- **A split moved between window groups is titled after its focused pane** (`rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`)

### Dependencies

- **ashpd 0.14.2 → 0.14.3**, **hashbrown 0.17.1 → 0.17.2**

## [0.26.0] - 2026-11-21

### Added
- **Duplicate via Wizard… in the tab menu (#437)** — right-click a tab of a saved connection and choose **Duplicate via Wizard…** to open the connection wizard prefilled from that connection, with a new name. It uses the tab's own connection, not the sidebar selection. (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)
- **Built-in script recording (Phase 0 of #373) — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — the terminal right-click menu gains **Record Script…**, which captures the keystrokes you type in a session and writes them to `~/.config/rustconn/scripts/<connection>-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>.rsc` as readable, diffable text (a `-1`, `-2` suffix prevents overwriting an earlier recording). Stopping is the same menu item, relabelled **Stop Script Recording**, and reports the step count and path in a toast. The file is line-based (`send` / `wait` verbs) and each `send` carries the existing `KeySequence` syntax, so `{ENTER}`, `{WAIT:500}` and `${var}` mean the same thing in a script as they do in a connection's key sequence — one grammar, not two. The recorder lives in `rustconn-core` (`automation::script`) and emits the same `.rsc` source the existing script engine parses, so a recorded file opens in the Scripts dialog unchanged. The action is also in the connection menu of the sidebar for a connected host with one session, where it targets that connection. It is deliberately **not** in the app menu: it targets "the session you are looking at", which is the same ambiguity that keeps `execute-snippet` out of it, and here the cost of guessing wrong is a file on disk full of one host's commands. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/script_recorder.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/script_actions.rs`)
- **Read-only session sharing — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — **Share Session (read-only)** in the command palette, the terminal/tab menu and the connection menu starts a local HTTP/SSE relay for the focused terminal and gives you a link that works until you stop sharing. The default binds `127.0.0.1`; an **Access** list can bind one chosen local IPv4/IPv6 interface instead, with a warning that a network link is plain HTTP and the token is the only protection. Viewers see the session in the vendored xterm.js terminal, so colours, cursor addressing and full-screen programs like `atop` render correctly; there is no input path and no upload. The token is compared in constant time and is never written to a log or to the page body. The relay stops from the dialog. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/session/share_assets/xterm.min.js` (MIT), `rustconn/src/window/network_tools.rs`)
- **Read-only session sharing in the browser and in another RustConn over WebTransport — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — the **Share Session** dialog gains **Internet — WebTransport in a browser**: a public address discovered with STUN and shown in a dropdown (several servers produce several entries), plus an `https://…` link that opens the session directly in Chrome/Edge 100+, Firefox 125+ or Safari 26.4+. The page is served over TCP-TLS with a per-share ECDSA P-256 self-signed certificate; the browser shows a certificate warning, and the WebTransport (HTTP/3 over QUIC/UDP) connection is then pinned to that certificate by the SHA-256 carried in the link fragment. The browser path is trust on first use: the page loads before there is anything to pin, so someone who sits between the viewer and the sharer on that first load can serve a forged page that reads the token and the pin from the link. Another RustConn never loads the page and checks the pin itself. The listener keeps its NAT mapping alive with binding requests until a viewer connects, and hints at forwarding the TCP and UDP ports when the router filters inbound packets. Full-cone UDP or forwarded ports work; restricted/symmetric NAT, CGNAT and a local firewall that drops inbound traffic need a relay or a rule, which this mode does not provide. The same link also opens in another RustConn via command palette → **Join Shared Session…**, which shows a native read-only terminal and needs only the UDP port, not the page or its TCP port. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share_quic.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/network_tools.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Read-only TFTP server — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — **Start TFTP Server** in the command palette and the terminal/tab menu serves a folder you pick over TFTP RRQ. Choose port 6969 (unprivileged) or 69 (standard TFTP, privileged on Linux/macOS/FreeBSD) and the interface a device will reach (loopback or one local IPv4/IPv6 address); a bind on 69 without the rights explains how to grant them instead of failing silently. Octet mode, `blksize`/`tsize` OACK and retransmission; paths are taken relative to the chosen root, where a leading `/` means the root itself (RouterOS requests rooted paths), while `..`, NUL bytes and symlinks escaping the root are refused. Only one server runs at a time and it stops from the dialog. This is for pulling configuration dumps from serial-attached network gear. (`rustconn-core/src/transfer/tftp.rs`)
- **Remote file editor — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — **Edit Remote File** in the command palette, the terminal/tab menu and the connection menu downloads a file over `sftp` into a temporary copy, opens it in your default text editor, and uploads it when you confirm. It uses the connection's own SSH settings (key, jump host chain, ProxyCommand, custom options, agent socket). Key or agent authentication only: a password is never placed on the command line or in the batch file. Hosts without an SFTP subsystem fall back to `scp -O`. Limits are in the User Guide. (`rustconn-core/src/remote_editor.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/network_tools/remote_edit.rs`)

### Fixed
- **Pre-connect task, port knock and SPA no longer freeze the window** — they ran on the main loop, so a slow task, a knock sequence or a vault read of the SPA keys (up to 45 seconds per key on a CLI-backed vault) stopped the whole window. They now run on a worker thread and the session starts when they finish; a failure shows its toast and marks the connection as failed, as before. (`rustconn/src/window/session_start.rs`)
- **WebTransport sharing without STUN listens on one address** — declining the public-address lookup used to bind the share to every interface. It now binds to the local network address (or loopback when there is none); only **Look Up Public Address** uses the wildcard address a NAT mapping needs. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`)
- **TFTP server: safer file access and log lines** — the file is checked again after it is opened, so a symlink swapped into the served folder in between cannot leak a file from outside it, and a requested file name is logged escaped so control characters cannot forge log lines. (`rustconn-core/src/transfer/tftp.rs`)
- **Duplicate via Wizard… shows a new name and keeps the group (#437)** — the wizard opened with the source's name and saved the copy at the top level of the tree. It now suggests `name (copy)` (made unique) and the copy stays in the source's group. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **The custom SSH agent socket in Settings now works, and the row is back (F-01)** — the setting was saved but no launch read it, so the row was hidden. **Settings → Secrets → SSH Agent → Custom Socket** now applies to every `ssh`, `sftp`, `scp`, `ssh-add` and file-manager launch: terminal SSH, SFTP and Midnight Commander, the key unlock and add paths, standalone tunnels, the monitoring bar, remote-file edit, remote recording copy, the agent status page and `rustconn-cli connect` and `sftp`. One function picks the socket: the connection's own socket, then its group's, then this setting, then the agent RustConn started, then `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`. A leading `~/` is expanded. The path is checked as you type (absolute, exists, is a socket, owned by you) and the row says what is wrong; a launch with a bad path also prints a warning in the terminal tab instead of silently using another agent. The Remote file editor now also honours a socket inherited from a group, and tunnels, monitoring and the recording copy no longer ignore a socket RustConn had to start itself. (`rustconn-core`, `rustconn`, `rustconn-cli`)
- **Terminal search: Highlight All now shows the matches, and the search sheet no longer covers them (#441, #442)** — "Highlight All" only registered a VTE hover regex, which underlines text under the mouse pointer and nothing else, so ticking it changed nothing visible; every visible match is now tinted on the layer the highlight rules already use. The search regex was also compiled without the PCRE2 multiline flag that `vte_terminal_search_set_regex()` requires (VTE 0.84 logged a runtime-check warning and ignored the regex), and closing or retyping the search called `match_remove_all()`, which also dropped the URL and highlight-rule hover matches. The search dialog is now a bottom sheet, and the terminal gives up the sheet's height while it is open, so a match on the last row stays visible. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/terminal_search.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/highlight_overlay.rs`)
- **Remote editor no longer loses an edit when the upload fails** — the edited copy and its temporary folder are kept. The error dialog offers **Retry**, **Save a Copy…** and **Discard**, and Escape keeps the copy. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/remote_edit.rs`)
- **Remote editor no longer freezes the window** — download, upload and the copy run on a worker thread. `sftp`/`scp` run with `BatchMode=yes` and the shared `ConnectTimeout`, stop after 120 seconds, and a download is cut off at 16 MiB. (`rustconn-core/src/remote_editor.rs`)
- **Remote editor refuses hostile hosts** — a host, user name or jump host that starts with `-` or contains whitespace or control characters (for example `-oProxyCommand=…` from an imported connection) is refused, and `--` ends the options before the destination. (`rustconn-core/src/remote_editor.rs`)
- **Remote editor scp fallback and paths** — the `scp -O` fallback is used only when the server has no SFTP subsystem, so a missing file or a refused login shows the real error. Its path check is now an allow-list, `* ? [` in `sftp` paths are escaped, the temporary copy is mode 0600 with a sanitized file name, and it opens with the default `text/plain` handler. (`rustconn-core/src/remote_editor.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/network_tools/remote_edit.rs`)
- **Key sequences containing `{`, `}` or `$` no longer corrupt on `Display` — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — `KeyElement::Display` escaped those characters with `write!(f, "{{")`, but `{{` is *format-string* syntax for a single literal `{`, so each escaped brace was emitted **once** and the `Display` → `parse` round trip was lossy. A stored `key_sequence` containing braces printed back into an unparseable string — `{ENTER}` in literal text degraded into an actual Enter keypress. The escaping now goes through `f.write_str("{{")`, and a new property test (`key_sequence_round_trip_escaped_text`) generates exactly the characters every previous round-trip test filtered out, so the gap that hid this cannot reopen. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/key_sequence.rs`, `rustconn-core/tests/properties/key_sequence_tests.rs`)
- **Automated sends are never written into a recorded script** — a script recording listens to VTE's `commit`, which also fires for bytes RustConn itself writes. Automatic logins would therefore have recorded `${password}` to disk in clear text. Every send RustConn originates (key sequences, macros, snippets, and both the immediate and delayed expect-rule responses) now runs inside a thread-local `suppress_recording` guard, entered at the moment the bytes are written so that delayed sends are covered too. (`rustconn/src/terminal/script_recorder.rs`, `rustconn/src/automation.rs`)
- **Save-config scripts no longer close the log on a stale prompt — [PR #440](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/440), contributed by @shafiev** — `expect` could match a prompt left in the engine buffer by a previous command, so the bundled `example-cisco-save-config` (and the other vendor profiles) stopped `log` before `show running-config` printed anything and saved a two-line transcript. `send` now discards buffered output, starting a fresh window for the command it runs; `expect` still matches output that was on screen when the script started, because only a `send` drops the seed. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/script.rs`)
- **Idle or slow sockets could exhaust the share relay** — the relay had no read timeout and no limits. It now gives a client 10 seconds to send its request, closes sockets past 64 open connections, answers `503` past 8 live viewers, drops a viewer that does not read for 15 seconds, and frees a viewer's slot as soon as it disconnects. Stopping a share also ends sockets still waiting to send a request. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share.rs`)
- **A slow viewer could corrupt its own screen** — a viewer that fell behind skipped output silently, which could cut an escape sequence in half. It now receives a `reset` event; the page clears its terminal and shows a notice. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share.rs`)
- **TFTP transfers of an exact block multiple now end** — a file whose size was a multiple of the block size (512, 1024 B, …) never sent the final empty block, so the client waited until it timed out. The server also replied with the wrong ERROR opcode (7 instead of 5), now fixed. Each request is served from its own port in its own task (up to 8 at once), so one silent or spoofed request no longer blocks the server for seconds, and files over 32 MiB are refused with an ERROR. Choosing `$HOME` or a folder that holds `.ssh` shows a "Share this folder?" warning first, and the two port labels are translatable. (`rustconn-core/src/transfer/tftp.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/network_tools/tftp.rs`)
- **Script recording keeps no-echo input off disk** — while a program reads a password (the terminal's `ECHO` flag is off, as for `sudo`, `ssh` or `passwd`), the keystrokes are not stored; the script gets `send ${password}` and `{ENTER}` instead, so the replay asks you for a `password` variable. A password typed at a prompt that echoes is still recorded as typed. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/script_recorder.rs`, `rustconn-pty-sys/src/lib.rs`)
- **Script recorder no longer writes escape-sequence garbage** — arrow keys, function keys, bracketed-paste markers and terminal replies (`ESC [ 1;1 R`) were recorded as text such as `[A`. CSI, SS3 and OSC sequences are now dropped, Backspace edits the line being typed, Tab is recorded as a `{TAB}` step, and the `.rsc` file is written atomically with mode 0600 because it can hold typed passwords. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/script_recorder.rs`)
- **Idle or flooding sockets could exhaust the WebTransport share** — the page listener and the QUIC endpoint had no deadline and no cap. They now give a client 10 seconds for the TLS handshake plus its request and 15 seconds per write, refuse sockets past 64 open connections, refuse a ninth live viewer, and drop a viewer that stops reading. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share_quic.rs`)
- **A slow WebTransport viewer could corrupt its own screen** — output skipped for a viewer that fell behind could cut an escape sequence in half. The viewer now gets a terminal reset and a one-line notice. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share_quic.rs`)
- **A viewer who joins during a full-screen program keeps the leftovers after it exits** — a viewer that connected while `htop` or `vim` was running drew it into its normal buffer, so the program's exit sequence cleared nothing and the shell prompt printed over the old screen. The relay and the WebTransport share now track the alternate-screen switches (`?1049`, `?1047`, `?47`) of the output, also while nobody watches, and send a joining viewer into the alternate screen first. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/session/share_quic.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`)
- **Keys act on the whole multi-selection** — with several rows selected, **Delete** asked about and deleted only the first row, and **Menu** / **Shift+F10** opened the one-row menu for it. **Delete** now deletes the whole selection (after the usual confirmation), the keys open the multi-selection menu, and **F2**, **Ctrl+E** and **Ctrl+D** do nothing instead of acting on one arbitrary row.
- **Tab Broadcast says what it needs** — adding the first tab from the tab menu now toasts "Add one more tab to start Tab Broadcast", and **Ctrl+Shift+A** in a cluster window with fewer than two terminal tabs toasts why nothing started instead of doing nothing.
- **Close All in Group no longer says "Close 1 tabs"** — the confirmation reads "Close all tabs in group “name”?" with typographic quotes.

### Changed
- **Header-bar actions follow the GNOME button layout (F-05)** — Add, New, Refresh and the primary button of single-button dialogs sit on the side opposite the close button. With the default layout nothing changes; with `button-layout` set to `close:` they move to the end. libadwaita places only the window controls by that setting, so hand-made buttons needed this. Dialogs with their own Cancel button keep the HIG order (Cancel first, affirmative last). (`rustconn/src/dialogs/widgets.rs`)
- **fwknop SPA keys leave `connections.toml` and go to the secret store (audit F-08)** — the Rijndael and HMAC keys were written to the connection file as typed, although the model documented a vault reference. Each key is now its own entry in the backend you selected (keyring, KeePass/KDBX, Bitwarden, 1Password, Passbolt, pass, encrypted files), under the flat key `rustconn/spa/<connection id>/rijndael` or `…/hmac`. The id does not change on rename or move. On start, and after saving a connection, existing plaintext keys are copied to the vault and read back; only then are they removed from the file, and the file gets `*_key_in_vault = true`. Before the first rewrite `connections.toml` is copied to `connections.toml.pre-spa-vault.bak` (owner-only; it still holds the keys and is deleted after the first successful SPA connection). A locked, missing or read-only backend leaves the key in the file and shows a toast; the next start retries. A key in the vault is never shown in the editor: the row reads "(in vault)" and an empty row keeps it; type a new key to replace it. `Debug` output shows `<redacted>`. **Downgrade:** a RustConn older than 0.26.0 does not know the flags, finds no key in the file and skips the SPA packet silently; enter the keys again there. Exports, Group Sync files and copies made elsewhere carry no key; enter it on the other machine. Duplicate, group duplicate, range duplicate and paste copy both vault entries to the new connection (all or nothing: if a copy fails, the new entries are removed again, the flags are cleared and a toast asks you to enter the keys). **Credential Transfer** between backends moves the SPA entries too. Switching SPA off in the editor, or saving without a key that was in the vault, removes the entries after the save, and permanent delete removes them as before. Keys are kept in the global KeePass database, not a per-connection one. Switching the backend in Settings alone does not move SPA entries; use Credential Transfer. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/spa_vault.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/knock.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`)
- **Session sharing is visible and easy to stop** — a shared tab's title starts with **◉**, its right-click menu shows **Stop Sharing** instead of **Share Session (read-only)**, and closing the tab stops the relay. The link dialog says that the link is not one-time (any viewer can use it until you stop) and that a LAN link is plain HTTP readable by others on the network. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`)
- **Internet sharing asks before it contacts Google or Cloudflare** — choosing **Internet** now opens **Look Up Your Public Address?**, which names the STUN servers (stun.l.google.com, stun.cloudflare.com), says they see this computer's IP address and that no terminal data is sent. **Local Network Only** shares without any lookup; Cancel starts nothing. No STUN packet is sent before you choose. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/session/share_quic.rs`)
- **The WebTransport share dialog states the real trust model** — the earlier text said RustConn pins the certificate automatically and that the token never rides an unauthenticated channel. That holds for another RustConn, not for a browser, which loads the page over a self-signed certificate first. The dialog, `USER_GUIDE.md` and the module documentation now say so and tell the viewer to compare the certificate fingerprint with the `fp=` part of the link.
- **WebTransport shares are visible and easy to stop** — the shared tab shows **◉**, its menu offers **Stop Sharing**, closing the tab stops the share, and the share starts and stops off the main loop. Output is copied only while a viewer is connected. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`)
- **The share token moved out of the query string** — the link is `http://host:port/#token=…`; the page reads the fragment, removes it from the address bar and sends it in an `Authorization` header, so it no longer reaches server logs, proxies or `Referer`. A link from an earlier build no longer works; share again. It is still plain HTTP on a LAN. (`rustconn-core/src/session/share.rs`)
- **`send` discards output buffered before it** — a custom script that relied on an `expect` matching text that arrived before the preceding `send` now waits for output produced after the command. Scripts where each `expect` ends at the prompt are unaffected, as are the bundled `example-*` scripts that have engine tests (OpenWrt, Cisco, first-run). (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`)
- **Menu wording follows the GNOME HIG** — entries that only open a window lose the trailing ellipsis: **Snippets**, **Clusters**, **Workspaces**, **Templates**, **Variables**, **Scripts**, **Password Generator**, **Network Scan**, **SSH Tunnels**, **Active Sessions**, **Connection History**, **Statistics**, **Recordings**, **Session Logs**, **Components**, **Settings**, **Keyboard Shortcuts** and **Debug Logging**. **Quick Connect…** in the main menu keeps its ellipsis because it asks for a host. Existing translations are kept.
- **One name for each broadcast mode** — the header button of the split view is **Split Broadcast**, the cross-tab one is **Tab Broadcast** in the header bar, the banner, the toasts and the cluster window (its button was **Broadcast**, its banner said "Broadcast active"). The cluster window's **Move Tab to Main Window** is **Move to Main Window**, as in a detached window, and the tab submenu **Monitor** is **Activity Monitor**, so it no longer reads like **Move to New Window on Monitor N**.
- **Command palette commands are translated and show your keys** — the `>` list was always English and showed typed-in keys (it said F1 for Keyboard Shortcuts). The labels now go through gettext and the key beside each command is the one bound in **Settings → Keybindings**. A query matches the translated or the English name.
- **Tooltips show the bound key** — **Settings**, **Split Down**, **Split Right**, **Local Shell**, **Tab Overview**, the broadcast buttons and **Move to Main Window** read the key from the application instead of a default typed into the string, so a rebinding is shown (the text is read when the window is built).
- **Split names, live shortcut labels and a tidier tab menu (UX leftovers)** — the split commands are now called **Split Down** (top and bottom, Ctrl+Shift+H) and **Split Right** (left and right, Ctrl+Shift+S) in the header, the shortcut list, the command palette and the keybinding settings; the User Guide wrongly described the two the other way round. The welcome page key list, the sidebar **Search** tooltip and **Tab Overview grid** now read the keys from your own keybindings instead of fixed text. The sidebar menu item **Rename…** has the ellipsis because it asks for a name. The **Activity Monitor** submenu of the tab menu is shown only on terminal tabs, not on embedded RDP/VNC/Web tabs. (`rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`)

### Removed
- **Two unused validators** — `ExpectEngine::validate_patterns` and `KeePassStatus::validate_key_file_path` had no caller. Per-pattern and per-path checks stay where they are used. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/expect.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/status.rs`)
- **Unused cluster session tracking** — `ClusterSession`, `ClusterMemberState`, `ClusterSessionStatus`, `ClusterSessionSummary` and the matching `ClusterManager` methods were written to, never read, and tested only against themselves; connecting a cluster no longer starts one. Clusters, their members and broadcast behave as before. (`rustconn-core/src/cluster/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/sessions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/clusters.rs`)
- **Unused `ProtocolCapabilities` flags** — the per-protocol capability set and `Protocol::capabilities()` were documented as driving the UI but nothing in the GUI or CLI read them (split-view eligibility is decided from the widget). Removed with their 13 implementations. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`)

### Improved
- **Stop Sharing and Stop Script Recording in the command palette** — both can be run from the palette. With nothing to stop, a toast says so instead of doing nothing. (`rustconn-core/src/search/command_palette.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/network_tools.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/script_actions.rs`)
- **A tab being recorded shows ⏺** — a script recording captures every keystroke, passwords included, so its tab carries a marker as a shared tab does. The ◉ and ⏺ markers now toggle independently of each other and of ⇄. (`rustconn/src/terminal/script_recorder.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/types.rs`)
- **Share ends with a warning** — five minutes before the one-hour limit a toast says the share is about to stop. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`)
- **The SPA key backup removes itself** — `connections.toml.pre-spa-vault.bak` still holds the keys in clear text, so it is deleted after the first SPA packet sent with keys read from the secret store. (`rustconn/src/window/session_start.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/spa_vault.rs`)
- **Menu items that name no action are caught by a test** — every `win.<name>` in a menu must be registered somewhere. (`rustconn/src/window/action_wiring_tests.rs`)
- **Import lists a warning for entries that fail validation** — an imported connection with no name, no host, port 0 or a jump host that is not in the import, a group with no name, or a snippet with no name or command is still imported unchanged and gets a line in the result's **Warnings** list (and in `rustconn-cli import`). Before, the GUI skipped such a connection and only logged it. Loading `connections.toml` logs the same problems without changing the data. Warnings carry no host, user or password. (`rustconn-core/src/import/validation.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/import/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/export_import.rs`)
- **Starting and stopping a share no longer freezes the window** — the relay is started and stopped off the main loop. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`)
- **Selection mode counts and ticks, and Escape leaves it** — the bulk-actions bar shows **Selected: N**, each selected row shows a tick, and **Escape** in the list leaves the mode (it used to clear the selection only, so the mode stayed on with no hint). **Clear Selection** still keeps the mode.
- **Deleting several items offers Undo** — **Delete Selected** moved the items to Trash and showed a toast without a button, so the only way back was the Trash row. The toast now reads **Moved N item(s) to Trash** with **Undo**, which restores all of them (groups first). It also appears when some items failed to delete; those that went are still undoable.
- **A forgotten share stops by itself, and viewers are counted** — a read-only or WebTransport share ends after one hour with a toast (share again to continue), and a toast reports the number of viewers whenever it changes. After the fwknop SPA keys move to the secret store, a toast says so and names `connections.toml.pre-spa-vault.bak`, which still holds the keys. (`rustconn/src/window/network_tools/share.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`)
- **Recording warns about passwords; a shared tab is described to screen readers** — the toast that starts a script recording now says that everything you type, passwords included, is saved. A shared terminal gets the accessible description "This session is shared read-only", because the **◉** tab prefix is only a glyph. The User Guide covers the SPA vault migration (the `.pre-spa-vault.bak` file, downgrade, exports), the header-bar button layout and why Record Script is not in the command palette, and the README lists sharing, TFTP, the remote editor and script recording. (`rustconn/src/window/script_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_labels.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `README.md`)
- **New texts translated in all 19 locales** — the share, TFTP, remote editor, script recorder, SPA vault, SSH agent, import warning, selection mode and menu wording strings are translated in every catalogue. Ukrainian uses «сеанс», not «сесія». (`po/`)
### Dependencies
- **ashpd 0.14.1 → 0.14.2**, **cc 1.11.2 → 1.11.3**, **clap 4.6.8 → 4.6.9**, **form_urlencoded 1.2.2 → 1.2.3**, **oo7 0.7.2 → 0.7.3**, **toml 1.2.1 → 1.2.2**, **url 2.5.8 → 2.5.9**, **walkdir 2.5.0 → 2.5.1**, **x509-cert 0.3.0 → 0.3.1**, **zbus 6.3.0 → 6.5.0**, **zcheapstr 1.3.0 → 1.4.0**, **zerocopy 0.8.63 → 0.8.64**, **zip 9.8.0 → 9.8.1**, **zruntime 0.2.3 → 0.2.4** (and their patch-level companions).

## [0.25.6] - 2026-11-20

### Added

- **Open SFTP in the tab menu** — the right-click menu of an SSH or SFTP tab has **Open SFTP** next to **Edit Connection…**, so the file manager or mc opens for the tab's connection without going back to the sidebar. It asks for the key passphrase first when needed, as the sidebar item does (#240). (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)

### Changed

- **Wake-on-LAN only where it can work** — the sidebar connection menu shows **Wake-on-LAN** only for a connection that has a Wake-on-LAN configuration (MAC address). Before, every connection offered it and a click without one ended in a "No Wake-on-LAN configured" toast. The connection menu inside a smart folder follows the same rule. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/smart_folder_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sorting.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`)

### Fixed

- **`rustconn-cli test` no longer fails Zero Trust and serial connections** — `test` ran a TCP probe on the host, which for AWS SSM is an instance ID, so it ended in `DNS resolution failed: i-0…`. Zero Trust and serial connections are now reported as skipped (a `skipped` detail, no latency, still a pass) and `test all` no longer counts them as failures. (`rustconn-core/src/testing/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/test.rs`)
- **`rustconn-cli delete` left the stored password behind** — deleting a connection whose password source is the vault removed it from the configuration but kept its credential in the secret backend. Builds with `secret-management` now remove that credential first (a key shared by another connection is kept, a backend failure is a warning and the connection is still deleted); a build without it warns that the credential stays. (`rustconn-cli/src/commands/delete.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP overflowed the window at Display Scale above 100%** — after a resize or the first layout pass the desktop was drawn at its full pixel size and spilled past the right and bottom edges. A new frame buffer surface lost its device scale because the draw function remembered the old value; the buffer now keeps the scale itself (#439). (`rustconn/src/cairo_buffer.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/drawing.rs`)
- **Kerberos NLA failed with "invalid MIC token sequence number"** — the retry through external FreeRDP was pinned to `/sec:tls`, which a server that requires NLA refuses. This error now hands off once to external FreeRDP with `/sec:nla`, which keeps NLA on and takes the KDC from the system krb5 configuration. The `/sec:tls` retry is skipped for it. If the NLA retry fails too, the usual error is shown and the session does not loop (#351). (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
### Improved
- **`rustconn-cli show` lists more settings** — an AWS SSM connection shows its profile and region, and an RDP connection shows when its certificate is not verified, in the table and in JSON. (`rustconn-cli/src/commands/show.rs`)
- **No placeholder port for AWS SSM** — `add`, `show` and `list` print `-` (and `add` only the instance ID) instead of the stored `22` for an AWS SSM connection. JSON and CSV keep the number. (`rustconn-cli/src/util.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/add.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/list.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/show.rs`)
### Dependencies
- **ashpd 0.14.0 → 0.14.1**, **libbz2-rs-sys 0.2.6 → 0.2.7**, **oo7 0.7.1 → 0.7.2**, **pin-project-lite 0.2.17 → 0.2.18**, **zcheapstr 1.2.0 → 1.3.0**, **zip 9.7.0 → 9.8.0**

## [0.25.5] - 2026-11-19

### Added

- **Debug Logging… menu item** — the main menu (and the detached-window menu) shows the exact command that starts RustConn with a debug log, in the native or Flatpak form, and copies it to the clipboard. The log filter stays `info,rustconn_core::rdp_client=debug`; the `sspi` and `ironrdp` directives remain forced to `warn`, so no password reaches the log. The user guide gains an RDP troubleshooting section (what the log discloses, FreeRDP 3, Kerberos/KDC for Protected Users accounts) (#351). (`rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/detached_window.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Sidebar empty states** — an empty connection tree now shows a **No Connections Yet** hint, and a search with no match shows **No results**, instead of a blank list. The hint is also visible when the Welcome tab is off or closed. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `po/*.po`)
- **Per-tile Broadcast check in a cluster window** — while the window's broadcast is on, each tile in **Tile Tabs** shows a **Broadcast** check, as the main window's split view does. Unticking it keeps that tab out of the broadcast: it neither sends nor receives. The check stays until the tab is ticked again; if fewer than two tabs are left, the broadcast stops. The checks appear only with three or more terminal tabs; with two, the header-bar **Broadcast** toggle already does the job. Not remembered between openings (#436). (`rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`)

### Fixed

- **Pager text in script logs** — text matched by the script's `pager <regex>` instruction is no longer written to the `log` file, so a transcript has no `---- More ----` lines between pages. (`rustconn/src/script_runner.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Script log permissions** — opening a script log no longer changes the permissions of a file that already existed; only a file the script creates gets `0600`. (`rustconn/src/script_runner.rs`)
- **Tab broadcast toast** — the notice shown when a broadcast stops now reads **Tab broadcast disabled**, matching the renamed toggle. (`rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/group_broadcast.rs`, `po/`)
- **`ask … default` with a hidden answer** — a default built from an `ask … hide` answer now stops the script with an error instead of being dropped silently. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`)

### Changed

- **Per-pane Broadcast checks need three terminals** — in a split view the **Broadcast** check box on each pane (and the matching pane-menu item) is shown only when three or more panes hold a terminal. With two, unticking one left nothing to mirror to, which is what the header-bar **Broadcast** toggle does; dropping to two puts every pane back in. (`rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Consistent names for tab broadcast, snippets and the output filter** — the header toggle **Group Broadcast** is now **Tab Broadcast** (Ctrl+Shift+A, **Toggle Tab Broadcast**), and the tab menu items are **Add to Tab Broadcast** and **Remove from Tab Broadcast**, so "Group" no longer means a fourth thing. The sidebar item **Run Snippet…** is now **Execute Snippet…**, as in the terminal menu and the picker, and its confirmation reads **Execute Snippet?**. The output-filter row **Postpend Command** is now **Filter Command**. Translations carry over; Ukrainian reads **Трансляція на вкладки** and **Виконати сніпета?**. (`rustconn/src/window/ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/shortcuts.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/config/keybindings.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/automation_tab.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)

### Dependencies
- **regex 1.13.1 → 1.13.2**, **regex-automata 0.4.18 → 0.4.19**, **regex-syntax 0.8.11 → 0.8.12**, **smallvec 1.16.5 → 1.16.6**
## [0.25.4] - 2026-11-18

### Added

- **Tile Tabs in a cluster window** — with two or more tabs, a **Tile Tabs** toggle next to **Broadcast** shows every tab at once in a balanced grid (the layout planner of **Split with Other Tabs**), each tile titled with the tab name and a disconnected mark. The active tile is the selected tab, so **Move Tab to Main Window**, close and the window's broadcast work as before; **Show Tabs** brings the tab bar back with every tab intact. The mode is not remembered between openings (#436). (`rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Vendor save-config and inventory scripts** — read-only examples for Cisco, Huawei VRP/OLT, H3C, Juniper, MikroTik, OpenWrt, D-Link, TP-Link, ZTE, Ruijie, Eltex, BDCOM, Ubiquiti, Raisecom, QTECH, Zyxel, C-Data, VSOL, Maipu, Hikvision, Dahua, Netgear, Aruba, Arista, Extreme and Allied Telesis. Each refuses the wrong CLI mode, escalates from user mode when the device has an enable level, answers pager prompts, and writes a timestamped transcript only after an explicit yes (`n`, `no` or an empty answer cancels). The device is never written to; the MikroTik and Eltex exports include secrets (the question says so) and the log is created with mode `0600` on Unix. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/script_examples.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/script.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **`pager <regex>` instruction** — answers a pager prompt with a space while an `expect`, `capture` or `readline` waits, so a CLI without a paging command still produces a complete transcript. The matched text is removed and the waiting step runs again. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/script_reference.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Huawei OLT ONT provisioning and `ask ... default`** — `example-huawei-olt-provision-ont` creates the VLAN, uplink, service/line profiles, ONT and service port on SmartAX OLTs. Answers are validated and pre-filled through the new `ask "…" default "<value>"` form, the plan is printed before an explicit yes, and the transcript is logged without saving the OLT configuration. Checked against the MA5600T V800R011C00 guide and the MA5800 V100R017 grammar. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/script_runner.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/script_examples.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/script_reference.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **`${CWD}` and private log paths** — a log path can start with `${CWD}` so the question shows the absolute file location without a literal OS path in the script; `ask … hide` answers are refused in log paths, as `run` already refuses them. Logs are created as `0600` on Unix. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn/src/script_runner.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Fixed

- **New/Edit Cluster dialog fits its content** — the dialog had a fixed 580 px height, so the form was cut off and the connection list was squeezed. It now takes the height of its content, never more than the window, and scrolls on small screens.
- **Editing a connection keeps its hidden protocol fields** — saving the connection dialog dropped settings it has no widget for: MOSH custom arguments, the username of a Cloudflare Access, Teleport or Tailscale SSH connection, RDP paste-via-clipboard turned off, and a Kubernetes shell outside the dialog's four choices. A default value from the dialog now leaves the stored one in place. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`)
- **Deleting a smart folder asks first** — the red menu item removed the filter at once, with no Undo. It now opens a **Delete …?** dialog (Cancel is the default). (`rustconn/src/window/smart_folders.rs`)

### Changed

- **Scripts dialog redesigned** — the dialog is now 900×640 (never larger than the window) with a collapsing two-pane layout: a sidebar with a labelled **New Script** button, search and the list, and an editor in a framed, scrollable box. Before a script is picked the right pane shows an empty state. **Delete** is red and still asks first, **Run** is the suggested action; the per-row trash buttons are gone (use **Delete** in the editor). Files, backups and run/stop behave as before. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/script.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Bundled examples are refreshed without losing edits** — a changed bundled example is copied to `<id>.rsc.bak` before the new source replaces it; an existing backup is never overwritten (the next copy is `.bak.1`, `.bak.2`), so an edit survives repeated upgrades. An identical file is left untouched. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/script.rs`)
- **Script API break for `ScriptHost` implementors** — `ScriptHost::set_log` now returns `bool`, `ScriptHost::ask` takes a `default: Option<&str>` parameter, `ScriptStep` gains the variant `Pager`, and `ScriptStep::Ask` gains the field `default`. RustConn's own `GuiHost` is updated; an external implementation of the trait must be too. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`)
- **`default` and `hide` are keywords in `ask`** — a script can no longer name the answer variable `default` or `hide` in an `ask` line; rename such a variable. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`)

### Improved

- **Scripts dialog: the library fills the pane and can be widened and searched** — the list/editor split is a draggable `GtkPaned`, the list uses the full height, long ids ellipsize with a tooltip, and the search field filters through `set_filter_func` and has an accessible label. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/script.rs`)
- **The password show/hide button tells screen readers its state** — in the connection and group dialogs the button now reports as pressed while the password is visible; before, only its icon changed. (`rustconn/src/window/connection_dialogs.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_group.rs`)


### Dependencies
- **bytes 1.12.1 → 1.12.2**, **zip 9.6.1 → 9.7.0**
## [0.25.3] - 2026-11-17

### Added

- **Broadcast in a cluster window — [#436](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/436)** — a cluster opened in its own window gets a **Broadcast** toggle in its header bar once it holds two or more terminal tabs. On (the toggle or Ctrl+Shift+A), a keystroke typed in any tab of the window goes to all of its terminal tabs; a banner names how many, and its **Stop** turns it off. Each cluster window has its own broadcast, independent of the main window's Group Broadcast and of other cluster windows, so separate clusters can take separate commands. More than five tabs ask for confirmation first, as Group Broadcast does. All broadcasts now share one re-entrancy guard, so a session in two of them does not get each character twice. (`rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/group_broadcast.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **Duplicate Tab in the tab menu — [#437](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/437)** — right-click a tab with a saved connection → **Duplicate Tab** opens another session of that connection, what **Open new session** does in the sidebar, without selecting the connection there first. (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **Closing a cluster window asks first** — closing a cluster window that still holds two or more sessions now opens a **Close this window?** dialog (Cancel is the default); confirming ends all of them. Quitting the app is not asked again.
- **A detached window opened fullscreen says how to leave it** — its header bar is hidden in fullscreen, so a toast now says to press **F11**.

### Fixed

- **Closing the import dialog from the result page now imports what it lists** — the result page said "Successfully imported N connection(s)", yet the connections were added only when **Done** was clicked, so Esc or ✕ lost them. Closing the dialog from the result page now does what **Done** does. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/import/mod.rs`)
- **Deleting a cluster asks once** — the cluster list asked "Delete Cluster?" and then the cluster manager asked again with a different text. Only the second dialog, which names the cluster, remains. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/cluster.rs`)
- **Editing a cluster offers Save, not Create** — the header button of the cluster dialog is now a text button, **Create** for a new cluster and **Save** when editing. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/cluster.rs`)
- **A failed Undo or Restore says so** — when restoring a deleted connection or group failed, the **Undo** button of the toast and **Restore** in the Trash did nothing and said nothing. A **Restore failed** error toast appears and the cause is logged. (`rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)
- **A partly failed Move names the items** — the "Move Partially Complete" dialog listed UUIDs; it lists names. (`rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`)
- **Workspace dialogs no longer swallow input or errors** — **Save** in Save Workspace stays disabled while the name is empty, **Rename** while it is empty or unchanged, and a failed delete or rename shows an error dialog instead of only logging. (`rustconn/src/window/workspaces.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/workspace.rs`)
- **Singular and plural in the session-restore texts** — "Reopen the 1 connections…" and "1 restored connections no longer exist" now use plural forms in every language; the second reads "connection from the saved session no longer exists". (`rustconn/src/window/session_restore.rs`, `po/`)
- **Run Snippet from the context menu without a terminal says so** — the menu item did nothing; it now shows the toast "Open a terminal session first before executing a snippet." (`rustconn/src/window/snippets.rs`)
- **Success toasts have the success type** — "Recording exported/imported successfully" and "Connection copied to clipboard" were shown as plain info. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/recording.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`)
- **Escape closes OK-only alerts through their own response** — `show_alert` and `show_error` set the close response to **OK**. (`rustconn/src/alert.rs`)
- **Remove from Favorites in the Favorites group** — the context menu of a connection inside the ★ Favorites group offered **Add to Favorites**, because that copy of the row was built as not pinned to hide its star; clicking it removed the favorite anyway. The row now keeps the real pinned state and hides the star by a separate flag, so the menu says **Remove from Favorites** in both places. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sorting.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Search results keep the connection's real state and name** — a row in the sidebar search results was built without its state, so the menu of a favorite offered **Add to Favorites** and a connected one lacked **Show Session**, **Open new session** and **Disconnect**, and its name carried a relevance prefix (`★★★ srv`) that **Rename** and the delete dialog then showed. Results are now built by the same function as the tree rows and keep the plain name; their order is still by relevance. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sorting.rs`)
- **The Favorites group has no dead context menu** — right-clicking the virtual ★ Favorites group opened the group menu whose **Rename**, **Edit**, **Delete** and others silently did nothing. It now opens no menu. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`)
- **Tags and groups in the command palette work** — typing only `@` or `#` listed the tags or groups, but choosing one did nothing; it now narrows the palette to that tag or group. `#` lists and searches groups of every depth, not only top-level ones. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/command_palette.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Connect in the command palette shows a running session** — choosing a connection that already runs opened a second session; it now shows the running one, as **Show Session** in the sidebar does. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Keyboard Shortcuts hint in the command palette** — it read F1, which opens Help; it reads Ctrl+?. (`rustconn-core/src/search/command_palette.rs`)
- **Dragging onto Favorites, Trash or a Favorites copy is refused** — these rows showed a drop highlight and then ignored the drop (a copy in Favorites moved the connection out of its real group). They now take no drop. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`)
- **Deleting selected items counts a favorite once** — a favorite has two rows (Favorites and its group), so a bulk delete listed it twice and then reported a failure for the second. The selection is now de-duplicated, and a failure names the item instead of its UUID. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`)
- **Right-click keeps a multi-selection** — in Select Connections mode a right-click on a selected row cleared the selection, so **Run Local Script…** on several rows worked only from the keyboard. The selection is kept and a menu with the bulk actions opens: **Run Local Script…**, **Move to Group**, **Batch Edit**, **Delete Selected**. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`)
- **Editing a connection keeps its window mode, dynamic-folder flag and post-login scripts** — the connection editor has no widgets for `window_mode`, `remember_window_position`, `is_dynamic` and `automation.post_login_scripts`, so saving any edit reset them to the defaults (an External or Fullscreen RDP/VNC connection set with `rustconn-cli add --window-mode` went back to Embedded). `ConnectionManager::update_connection` now keeps the stored value when the edited copy holds the default. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`)
- **The connection wizard keeps its input and shows the error when creating the connection fails** — **Save**, **Save & Connect** and **Duplicate via Wizard…** closed the wizard before the connection was created and dropped a failure silently. The wizard now stays open and shows **Error Creating Connection** with the reason. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Batch Edit moves the Vault credential with the connection** — changing the group of several connections in **Batch Edit** left a `Vault` password under the old group path, so the connections asked for it again. The group change now goes through `move_connection_to_group`, as **Move to Group** does, and so does its **Undo**. The check boxes in front of the fields also have an accessible label now. (`rustconn/src/window/batch_edit.rs`)
- **An invalid port knock sequence is reported on Save** — the connection dialog dropped a sequence it could not parse and closed as if it had been saved. **Save** now stops with the format hint. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/builders.rs`)
- **Enter confirms the `@ask:` prompt** — the prompt for `@ask:` variables now focuses its first field and Enter in a text or secret field answers **Connect**. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/ask_prompt.rs`)
