- **Custom SSH options keep their order and no longer suggest turning host key checking off** — the Custom Options field listed the options in a different order on every open, and its placeholder and the User Guide example offered `StrictHostKeyChecking=no`. Options are shown sorted and the example is `ServerAliveInterval=60, Compression=yes`. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/populate.rs`, `ssh.rs`)
- **Untranslated texts in the connection dialog and Template Manager** — the **Jump Host** and **Tunnel** lists showed `(None)`, the **Username** placeholder `(default: …)`, the template rows `Host:`/`Port:`/`User:`, and the Expect pattern tester its result lines in English. They go through gettext now. (`general_tab.rs`, `dialog/populate.rs`, `dialog/rows.rs`, `template_manager.rs`)
- **Duplicate via Wizard… fills in everything the wizard can show** — the wizard copied only name, host, port, user, domain and icon. It now also restores the Serial device and baud rate, the Kubernetes context, namespace, pod and container, the Zero Trust provider and its fields, the Web URL, the jump host, the SSH authentication method and the key file. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/`)
- **fwknop SPA needs both keys** — a connection with SPA enabled and only one of the Rijndael and HMAC keys sent no packet and gave no hint. **Save** now asks for both. The model documentation no longer says the keys are stored in the vault: they are still written to `connections.json` as typed. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/builders.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/knock.rs`)
- **Switching OpenH264 off now sticks** — the **Use OpenH264** switch saved itself at once, but closing Settings wrote back the value it had when the dialog opened, so the codec could come back on after any other change; and the choice was applied to the decoder only after Settings had been opened once in a run. Closing Settings now keeps the saved value, and the choice is applied at startup. Required by the Cisco OpenH264 licence, which says the codec must be switchable off. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **Minimize to tray no longer hides the window when there is no tray** — closing the window hid it whenever the setting was on, even on a desktop with no tray host or after **Show icon** was switched on in the same run, leaving no way to bring it back. The window is hidden only when a tray icon really exists. **Show icon** now says that it needs a restart. (`rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)
- **Closing Settings or the shortcut recorder keeps keyboard passthrough on** — both put every application shortcut back, so Ctrl+N, Ctrl+F and the rest were taken from the session again while the passthrough indicator was still lit. Only the configured exceptions are restored while passthrough is on. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/keybindings_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **Ctrl+G and Ctrl+T reach a focused terminal** — they opened the password generator and the SSH tunnel manager over `abort` in emacs, nano and vim and `transpose-chars` in readline. They now join the single-Ctrl shortcuts that are handed to the terminal while it has focus. (`rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **Ctrl+Backspace and Shift+Escape can be recorded as shortcuts** — the recorder reset the binding on any Backspace and cancelled on any Escape, so the default Ctrl+Shift+Backspace (keyboard passthrough) could not be assigned again. Only a bare Backspace or Escape is a command now. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/keybindings_tab.rs`)
- **Shortcuts on the Keybindings page read as Ctrl+Shift+Q** — the page showed the raw GTK strings such as `<Control><Shift>q`. They now read like the shortcuts dialog, and alternatives are joined with " / ". (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/keybindings_tab.rs`)
- **The external browser command is dimmed while the embedded browser is on** — **External browser command (tunnelled)** only applies when the embedded browser is off. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)
- **About dialog** — the debug information named the minimum supported Rust version as the compiler; the line is removed. **Get Support** opens GitHub Discussions instead of the donation page (the donation links stay in the link list), and the issue link uses the canonical `RustConn` path. (`rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **The Settings dialog is titled Settings** — it showed libadwaita's own "Preferences". (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/mod.rs`)
- **Close Others, Close to the Left and Close to the Right act on the tab you right-clicked** — they used the selected tab, so a right-click on a background tab closed every tab except the selected one.
- **Group broadcast can always be stopped** — the banner's **Stop** button and **Ctrl+Shift+A** did nothing while the active tab was not in the broadcast set. While the broadcast is on, the toggle, the shortcut and **Stop** now work from any tab.
- **Tab titles keep the `⇄` and `●REC` markers when a tab joins or leaves a group** — **Set Group…** and **Remove from Group** rebuilt the title by string stripping, which stacked `[a] [b]` labels, left `[group]` in a title after leaving the group and left a `⇄` that could not be removed.
- **Set Group… takes Enter and has focus** — the name field is focused when the dialog opens, Enter applies it, and **Apply** is off while the name is empty.
- **The Welcome tab menu no longer offers Set Group…, Add to Broadcast and Monitor** — they did nothing there.
- **The disconnected marker on a tab has a tooltip** — hovering the offline icon says **Session disconnected**.
- **Ukrainian wording** — the confirm dialog read "Завершуй сеанса?" and now reads **Завершити сеанс?**; saved-search, split, sort, filter and Empty Trash actions use the imperative, and "немає", "змінна" and "підключення" follow the project glossary (**нема**, **змінниця**, **з'єднування**).

### Changed

- **The connection dialog has Cancel, Enter saves and unsaved edits are confirmed** — the header now has a **Cancel** button, a text **Create**/**Save** button at the end instead of the plus and floppy-disk icons, and Enter in the Name, Host, Username, Domain and Tags fields presses it. Cancel, Escape or the close gesture after an edit asks **Discard Changes?**; a dialog nothing was typed or clicked in closes at once. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/construction.rs`, `save.rs`, `populate.rs`, `general_tab.rs`)
- **The Custom SSH Agent Socket Path row is hidden in Settings → Secrets** — the value was saved but no ssh or SFTP launch read it, so the row promised an override that never applied; per-connection and per-group sockets are unaffected. A value already in the settings file is kept. Wiring it up is planned for 0.26. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ssh_agent_tab.rs`)
- **Split-view wording says "pane" everywhere, and one name closes a pane** — the pane menu's **Close Connection**, the × button's **Close session** and the empty pane's **Close panel** are all **Close Pane** now, matching Ctrl+Shift+X; "panel" in tooltips, toasts and the Empty Pane page became "pane". Translations carried over in all catalogues.
- **One spelling per menu item, button and tooltip** — **Open New Session**, **Stop Tracking**, **Copy/Paste Connection**, **Toggle Fullscreen**, **Quick Connect**, **Local Shell**, **Command Palette**, **Start/Stop Tunnel**, **Edit Snippet/Cluster**, **Execute Snippet** and similar labels had a second, lower-case msgid; they now share the header-case one, and **Wake On LAN** is spelled **Wake-on-LAN**. Translations carried over; the User Guide uses the same names.
- **Ukrainian says «сеанс», not «сесія»** — 11 strings in `uk.po` (cluster disconnect, session timeout, total/active sessions, workspace and profile save, remote typing) now use «сеанс» in the right case; the glossary in the uk reviewer rules says so. Other catalogues are unchanged. (`po/uk.po`)

### Improved

- **Duplicate as Range shows a syntax example** — the dialog text now names `web[01-10]`. (`rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`)

### Documentation

- **USER_GUIDE matches the sidebar** — Layout and Components (the ⋯ menu, six bottom-toolbar icons), Group Operations Mode (no checkboxes, no right-click entry, Escape only clears), the Trash menus, the Smart Folder **+** button, the Command Palette `!` prefix and drill-down, the passthrough palette shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+P), WinRM in the protocol filter, Local Shell in the ☰ menu, and the context-menu actions the Connection Actions table lacked. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **User Guide matches the connection editor** — removed the Window Mode and Remember Position fields the Advanced tab does not have (the mode is set with `rustconn-cli --window-mode`), renamed the "Authentication Tab" to the Authentication group on the Basic tab, pointed ProxyJump to the Protocol tab, corrected the template buttons (**New Template**, **Use Template**) and dropped **Create Template from Connection**, which does not exist. Added **Batch Edit**, **Duplicate via Wizard…** and the editor's Cancel, Enter and discard check. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **User Guide, Settings and shortcuts** — Monitoring is described as its own page, the Keybindings steps point to the Interface page and say when a shortcut is written to disk, the Logging rows carry their real names with a warning about recorded keystrokes, the Settings rows that were missing (toolbar on hover, terminal control shortcuts, remember passthrough, auto-capture, split-pane hover controls, directional tab-close items, scrollbar, right-click paste, Option as Meta, auto-hide sidebar, Use OpenH264) are listed, **Ctrl+Shift+A** is in the shortcut table, and Ctrl+M is no longer called sidebar-only. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **User guide matches the import, export, cluster and trash dialogs** — removed the Merge Strategies list and the Import Preview paragraph (neither exists; duplicates are handled by the Cancel / Skip Duplicates / Import All dialog), replaced "Export selected only" with the group filter, named the **Create**/**Save** button of the cluster dialog and noted the confirmation before a single delete. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **User Guide** — the cluster section no longer says broadcast is only a split-view feature, the tab menu no longer lists a **Copy** section, **Move to New Window on Monitor N** is described as flat items, the group broadcast shortcut **Ctrl+Shift+A** is in the shortcut table, and the detached window section lists what it lacks.
- **User Guide: command macros, Create Another, import steps, contents** — added a Command Macros section (Name, Command, Shortcut, Run), the New Group **Create Another** switch, replaced the Remmina migration step that named a merge strategy with the real result-page steps, and listed Quick Connect, Undo/Trash, Command Macros, Status Indicators, Run Local Script and Keyboard Passthrough in the contents.

### Dependencies

- **cargo-deny passes again** — RUSTSEC-2026-0543 (chrono is unmaintained, all versions, no patched release) is accepted in `deny.toml` and `.cargo/audit.toml` until chrono moves to `time` or `jiff` (0.26). (`deny.toml`, `.cargo/audit.toml`)
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): clap 4.6.7→4.6.8, clap_complete 4.6.11→4.6.12, clap_derive 4.6.7→4.6.8, clap_lex 1.1.1→1.1.2, clap_mangen 0.3.3→0.3.4, uuid 1.29.2→1.29.3, zeroize 1.9.1→1.9.2.

## [0.25.2] - 2026-11-17

### Fixed

- **Quitting while an SSH tunnel is starting no longer leaves `ssh -N` running** — a tunnel (Web `tunnel_via`, **Open Browser via Tunnel**, jump-host RDP, VNC, SPICE and Telnet) is raised on a worker thread that waits up to ten seconds for its port, and nothing else knew the process until that wait ended, so quitting in that window left the `ssh -N` alive and its askpass script and password file on disk. Every tunnel is now registered the moment it spawns; the exit path kills them, deletes those files and refuses to start another. A crash of the application itself is still not covered. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)

### Changed

- **The existing-key list of the SSH key setup dialog loads off the GTK thread** — picking **Use an existing key** scanned `~/.ssh` and the other key directories on the main thread; it now runs on a worker and the list fills in when the scan ends. A dialog closed meanwhile is left alone. (`rustconn/src/window/key_setup.rs`)

### Dependencies

- **Updated** (semver-compatible): filetime 0.2.29→0.2.30, notify-types 2.3.0→2.3.1, rand 0.10.3→0.10.4, zip 9.5.1→9.6.1. `cargo audit` reports no vulnerabilities; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak manifests
- **nix 0.31→0.32** — zip 9.6.1 now depends on nix 0.32, so the workspace moves with it and the lockfile keeps one copy of nix

## [0.25.1] - 2026-11-15

### Added

- **Closing a split tab asks what to do with the split** — closing a tab whose split shows two or more sessions (its close button, a middle-click, **Close Tab…** in the tab menu, Ctrl+Shift+W) opens a dialog that names the session the tab belongs to and the other sessions it shows (the first three, then how many more). **Remove Split**, the default, returns every session to its own tab and closes none; **Close All Sessions** disconnects all of them; **Cancel** or Escape keeps the tab. Until now the tab closed its owner session silently, though the tab title names the focused pane, which may be another session. **Close Others**, **Close to the Left**/**Right**, **Close All Tabs** and **Close All Ungrouped** ask once for the whole batch when it includes a split tab, and **Close All in Group** says so in the confirmation it already had; confirming closes the other sessions of those splits too. Closes the application makes on its own (scripts, clusters, a session that ends, reconnect, a pane's **Close Connection**, the sidebar's **Disconnect**, closing the window) do not ask. (`rustconn/src/terminal/split_close.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/detach.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **Leave a split panel out of the broadcast — [PR #432](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/432), contributed by @roms2000** — while split broadcast is on, each terminal panel has a **Broadcast** check box in its header. Unticking it keeps that panel on screen but out of the broadcast in both directions: it receives nothing typed in the other panels and what is typed in it stays in it. Ticking it again brings the panel back; the panel menu (Shift+F10, Menu key) has the same **Broadcast** item for the keyboard, and screen readers hear the session's name with the check box. The exclusion is kept per session, so it survives a pane being added or closed and broadcast being switched off and on; a session that leaves the split is forgotten and comes back included. The headers of terminal panels are shown while broadcast is on even when the connection-name labels are switched off, so the check boxes are always reachable; embedded RDP, VNC and SPICE panels get none. (`rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/navigation_actions.rs`)
- **R reconnects, Q closes and Enter twice runs the banner's action on a session that has ended — [PR #431](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/431), contributed by @roms2000** — when a terminal session ends (the connection drops, the remote shell exits), the **Session disconnected** banner now says so and three keys work while it is shown, with focus in its terminal: **R** does what the Reconnect button does, **Q** closes the tab, the split pane or the detached or cluster window tab the session is in, without asking. **Enter** is two-step: the first press highlights the banner's suggested button and the hint changes to *Press Enter again to reconnect* (or *…to log in* when the banner offers **Log In to AWS** and the like for expired cloud credentials); the second press clicks that button. Any other key cancels the first Enter, and holding Enter down does not count as two presses. In a split, Q closes the pane whose terminal got the key, not the pane last clicked. An ended terminal takes no input, so no keystroke is taken from a live session, and the keys are never mirrored by broadcast; they work under a non-Latin layout and are ignored with Ctrl, Alt or Super held. The hint shortens with an ellipsis in a narrow pane and its full text is in its tooltip; screen readers get the three keys in the banner's description and hear the change after the first Enter. A **Local Shell** whose shell exits used to leave a dead tab with no banner; it now gets the same banner, and R or Reconnect starts a new shell in the same tab, through `flatpak-spawn --host` in the Flatpak as before. With **Close tab on clean exit** on, a local shell that exits cleanly (`exit`, Ctrl+D) closes its tab instead, like a connection does. Embedded RDP, VNC and SPICE sessions keep their own banner without the keys. (`rustconn/src/terminal/session_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/session_lifecycle.rs`)
- **Reconnect in the terminal's context menu** — right-click in the terminal of a session that has ended (SSH, Telnet, Serial, Kubernetes, Zero Trust, Local Shell): **Reconnect** heads the menu and does what the banner's button does. It is offered only while the session has ended, so a live session's menu is unchanged, and it is withdrawn while a cloud login runs in the terminal. It reconnects the right-clicked session in a tab, a split pane, a detached window and a cluster window. With **Right-click pastes** on there is still no menu. (`rustconn/src/terminal/config.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/session_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Network Scan — [PR #427](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/427), contributed by @roms2000** — Tools → **Network Scan…**, also in the command palette, looks for the hosts of an IPv4 range that answer on the SSH, RDP, VNC, Telnet, HTTP and HTTPS ports. The range is a network with its mask (`192.168.1.0/24`, `192.168.1.0/255.255.255.0`) or a first and a last address (`192.168.1.10-192.168.1.50`), up to a `/16`, and is pre-filled with the local `/24`. Hosts are listed as they answer, and each can be added as a connection with one click per protocol found. It is a TCP connect scan, needing no privilege and no external tool; a host whose firewall drops every scanned port is not listed, and the dialog says so. (`rustconn-core/src/network_scan.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/network_scan.rs`)
- **Open a cluster in a window of its own — [PR #428](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/428), contributed by @roms2000** — a second button on each row of Tools → **Clusters…** connects the cluster with every member's tab gathered in a separate window titled after the cluster, instead of the main window's tab bar. The sessions are detached sessions like those of *Move to New Window*: closing a tab or the window ends them, **Move Tab to Main Window** hands one back, and Disconnect all, renames and the disconnected marker reach them there. A member that cannot leave the main window (an external viewer) stays there with the cluster's tab group, a tab dragged onto another window's tab bar is handed back to its own window, and a member that fails to start no longer pulls that connection's next tab into the cluster. The cluster window has no tab context menu, split view, keyboard passthrough or F10 menu. (`rustconn/src/session_group_window.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_transfer.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/clusters.rs`)
- **Remove Split in the command palette — [PR #426](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/426), contributed by @roms2000** — the command palette now offers **Remove Split**, which returns every session of the current tab's split to its own tab without closing any of them; it was reachable only through `Ctrl+Shift+J` and the tab and pane context menus. The `win.unsplit` action behind all of them is now enabled only while the current tab is split, so on a tab without a split the shortcut and the palette entry do nothing. (`rustconn-core/src/search/command_palette.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`)
- **Enter in the sidebar search opens the connection — [PR #425](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/425), contributed by @roms2000** — pressing Enter in the search field opens the connection that was selected, when the results still hold it, or else the first result, so a connection can be found and opened without leaving the keyboard or moving focus into the list. This changes what Enter does: until now it only saved the query to the search history, which it still does. Enter with no match, or in an empty field, opens nothing. Enter pressed right after typing, before the list has caught up, filters with the typed text first, so it opens what was typed and not a row of the previous list. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/search.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Fixed

- **The Session disconnected banner stays in a split pane** — a session that had ended lost its **Session disconnected** banner, and its **Log In to AWS** button, when it was moved into a split, when a pane was added or closed next to it, or when it moved between a tab, a split and a window: only the terminal moves, and the box holding the banner was rebuilt without it, while R, Q and Enter kept working with nothing on screen. An AWS SSM session with an expired `aws login` fails within seconds, so it was usually already ended when it went into the split. The banner now comes back each time the terminal is shown again, and for the pane that hosts the split it sits in that pane instead of below the whole split. (`rustconn/src/terminal/session_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **SSH sessions dropped by a keepalive timeout reconnect on their own** — OpenSSH exits with status 255 for a refused key and for a lost connection alike, and every such exit was treated as an authentication failure: automatic reconnection was skipped and the cached password forgotten. An exit 255 now counts as an authentication failure only when ssh's last message says so (`Permission denied`, `Authentication failed`, `Too many authentication failures`), or when it came within 60 seconds of the start with no message; `Timeout, server … not responding`, `timed out`, `Connection reset` and `Broken pipe` are reconnected. The decision is `rustconn_core::protocol::ssh_exit_is_auth_failure`, and its 60-second window is the one the expired cloud sign-in check uses (`STARTUP_FAILURE_WINDOW_SECS`, formerly `LOGIN_FAILURE_WINDOW_SECS`). (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/exit_cause.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/cloud_login.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/session_lifecycle.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Command palette commands that name a window action run again** — **Toggle Fullscreen**, **Toggle Keyboard Passthrough**, **Split Horizontal**, **Split Vertical**, **Keyboard Shortcuts**, **Tab Overview** and **Switch Tab** did nothing when chosen from the command palette. The palette passed the prefixed action name (`win.split-vertical`, `app.shortcuts`) to the main window's own action group, which knows only bare names, so no action was found. It now activates the action through the window widget, which resolves the `win.` and `app.` prefixes, and logs a warning when a name matches no action. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Dragging a connection or a folder drops it where the line shows — [PR #424](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/424), contributed by @roms2000** — the sidebar drew the drop line above or below the target row, but the drop itself always placed the dragged item *after* the target: an item could not be moved to the top of its folder, and to land in a given place it had to be dropped on the row above it. A drop on the upper half of a row now places the item before that row and a drop on the lower half after it, for connections and for folders. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sorting.rs`)
- **Closing a disconnected session stops its auto-reconnect probe** — a tab closed with its close button, or a session ended from a detached or cluster window, kept its background "is the host back?" probe running until the host answered, because only `TerminalNotebook::close_tab` cancelled it. The shared tab teardown now cancels the probe and forgets the session's reconnect banner. (`rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`)
- **Closing a session that sits in another tab's split closes it** — **Close** (now **Disconnect**) in the sidebar on a session shown in a split panel of another tab (a split guest) emptied its panel and lowered the sidebar's session count, but the session kept running with no panel and no tab. The session manager's **Terminate**, a script's stop, a cluster disconnect and a clean exit with "Close tab on clean exit" did nothing at all for such a session. `TerminalNotebook::close_tab` now closes a split guest the way it already closed a detached session, and the guest's panel is removed instead of left as an empty panel: a split left with a single session becomes an ordinary tab again, and **Remove Split** follows. A local shell in a split panel that exits cleanly with "Close tab on clean exit" on now closes its panel too, instead of showing the "Session disconnected" banner. (`rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/detach.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`)
- **Move to Own Tab and Remove Split in the sidebar act again** — on a connection shown in a split pane, the sidebar's **Move to Own Tab** (a guest) and **Remove Split** (the split's owner) did nothing, for terminals and embedded RDP/VNC panes alike; **Move to New Window** did not take the session out of the split first. The sidebar focused the pane by the session's id, while panes are looked up by their own id, so no pane was focused and the split action found nothing to move. **Show Session** on a split guest selected the guest's own hidden tab instead of the tab that shows the split, so the split stayed out of view when another tab was open. Both now go through one function that selects the tab hosting the split and focuses the session's pane (`focus_split_pane`). The sidebar actions now run on the main window rather than on the last focused window, and each one logs why it stopped when it cannot act. (`rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)

### Changed

- **Close Tab… on a split tab** — the tab menu's close item reads **Close Tab…** on a tab that hosts a split, because there it opens the split close dialog; other tabs keep **Close Tab**. (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`)
- **Split and session menu items say what they do** — **Remove from Split** is now **Move to Own Tab**, in a split pane's header menu, on the pane's corner button, in the sidebar menu and in **Keyboard Shortcuts** (`Ctrl+Shift+R`). On the pane of the split's owner the header menu leaves it out, because there it collapses the whole split, which is what **Remove Split** next to it does; in the sidebar the owner's row shows **Remove Split** instead. On a connected row the sidebar menu's **Connect** is now **Show Session**, and it does that: it focuses the newest live session, the way a double-click does, or shows the "Already running in an external window" hint for a session in an external viewer, whatever the "new session on every double-click" setting says; it used to start a second session, which **Open new session** in the same menu still does. The sidebar's **Close** for a connected row is now **Disconnect**, and a row whose session runs in an external viewer shows only the **Disconnect** it already had at the top. **Reconnect** in a pane's header menu is offered only once the pane's session has ended, read each time the menu opens; on a live session it used to do nothing. The pointer, long-press and keyboard routes to the pane menu now share one builder, so their items cannot drift apart. (`rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/shortcuts.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/config/keybindings.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)

### Documentation

- **Closing a split tab is described in the User Guide** — the Split View section now describes the dialog shown when a split tab is closed, its three answers, how bulk closes ask once, and which commands end only the split or close a single pane without asking.

### Dependencies

- **Updated** (semver-compatible): cairo-rs 0.22.9→0.22.11, gdk-pixbuf 0.22.0→0.22.11, gio 0.22.10→0.22.11, gio-sys 0.22.9→0.22.11, glib 0.22.10→0.22.11, glib-macros 0.22.9→0.22.11, glib-sys 0.22.9→0.22.11, pango-sys 0.22.9→0.22.11, bumpalo 3.20.3→3.20.4. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak manifest. GNOME runtime 51, VTE 0.84.1, FreeRDP 3.33.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.7.0, inetutils 2.8, mc 4.8.33, the Snap on `core26` and the pinned TigerVNC 1.16.2 download are current
- **nix stays on 0.31** — nix 0.32.0 was released on 2026-11-14, but `zip` 9.5.1 still depends on nix 0.31, so moving now would build two copies of nix
- RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (`rsa`) stays ignored: `ironrdp-connector` 0.12.0, still the latest, pins picky 7.0.0-rc.26, which pins rsa 0.10.0-rc.18 and pkcs1 0.8.0-rc.4 and holds sspi 0.23.1, winscard 0.4.1 and picky rc.29 back

## [0.25.0] - 2026-11-14

### Added

- **New Subgroup in a folder's context menu** — right-click a group, then **New Subgroup**, opens the New Group dialog titled "New Subgroup" with that group already chosen as the parent (change it in the dialog if needed). Until now a subgroup meant opening **New Group** and picking the parent from the dropdown. Hidden on Cloud Sync import groups, like **New Connection in Group**, since their content mirrors the source. Translated in all 19 catalogues. (`rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/connection_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/connection_dialogs.rs`, `po/`)
- **Duplicate and Move to Group on a folder's context menu** — **Duplicate** copies a group with its subgroups and connections beside the original as "name (copy)", and selects the copy. The copy is a plain folder tree: Cloud Sync links (`sync_mode`, file, `sync_origin_id`) are dropped, so a Master copies as an ordinary group; a group's saved vault password is not copied (the copy's password source becomes None; username and domain stay); connections' own vault passwords are copied as a single **Duplicate** does; dynamic connections are left to the next refresh. Hidden on Cloud Sync import groups and dynamic folders, whose content is generated. **Move to Group…**, which already handled folders, is now offered on the menu too (not on import groups). (`rustconn-core/src/connection/group_copy.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`)
- **Create Another in the New Group dialog** — a **Create Another** switch keeps the dialog open after a group is created: the name is cleared and focused, a toast names the group just made, and the parent, credentials and description stay for the next one. Applies to **New Subgroup** too. (`rustconn/src/window/connection_dialogs.rs`)
- **Split a whole set of tabs in one step** — the tab context menu has **Split with Other Tabs…** ([#423](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/423)): tick the tabs to bring along and they fill a balanced grid on the right-clicked tab, instead of splitting once and picking each tab with **Select Tab…**. Also on **Ctrl+Shift+K** (rebindable), so it works from the keyboard (then Tab/Space/Enter in the dialog), the dialog labels every row for screen readers, and the sessions are moved, not reconnected. The balanced-grid planner is now shared with workspace restore (`rustconn_core::split::balanced_split_plan`, with unit tests). (`rustconn/src/dialogs/split_tabs.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/split/balanced.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Choose the monitors of an external RDP window** — the connection dialog has a **Monitors** row under **External Window**: FreeRDP monitor numbers, comma-separated, with the screens GTK reports listed in its subtitle. They become `/monitors:` (plus `/multimon`, which FreeRDP needs outside single-monitor fullscreen); empty keeps FreeRDP's current-monitor default and **All monitors** keeps `/multimon`. Stored as `monitor_ids`, omitted when empty, so older connections load unchanged and older versions ignore it. A list with something that is not a number is marked in the dialog and refused on save, instead of being dropped. External FreeRDP only; embedded sessions can still go fullscreen on one screen from the tab menu. (`rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/freerdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **`rustconn-cli import` reads PuTTY and mRemoteNG files** — `-f putty` takes a PuTTY / KiTTY `.reg` Registry export and `-f mremoteng` takes an mRemoteNG `confCons.xml`, through the same importers the GUI uses, so the README's import list is now true for the CLI too. (`rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/export_import.rs`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)
- **Saved searches in the sidebar** — the Filter menu now lists named searches: **Save current search…** stores the search text together with the ticked protocols, a click applies it (the same text in the search field, so the same list as typing it), and pencil / bin buttons rename or delete it. Kept in the settings file (`ui.saved_searches`, up to 50), not in `connections.toml`; older versions ignore the key. (`rustconn-core/src/search/saved.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/saved_searches.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **CI runs the ignored GTK tests** — new `gui-tests` job executes all 18 under Xvfb, one process per test (`scripts/run-ignored-gui-tests.sh`)
- **CI launches the GUI headlessly** — new `gui-smoke` job runs `rustconn --version` and a 10 s start under Xvfb, failing on a panic or early exit (`scripts/gui-smoke.sh`)
- **CI lints the RPM spec** — new `rpm-spec` job runs `rpmspec -P` and `rpmlint`; the filtered warnings are documented in `packaging/obs/rustconn.rpmlint.toml`
- **A missing desktop portal is reported** — at startup RustConn asks `xdg-desktop-portal` for its file-chooser backend (3 s limit) and shows one warning toast, plus a log line, when the service or the backend (`xdg-desktop-portal-kde`/`-gnome`/`-gtk`) is absent, instead of file dialogs silently failing
- **More terminals for the 1Password sign-in button** — it tries `xdg-terminal-exec`, `ptyxis`, `kitty`, `foot` and `alacritty` before `gnome-terminal`, `konsole`, `xfce4-terminal` and `xterm`
- **Pulsing indicators respect "reduce animations"** — the tunnel "starting" dot and the active broadcast icon stay still when the desktop turns `gtk-enable-animations` off, and follow the setting live
- **Empty Trash action** — `win.empty-trash` asks for confirmation, then permanently removes everything in the trash (and its vault credentials), now that deleted items stay there until it runs. It is reached from the Trash row in the sidebar. The vault credentials are removed on a worker thread (about 0.7 s each with a KeePass file), so the window stays responsive: the Trash row and the "Trash emptied" toast appear at once, and the header-bar spinner shows until the cleanup ends. The group hierarchy used to rebuild the credential keys includes the trashed groups, so a credential under a deleted group is found. (`rustconn/src/state/connections.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)
- **Trash row in the sidebar** — like Favorites, but at the bottom of the tree and only while the trash holds a deleted connection or group. It expands to the deleted items; right-click an item → **Restore** (the same `win.undo-delete` as the Undo toast) or **Delete Permanently…**, right-click the row → **Empty Trash**. The row updates after a delete, an undo, a restore and an empty, without a restart; it has an accessible label with the item count. Trashed rows carry non-UUID ids, so connect, edit, delete and drag skip them. The context menu of the row is sized to its items, with a scrollbar only for a menu taller than the cap. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/trash.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sorting.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Delete Permanently… on a trashed item** — right-click a connection or group under the Trash row → **Delete Permanently…** asks for confirmation, then removes only that entry from the trash, the single-item counterpart of **Empty Trash**. Its saved vault credential is deleted on a worker thread with the header-bar spinner showing, unless a live connection or another connection still in the trash resolves to the same vault entry; a deleted Web connection's browser data goes too. A group's own trashed connections stay in the trash; restoring one after its group was deleted permanently puts it at the top level instead of under a missing group, where the sidebar never showed it, so its vault key no longer includes that group's path. A connection restored while its group is still in the trash keeps the group, and appears with it when the group is restored. The item menu no longer offers **Empty Trash** next to it; the Trash row still does. (`rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/trash.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/connections.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`, `po/`)
- **Settings → Clients keeps detection results for five minutes and has a Refresh button** — reopening the dialog no longer re-runs every client probe; the refresh button in the Core Clients header forces a new scan. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/detection.rs`)
- **Invalid saved entries are reported, never removed** — loading connections, groups, snippets, clusters and templates now logs a warning for each entry that fails validation (empty name, empty host, port 0) and keeps the data exactly as saved. A test checks that such a config loads and stays byte-identical on disk. (`rustconn-core/src/config/manager.rs`)
- **CodeQL skips `rustconn-cli/tests` and `tests.rs` files** — CodeQL for Rust has no way to exclude inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules, so those stay in scope. (`.github/codeql/codeql-config.yml`)

### Fixed

- **Clear Session Data in the embedded browser asks first** — the browser menu item, now **Clear Session Data…**, erased the site's cookies and saved logins at once. It opens a confirmation that says what will be lost, with **Cancel** as the default and Escape response and a destructive **Clear**; only **Clear** erases the data. (`rustconn/src/embedded_web/navigation.rs`, `po/`)
- **`rustconn-cli run-script` reads a `.kdbx` set on a connection or group ([#411](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/411))** — it read saved passwords from the global KeePass database only, so a host with its own database got no saved password and fell back to its keys. It now opens the database the GUI does: the connection's own, else its nearest group's, else the global one, with the same database password and key file; an inherited password is read from the group's database. (`rustconn-cli/src/commands/run_script.rs`)
- **A subgroup made inside a Cloud Sync Master tree is exported** — creating a group under a parent never told the export channel, so the new subgroup waited for an unrelated change to the tree before the Master wrote it. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`)
- **A save no longer drops connections another process wrote** — the window saved its whole in-memory list over `connections.toml`, `groups.toml` and `trash.toml`, so a `rustconn-cli add` (or a second instance) made while the window was open vanished at the window's next save. Every save now reads the file under the write locks and merges it with the window's list by id against the state it last saw: an entry the other process added is kept, one it removed is removed here too, an edit on one side only is taken, and when both sides edited the same entry the newer `updated_at` wins (this window on a tie). A deletion made in the window is honoured. A file that cannot be parsed is replaced as before, and a file that already matches the merge is not rewritten. (`rustconn-core/src/config/merge.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/config/manager.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`)
- **Connections added with `rustconn-cli` appear in a running window** — the window watches the three files and, a moment after another process rewrites one, merges the change in (`ConnectionManager::reload_from_disk`) and refreshes the sidebar, with no restart. A reload never waits for a disk write: if a save is running it tries again a second later. If the directory cannot be watched the window still merges on save and only loses the live update. (`rustconn-core/src/config/watch.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **A WinRM or Telnet connection now turns green in the sidebar** — a fresh connect never counted the session, so the row stayed on "connecting" while the shell was already open; only reconnect, SSH and Zero Trust did. It now uses the same established-check as an in-place reconnect, counted from below the connect banner. (`rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`)
- **A jump host or key path with a shell metacharacter could run a command** — the `ProxyCommand` that ssh hands to `sh -c` for a jump host (a hop with its own password, an identity file, PKCS#11, or Flatpak) took the hop address and the `-i` key path as written, so a host such as `bastion;cmd` from an imported or synced connection ran locally when the connection was opened. Every such value is now one shell word (single-quoted when it holds anything but letters, digits and `@:./-_%,=+[]`), a hop that starts with `-` is no longer read as an ssh option, and a PKCS#11 provider path with a space or metacharacter is left out, as the tunnel route already did. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/jump_route.rs`)
- **`-f putty` on a real `regedit` export** — `regedit` writes UTF-16 with a byte-order mark, which the importer rejected as unreadable text; UTF-16 (either byte order) and UTF-8 with or without a BOM are now read, in the GUI and the CLI. (`rustconn-core/src/import/putty.rs`)
- **CLI downloads over HTTPS only, with timeouts** — a redirect to `http://` could swap a download whose vendor publishes no checksum (AWS CLI, SSM plugin, gcloud, cloudflared, 1Password CLI), and a stalled server hung the download. Redirects must stay on HTTPS, connecting is limited to 30 s and each stalled read to 60 s. (`rustconn-core/src/cli_download/download.rs`)
- **`pass` and `gpg` stay behind after a timeout** — the caller drops the call on timeout, and the child kept waiting on pinentry; it is now killed with the call. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/pass.rs`)
- **A damaged machine-bound credential store no longer quotes its content in the error** — the parse error names no value. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/encrypted_file.rs`)
- **Tray click now hands the window its activation token on Wayland** — the tray library hands over the compositor's activation token with each click, but `present()` never received it, so KWin treated the raise as focus stealing and only flashed the taskbar entry. The token is now passed to the window before it is presented (`rustconn/src/tray.rs`, `app.rs`)
- **PTY descriptors leaked into every child process** — `openpty` does not set close-on-exec, so the master and slave of each terminal session were inherited by anything spawned afterwards, from any tab; a process that kept the master open could stop a closed session from ever seeing its hangup. `rustconn-pty-sys::open_pty_pair` now sets `FD_CLOEXEC` on both, keeps the OS `errno` in its error, and `pty_wait_readable` reports a closed descriptor (`POLLNVAL`) as `EBADF` instead of "not ready", which made the reader loop spin. (`rustconn-pty-sys/src/lib.rs`)
- **Sync exports were briefly world-readable and not crash-safe** — Simple Sync files were created with the umask and set to `0600` only after the rename (ignoring a failure), with a fixed temp name and no `fsync`; the machine-bound credential store also skipped `fsync` before its rename, so a power loss could leave it empty. All eight temp-and-rename writers (config, both credential stores, sync exports, custom themes, recordings, OpenH264) now share `rustconn_core::fs_atomic::atomic_write`, which creates the temp file with its final mode, `fsync`s it, removes it on any failure and syncs the directory. (`rustconn-core/src/fs_atomic.rs`)
- **An invalid custom log-sanitize pattern was silently ignored** — `SanitizeConfig::with_custom_pattern` dropped a regex that did not compile while keeping it in the list, so a pattern meant to hide a secret hid nothing. It now returns `LogError::InvalidPattern`. (`rustconn-core/src/session/logger.rs`)
- **Passbolt passphrase visible in the process list** — the GPG passphrase went to `go-passbolt-cli` as `--userPassword`, readable by any user in `/proc/PID/cmdline`. It now goes through the child's environment (`USERPASSWORD`), which only the owner can read. The resource password still has to be an argument; this and the RDP gateway `Zeroizing` exception are now documented in `AGENTS.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `SECURITY.md`. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/passbolt.rs`)
- **Six CLI downloads installed without any integrity check** — kubectl, Teleport, Tailscale, Boundary and Hoop are now checked against the SHA256 their vendor publishes for that exact release, and the Bitwarden CLI against GitHub's asset digest; a missing or wrong hash stops the install. The Bitwarden and 1Password installers had not consulted the checksum policy at all. (`rustconn-core/src/cli_download/`)
- **A WinRM argument could be lost in release builds** — `WinRmClient::command` dropped the first argument whatever it was, guarded only by a `debug_assert`. It now replaces only a leading `evil-winrm`. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/winrm.rs`)
- **A host name or option with a line break can no longer add directives to the ssh configuration Midnight Commander uses** — the generated `ssh_config` took the host, jump hosts, `ProxyJump`, custom options and key paths as written, so an imported connection or a Dynamic Folder script could slip in a `ProxyCommand` of its own. A connection with such a value now opens mc without the wrapper, so it gets no jump host and no saved password, and the log names the option (not its value).
- **Known-hosts and key paths with an apostrophe, `${VAR}` or `%` reach ssh as written** — an apostrophe in the Flatpak `known_hosts` path made ssh fail on the option, `${HOME}` in it was expanded, and the mc ssh configuration read `%d` in a key path as a token. A path ssh cannot be given is left out, as before. The remote clean-up after a session recording quotes its paths as well.
- **`rustconn-cli completions` and `man-page` no longer panic in a debug build** — the global `--config` and several subcommands (`--connection` in `group`, `cluster`, `tag`, `history`; `--command` in `snippet add`/`edit`; `--category` in `snippet list`) all claimed `-c`, and `snippet run --var` claimed the global `-v`. Release builds skip clap's check, so only debug builds crashed. A test now validates the whole command tree. (`rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`, `rustconn-cli/tests/integration_tests.rs`)
- **`packaging/obs/rustconn.spec` macros in comments and `%changelog`** — `%description`, `%prep`, `%build`, `%find_lang`, `%check` and `%{?fedora}` in prose were expanded by rpm (rpmspec warned "Macro expanded in comment"); now escaped as `%%`
- **`start <name> cluster=<name>` in a script stopped silently ignoring the cluster** — the script engine parses the option but the GUI never implemented it, so the session started as a plain connection. The script now ends with "cluster=<name> is not supported", and the script reference and User Guide no longer list the option.
- **Bitwarden Unlock no longer freezes the window on a slow keyring** — the saved master password is read from (and, after a typed password unlocks the vault, written to) the system keyring on the worker thread instead of the GTK thread, where a hung Secret Service blocked the whole application for up to 5 s.
- **Monitoring and scripts are stopped when they should be** — closing the main window now stops every monitoring collector (`MonitoringCoordinator::stop_all`), and closing a tab cancels a script still running on it, which used to keep its timer and entry alive. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **A permanently deleted connection leaves workspaces** — once a deletion is final (Empty Trash) the connection is dropped from every workspace profile that listed it; Undo still restores it with its workspace entries. (`rustconn/src/state/connections.rs`)
- **The Smart Cards switch says when it does nothing** — with the Embedded RDP client the switch now reads "Not used in Embedded mode. Set Client Mode to External RDP client." The saved value is untouched. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`)

### Changed

- **A deleted connection or group stays in the Trash until you empty it** — until now a deletion was made permanent when its Undo toast closed (or at once for a bulk delete, which has no Undo), so the Trash row held items only for a few seconds. They now stay in the trash, which is saved across restarts, and the Trash row restores them at any time; only **Empty Trash** removes them and their saved vault credentials. The Undo toast remains as a shortcut, and a bulk delete of several selected items also lands in the Trash. Until the trash is emptied, the password of a deleted connection stays in your vault, and the trash has no size or age limit. (`rustconn/src/toast.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/connections.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Secret Service client updated to oo7 0.7 (ashpd 0.14)** — the GNOME Keyring / KWallet backend and the Flatpak portal path use the new client. The calls RustConn makes are unchanged. oo7 0.7 matches items stored without an `xdg:schema` attribute against the generic schema, and handles unencrypted keyrings. (`Cargo.toml`)
- **Screen-lock and suspend listener moved to zbus 6** — it needs no code change in behaviour. zbus 6 no longer depends on the unmaintained `zvariant` and `zbus_names` crates, so only the tray library still pulls them. (`rustconn-core/src/lock_events/dbus.rs`, `Cargo.toml`)
- **Config file locking uses the standard library** — `File::try_lock` replaces the `fs2` crate; the lock is the same advisory `flock`. (`rustconn-core/src/config/manager.rs`)
- **`rustconn-cli --config` has no `-c` short form any more, and `snippet run --var` has no `-v`** — after a subcommand `-c` already meant that subcommand's own flag, and the docs use `-c <connection>` throughout, so the global short was the one to drop. Use `--config <dir>` or `RUSTCONN_CONFIG_DIR`, and `--var key=value`. (`rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)
- **`scripts/verify.sh` names the tools it could not run** — a closing "NOT CHECKED" line lists every gate skipped for a missing tool, so a green run no longer hides them
- **Translated desktop entry** — the desktop entry now carries `Name`, `GenericName`, `Comment` and `Keywords` in every language of `po/`, merged in with `msgfmt --desktop` when the Flatpak, Snap, RPM, Debian, AppImage, `.deb`/`.rpm` release and Nix packages are built. Before, the translations were extracted into the catalogues but never reached the installed file, and only five hand-written `Comment[xx]` lines were shipped. Adds a translated `GenericName` ("Connection Manager"). (`rustconn/assets/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.desktop`, `po/*.po`)
- **`scripts/release.sh` validates the Nix flake** — when `nix` is installed it runs `nix flake check --no-build` and evaluates `packages.default`; without `nix` the gate is listed as skipped. `packaging/nixpkgs/package.nix` joins `PKG_FILES`, so its version is bumped by `scripts/bump-version.sh` and checked at release.
- **Saving the connection lists no longer copies them** — every `save_*` in `ConfigManager` cloned the whole list into a wrapper just to serialize it; the wrappers now borrow (`Cow`), so a save no longer doubles the memory of a large connection list
- **Fewer processes spawned for detection** — `virsh` (libvirt import dialog) and the `op`, `passbolt` and `pass` secret backends are found by an in-process `PATH` lookup instead of running `--version` before every secret operation
- **Group sync applies updates by index** — `GroupSyncPlan::apply_to` looked every updated item up with a linear scan and every deletion with `Vec::contains`; it now builds an index once, so large groups no longer cost O(N·U)
- **RDP printer discovery no longer parks the runtime** — the `lpstat` queries run in `spawn_blocking` (still bounded by the 2 s limit)
- **`rustconn-cli monitor metrics` help** says it shows the effective monitoring configuration, not live metrics; `cluster create/edit --broadcast` is hidden and documented as the no-op it has been since 0.14.8 (still accepted)
- **Fewer main-loop wake-ups** — `spawn_blocking_with_callback` / `spawn_blocking_with_timeout` (68 call sites) and three start-up waits (tray creation, Bitwarden auto-unlock, secret-backend probe) now wait on a one-shot future instead of a 16 / 50 / 100 ms polling timer.
- **Sidebar search no longer deep-clones every group per keystroke** — `SearchEngine::search_refs` and `DebouncedSearchEngine::search_debounced` take `&[&ConnectionGroup]`; `search` keeps its signature. (`rustconn-core/src/search/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)

### Removed

- **Nine unused credential-resolver methods** — `CredentialResolver::{needs_keepass_migration, migrate_to_keepass, store_unified, resolve_verified, store, retrieve, delete}_with_hierarchy`, `move_credential_on_group_change` and `rename_credential` had no callers (the GUI uses its own `vault_ops`); `resolve_with_hierarchy` stays. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/resolver.rs`)
- **Unused connection-string interner** — `StringInterner`, `interner()`, `InternerStats` and the `intern_*`/`*_interning_stats` functions are gone from `rustconn-core`: the interned strings were thrown away, so the global set only grew and took a lock on every connection add or update. (`rustconn-core/src/performance/`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/`)
- **Unused core modules** — `rustconn_core::cache` (`Cached`, `CacheRef`, `LoadCacheObject`) and `rustconn_core::drag_drop` (`DropConfig`, `calculate_*`, `ItemType`; the GUI has its own in `sidebar`) had no callers and are gone
- **Dead GUI code** — the window actions `copy-username`, `copy-password`, `cluster-from-selection` and `select-item-by-id` (no menu, shortcut or palette entry used them), the `dialogs::keyboard` and `dialogs::adw_dialogs` modules, `block_on_async` / `ensure_main_thread` / `is_main_thread`, the unused tab activity/silence indicator CSS classes, and about 40 functions that nothing called (FreeRDP detection helpers, formatting and accessibility helpers, terminal and dialog getters).
- **Unused `HistorySettings.track_quick_connect`** — nothing read it; configs that still contain the key load unchanged (covered by a test) and it is dropped on the next save. (`rustconn-core/src/models/history.rs`)
- **Unused `SearchEngine::fuzzy_score_optimized`** and its two private helpers — nothing called it outside its own unit tests; `fuzzy_score` is what search uses. `search::benchmark` stays (integration tests and saved-search tests use it). (`rustconn-core/src/search/mod.rs`)

### Improved

- **Every group picker has a search field** — Move to Group, Move Selected, the New Group / New Subgroup parent, the connection editor's Group, the Edit Group parent, Batch Edit's Group, the smart folder's Filter by Group and the export dialog's Group scope now filter as you type, the way the jump-host pickers already did. Matching is case-insensitive substring, so typing `RubyStudio` finds `Import/Dev/AimSmarter/RubyStudio`; the jump-host, tunnel and On startup pickers switch from prefix to substring matching too. The `adw::ComboRow` pickers (Edit Group parent, Batch Edit Group, Settings' On startup) need libadwaita 1.6 for substring matching and keep prefix matching in builds without `adw-1-6`. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/widgets.rs` `enable_string_search`, `enable_combo_row_search`)
- **Tests for the lock listener, the SSH key unlock dialog and the failed-command marker**, with the dialog's outcome decision and the marker's bar placement moved out of GTK code; the keyring save helpers in Settings share one timed helper, and the client version checks use `rustconn_core::proc::wait_bounded`. (`rustconn-core/src/lock_events/dbus.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/key_unlock.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/failed_marker.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/keyring.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`)
- **One tested rule for when mc's ssh gets the saved password** — `mc_ssh::password_plan` replaces checks that were spread over GUI code with no tests. Behavior is unchanged.
- **A jump host's password travels with its hop** — the route builder takes hops that carry their own password instead of two parallel lists, and the tunnel's jump-host route lives in `rustconn-core` (`jump_route::tunnel_jump_args`). Behavior is unchanged.
- **Packaging metadata descriptions** — the AppStream metainfo and `RustConn.doap` now mention WinRM, Web, SOCKS5 and Run Local Script.
- **Packaging fixes** — `packaging/nixpkgs/package.nix` was stuck at 0.19.4 and now follows the release version; 60 `%changelog` entries in `packaging/obs/rustconn.spec` named the wrong weekday (`rpmspec` "bogus date"), and the ignored GTK test `fractional_scroll_position_is_kept`, which failed on VTE 0.84 because it read the scroll adjustment of a terminal that was never placed in a window, now presents it in one first. Nobody noticed the test because no CI job ran the ignored tests.
- **CI** — new `Nix flake` job (runs on pull requests that touch `flake.nix`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`) and `Desktop and AppStream metadata` job (`desktop-file-validate` on the source and merged desktop file, `appstreamcli validate --strict`).

### Documentation

- **The async helpers section of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `rustconn/AGENTS.md` names the helpers that exist** — `spawn_async`, `with_runtime`, `spawn_blocking_with_callback` and `spawn_blocking_with_timeout`; `spawn_async_with_callback`, `block_on_async_with_timeout`, `is_main_thread` and `ensure_main_thread` are gone.
- **`codec download-h264` is in the CLI reference**, and `scripts/check-cli-docs.sh` (run by `scripts/verify.sh`) fails when any subcommand from `--help` has no entry in `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`. (`docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`, `scripts/check-cli-docs.sh`, `scripts/verify.sh`)
- **Docs match the code again** — feature tables in `docs/BUILD.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and the `rustconn-core` crate docs list `web-embedded`, `bitwarden-serve`, `lock-events` and `adw-1-10`; the Protocol trait section shows the real `capabilities()` default and all sixteen `ProtocolCapabilities` fields, and names WinRM and Zero Trust; the split-view note no longer mentions an embedded SPICE widget that was removed in 0.18.0; `rustconn-pty-sys` is documented with its `nix` dependency and as used for every VTE session; `-sys` comments no longer say no CI job builds macOS; the `unsafe` section of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` now points at each crate's own contract instead of repeating it; the attestation example in `docs/INSTALL.md` uses `<version>`. (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/BUILD.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md`, `rustconn-core/src/lib.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/performance/mod.rs`, `rustconn-pty-sys/AGENTS.md`, `rustconn-pty-sys/src/lib.rs`, `rustconn-dock-sys/src/lib.rs`, `rustconn-env-sys/src/lib.rs`)
- **Local tooling** — `CONTRIBUTING.md` lists typos, cargo-machete, cargo-nextest, cargo-deny, cargo-audit, rpmlint and Xvfb with what uses them, and the ~23 GB debug `target/` size
- **Nix `flake.lock` policy** documented in `docs/INSTALL.md` and `flake.nix`: no lock is committed on purpose. The release-verification example no longer names a fixed version.
- **Accessibility and KDE/Wayland in the User Guide** — new Accessibility section (keyboard-only use, screen-reader limits, leaving an embedded session with the keyboard, reduced motion) and a "KDE Plasma and Wayland" troubleshooting entry (tray, portals, external terminal, Flatpak on a KDE host)
- **`Ctrl+Shift+Y` (Reconnect Session) is in the User Guide shortcut table.**
- **`tmux_control` is marked experimental** in its module docs and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: a tested parser and model that the GUI does not use yet.
- **Workspace `split_directions` is documented as not read on restore** — restore always builds a balanced grid; old profiles that carry the field still load.

### Dependencies

- **resvg 0.49 → 0.50** (tray icon renderer; also usvg 0.50, harfrust 0.15, read-fonts 0.46, skrifa 0.49)
- **oo7 0.6 → 0.7.1**, **ashpd 0.13 → 0.14**, **zbus 5 → 6** (direct dependency); removed `fs2`, and the older duplicate copies of `cbc`, `hkdf`, `pbkdf2` and `block-padding` that oo7 0.6 needed (`cbc` stays as a direct dependency)
- Semver-compatible updates: rustls-webpki 0.103.16 → 0.103.17, vnc-rs 0.6.0 → 0.6.1, zstd 0.14.3 → 0.14.4 (zstd-safe 8.3.0, zstd-sys 2.1.3), cc 1.11.1 → 1.11.2, cfg-expr 0.20.10 → 0.20.11, mio 1.2.4 → 1.2.5, zbus and zbus_macros 6.2.1 → 6.3.0, zruntime 0.2.2 → 0.2.3. tokio was already at 1.54.1
- `cargo audit` reports no vulnerabilities; the six "unmaintained" warnings are `atomic-polyfill`, `pin-utils`, `unsafe-libyaml` and, through the tray library, `zvariant`, `zvariant_derive` and `zbus_names`. `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak and Flathub manifests. GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.33.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.7.0, the Snap on `core26` and every pinned CLI download (TigerVNC 1.16.2) are current
- Flatpak: fast_float 8.3.0 → 8.3.3 (VTE build dependency) and waypipe 0.11.2 → 0.11.3, with their `sha256`
- `nix`, `libc` and `socket2` are declared once in `[workspace.dependencies]`
- RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (`rsa`) stays ignored: sspi 0.23.1, winscard 0.4.1 and picky 7.0.0-rc.29 still pin rsa 0.10.0-rc.18

## [0.24.11] - 2026-11-12

### Added
- **Fallback jump hosts — [PR #420](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/420), contributed by Konstantinos Patronas** — a connection or a group can list saved jump hosts to try in order when its Jump Host is unreachable, for SSH, SFTP, RDP, VNC, SPICE and WinRM. Each new connection or reconnect checks for a reachable SSH server, and uses the primary again as soon as it answers. If none answers, the first is tried and the normal error is shown; a connection never falls back to going direct. Set in the connection and group editors, which show the list only for those protocols and only once the connection or group has a Jump Host of its own (saving without one drops it). SPICE over a Unix socket uses no jump host, so it gets no list either. In the CLI it is `--fallback-jump-host` in `rustconn-cli add` and `update`, which both refuse a list for a connection without a Jump Host of its own, since it would never be used. The editor keeps keyboard focus: after Move Up or Move Down it follows the moved entry, after Remove it goes to the entry that takes its place (or to the add dropdown when the list is empty), and after Add it returns to the add dropdown; a second click before the list redraws acts on the same entry. A screen reader hears which host each Move Up, Move Down and Remove button acts on, and the editor's add dropdown has a name of its own. `rustconn-cli connect` does not fail over, so it still warns that a deleted Jump Host is skipped even when a fallback exists. A group's list stays on its device like the group's Jump Host: Simple Sync leaves it out of the shared file and keeps each device's own. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/gateway_failover.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/jump_chain.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/connection/ssh_inheritance.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/connection.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/group.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sync/full_export.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/widgets.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/general_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/builders.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/construction.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_group.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/add.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/update.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/sftp.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Duplicate a connection into a numbered range — [PR #420](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/420), contributed by Konstantinos Patronas** — right-click → *Duplicate as Range…*, or `rustconn-cli duplicate --range`, turns `host01` into `host02` … `host10` with the pattern `host[02-10]`, and `host1` into `host2` … `host10` with `host[2-10]`. The host follows the name when it contains it, or takes its own range such as `10.0.0.[2-10]`. Existing names are skipped, and the GUI copies a saved vault password to each new connection. In the dialog, Enter creates the copies once the patterns are usable, the pattern at fault is marked invalid, and a screen reader reads the preview line with each pattern. A copy that cannot be stored is named in an error dialog rather than counted as created, and a password that cannot be copied to some of the copies gets a warning. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/host_range.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/operations.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/duplicate.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`)
- **Run a local bash script on many SSH hosts — [PR #420](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/420), contributed by Konstantinos Patronas** — right-click connections or a group → *Run Local Script…*, or `rustconn-cli run-script`. Shows live output and a result per host, with a limit on hosts at once, a time limit and a Stop button. Each host's result also has an icon, so a failed host stands out without relying on colour. A screen reader hears each host's status together with its name, the output pane is named after the host it shows, and the end of a run is announced with its counts. Keyboard focus moves to Stop when a run starts and to the host list when it ends, a setup error is read out and marks the script field, and on a narrow window the output goes below the host list. Closing the window or pressing Escape during a run asks whether to stop the script, and closes the window once every host has stopped. Why a host could not start (no `ssh` or `python3`, no `pywinrm`, no WinRM password, a tunnel that did not open) is shown in the interface's language. The script goes over stdin and nothing is saved on the hosts. It is syntax-checked first, runs through each host's saved jump hosts (with failover) and credentials, and a host whose key is not yet known is refused. A run never accepts a new key for a jump host either: every jump host of a run, including the jump host of a WinRM tunnel, must already be in `known_hosts` (`StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`), in the GUI and the CLI, and a jump host with no key of its own still offers `~/.ssh/id_*` and the agent's keys, unless a password is sent along the route. Three cases go through `ProxyJump` and follow your ssh configuration: a chain of several jump hosts whose first one has a key of its own and that sends no password and uses no PKCS#11 provider, a jump host address RustConn cannot pass to a shell safely (such as a comma-separated ProxyJump chain), and the jump hosts past the first of a WinRM tunnel that sends no password. A host with no saved jump host whose own `~/.ssh/config` routes it through `ProxyJump` or `ProxyCommand` is not sent its password, in the CLI as in the GUI, since the bastion's `ssh` would be the one answering the prompt (the issue #191 leak). Global variables in a host or user name (`${name}`) are filled in, in the CLI as in the GUI; a variable that asks at connect leaves that host out in the CLI, which cannot ask, and the host counts as not succeeded in the exit status; the CLI does not read a secret variable from the secret backend, so one whose value is not in the configuration file counts as undefined there. Passwords work too: saved ones in both the GUI and the CLI, and hosts set to ask at connect are each asked before the run, or asked once for all of them with **Use One Password for All Hosts** in the GUI or `--same-password` in the CLI (an empty answer sends no password rather than reusing another host's); the GUI empties those password fields once the run is confirmed. A saved password that takes longer than 30 seconds to read is skipped with a message, in the CLI as in the GUI. `rustconn-cli --quiet run-script` leaves out the progress lines (connections left out, hosts that succeeded, the closing count) and keeps the script's output, hosts that did not succeed, warnings and prompts. Stop, the time limit and a lost connection stop the script on the host, and a script exiting with 255 is no longer reported as a connection failure. `rustconn-cli run-script` builds the jump host route the same way the GUI does, so the first jump host's PKCS#11 token reaches it from the CLI too, and so does its own key when it is the only jump host or a password is sent along the route. (`rustconn-core/src/remote_script.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/jump_route.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_key_setup.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/remote_script.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/key_setup.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/run_script.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/main.rs`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **WinRM connections — [PR #420](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/420), contributed by Konstantinos Patronas** — a new connection type opens a PowerShell session on a Windows machine in a terminal tab, using the external `evil-winrm` client. Supports HTTPS, domain accounts and jump hosts, in the GUI and through `rustconn-cli add -P winrm`, and `rustconn-cli show` prints a WinRM connection's jump host, certificate setting (`Accept Invalid TLS`, as for Web) and the custom arguments the client is given, without a refused secret option; the WinRM page's Jump Host dropdown is named for screen readers, and the sidebar's WinRM filter label goes through translation like the other protocol filters. A saved password is answered at the client's prompt and never appears on a command line. In Flatpak, RustConn finds `evil-winrm` on the host and runs it there, through the jump host tunnel too. When `evil-winrm` is missing, the message says where to get it for this package: on the host for Flatpak, not at all in the Snap. Custom Arguments refuse and drop `-p`/`--password` and `-H`/`--hash` in every form evil-winrm accepts, including an abbreviation (`--pass`), an attached value (`-pSECRET`) and a short-option group (`-np`), so a secret typed there cannot reach the command line either. Run Local Script runs a `.ps1` file in PowerShell on WinRM hosts, using the `pywinrm` library. In Flatpak and Snap, Run Local Script's `pywinrm` library installs from Menu → Components, and a missing `pywinrm` is named before the run starts, with where to get it. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/winrm.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/remote_script.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/import/csv_import.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/dynamic_folder.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/cli_download/components.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/cli_download/install_pip.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/winrm.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/flatpak_components.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/remote_script.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/add.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/connect.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/run_script.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/show.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `docs/SNAP.md`)
- **Browse through an existing SOCKS5 proxy — [PR #420](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/420), contributed by Konstantinos Patronas** — Web connections have a **SOCKS5 Proxy** field (`--socks-proxy` in the CLI) alongside Tunnel Through SSH, including proxies that need a login, which RustConn relays for as long as the browser using it runs. The login's password comes from a secret global variable: the GUI and `rustconn-cli add`/`update --socks-proxy-password-var` refuse a plain one, whose value would sit in the configuration file. Group Sync exports that variable as a template, without its value, whichever connection mentions it first, so the other device knows which secret to fill in. `rustconn-cli add` and `update` refuse the proxy settings the editor refuses (a proxy together with a tunnel, a login without a proxy, a username or a password variable), and `update --socks-proxy ""` removes the login along with the proxy; `rustconn-cli show` prints the proxy, its username and the name of its password variable, never the password. In the editor a **SOCKS5 Proxy** group of its own, apart from the embedded browser's settings, holds both SOCKS5 routes: Tunnel Through SSH, and the proxy's Address, Username and Password Variable, since both apply to external browsers too. It works in the embedded browser, Chromium-based browsers and Firefox, and the tunnel now works with Firefox too; a Firefox behind the proxy or the tunnel keeps private mode (`--private-window` next to its temporary profile), in the GUI and in `rustconn-cli connect`. If no browser can use the proxy the page is not opened, so traffic never goes direct; an external Chromium or Firefox also keeps WebRTC on the proxy, so a page cannot learn this computer's own addresses through it. The GUI now counts Opera as a Chromium-based browser for the proxy and the tunnel, also when it is the system default browser or in `$BROWSER`, and a LibreWolf opened without a proxy gets private mode (`--private-window`) as Firefox does. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/web.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/socks_relay.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sync/group_export.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/web.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_web/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/add.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/update.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/connect.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/show.rs`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Fixed
- **The "Routing changed" warning now notices jump hosts chosen from the picker — [PR #420](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/420), contributed by Konstantinos Patronas** — it compared free-text ProxyJump values only, so re-pointing a connection's saved Jump Host went unseen. Connections that use one will show the warning once after updating, because their route is now recorded for the first time. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/gateway_failover.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`)
- **Tunnelled and proxied browsers no longer leave their profiles behind** — *Open Browser via Tunnel* started Chromium in a fresh `rustconn-tunnel-browser-*` profile that nothing deleted, so the cookies, history and cache of every tunnelled session piled up in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (on macOS in `$TMPDIR`, on disk), and the new SOCKS proxy launches did the same. A proxied browser's profile is now deleted when the browser exits, or when RustConn quits and closes it; *Stop Tracking* leaves it until the browser exits. Profiles left by a crash or by `rustconn-cli connect`, which hands itself over to the browser, are removed at the next start of RustConn, or the next `rustconn-cli connect` that opens a Web page through a SOCKS proxy, once they are 12 hours old and no browser holds their lock, including the old `rustconn-tunnel-browser-*` ones. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/web.rs`, `rustconn/src/external_session.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **A split tab is named after the pane you work in (issue #421)** — a tab holding a split layout kept the name of whichever session joined it last, so clicking into another pane left it on the wrong server. The tab title now follows the focused pane, whether focus moves by a click, by the Focus Next Pane shortcut or because a pane closed, and whatever the pane shows: SSH, Telnet, Serial, Kubernetes, MOSH, Zero Trust, RDP, VNC or a web page. Its tooltip describes the same session (name, host and working directory) and gains a *Panes:* line listing every session in the split; the tab's own tooltip comes back when the split ends. Names such as `R&D` no longer blank the tooltip while the tab is split; the tab's own tooltip that comes back afterwards still can. A rename of any session in the split reaches the tab title, the *Panes:* line and that session's pane header. *Rename Tab…* is offered when the session the tab is named after is a local shell, and renames that session. The window title follows the tab title as soon as it changes rather than at the next tab switch, so the two never disagree; on a tab with a single pane too, it now follows a rename, a group change and the REC and ⇄ markers at once, which matters if you track time by the window title (issue #211). With *Show hierarchy path in header* on, the header subtitle shows the focused pane's group path and follows pane focus. A tab with a single pane is titled as before; its tab menu's *Set Group…* and *Remove from Group* now keep the working-directory line. (`rustconn/src/terminal/split_title.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_menu.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/navigation_actions.rs`, `po/POTFILES.in`, `po/`)
- **A jump host's password no longer reaches the jump hosts behind it in a tunnel** — an RDP, VNC, SPICE, WinRM or Web (SOCKS) connection whose jump host is itself reached through further jump hosts started the tunnel's `ssh` with `-J` for those (or, in Flatpak and with a PKCS#11 token, with a `ProxyCommand` of its own), and every `ssh` started for them inherited the askpass helper that answers with the nearest jump host's password, so a farther bastion could be sent it (the issue #191 leak). When the tunnel sends a password, the farther jump hosts now go through a `ProxyCommand` built by the shared jump host route, which starts each of them without that helper; they must log in with a key or the agent.
