A GUI tunnel has no terminal to ask on, so there a farther jump host that accepts only a password now fails where it used to be sent the nearest jump host's password; a tunnel that `rustconn-cli connect` or `run-script` starts from a terminal may have that jump host ask for its password in the terminal. Sending each farther jump host its own saved password is a later item. A farther jump host whose address RustConn does not pass to a shell (such as a comma-separated ProxyJump chain) keeps `-J`, and the password is then not sent; `create_tunnel` refuses to send it along any such route. The same holds for the WinRM tunnel of `rustconn-cli connect` and `run-script`, which says so on stderr, and in a script run, in the GUI as in the CLI, those farther jump hosts must already be in `known_hosts`. In the Flatpak, `rustconn-cli`'s WinRM tunnel now hands those jump hosts the writable `known_hosts`, as the GUI does. (`rustconn-core/src/jump_route.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/rdp_vnc.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/remote_script.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/run_script.rs`)
- **A tunnel no longer sends its jump host's password when your ssh configuration routes that jump host through a proxy** — an RDP, VNC, SPICE, WinRM or Web (SOCKS) tunnel whose jump host `~/.ssh/config` reaches through `ProxyJump` or `ProxyCommand` started that proxy's `ssh` with the tunnel's askpass helper, so the bastion in front could be sent the jump host's password (the issue #191 leak). The tunnel now asks `ssh -G` first, as an SSH session does, and opens without sending the password when a proxy is declared; in the GUI and in `rustconn-cli connect` and `run-script`. A route RustConn puts on the tunnel's command line overrides the configuration and needs no check. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **SFTP through Midnight Commander signs in with the saved password** — a password-authenticated SSH connection opened in mc stopped at `shell: Waiting for initial line...` with a warning that password authentication is not supported, until the password was typed blind into the tab. RustConn now reads the password as Connect does (the session cache first, then the vault; a connection set to ask at connect is asked once), and the `ssh` that mc starts gets it through the askpass helper from an owner-only file, only when it connects to the connection's own host, user and port; any other `sh://` link opened in that mc gets nothing, and the password never enters mc's environment. Saved jump hosts are reached through RustConn's own `ProxyCommand`, so none is sent the target's password; they log in with a key or the agent, or ask in the tab as before. Where an SSH session would not send the password either (Keyboard Interactive, a PKCS#11 token, a custom routing option, a proxy in `~/.ssh/config`), and for a chain whose farther jump hosts have keys of their own, mc opens without it and a warning says to type it. The same warning appears, and the log says why, when mc's first `ssh` does not have the form RustConn hands the password to, as an mc build with an extra flag would give. When the saved password is refused, it is dropped from the session cache as soon as that mc exits, also when its tab is closed, so a Connect reads the vault again, and the next mc open of that connection, even one made while the first mc is still open or after RustConn was restarted in the same login session, says so and opens without it, so you can type the right one in the tab; the open after that reads the vault again. Every SFTP entry point that opens mc now checks that mc is installed first. A password-authenticated connection that also has a key file, its own or one from its group, gets the password too, as an SSH session does; when the key signs in, the password is never used. Key and agent connections open as before; `rustconn-cli sftp --mc` still leaves the password to you. (`rustconn-core/src/mc_ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mc_launch.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/sftp.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/POTFILES.in`, `po/`)
- **Flatpak: a home folder with a space in its path no longer splits the writable `known_hosts` in two** — SSH sessions, tunnels, port forwards, session recordings and the monitoring probe passed `-o UserKnownHostsFile=<path>` straight to `ssh`, which reads that option as a space-separated list, so `/home/my name/…/known_hosts` became the two files `/home/my` and `name/…/known_hosts`, and host keys were looked up in, and saved to, the wrong place. The path is now passed quoted (`UserKnownHostsFile="<path>"`), as the SSH key setup already did, through one shared helper; a path holding `"` or `\` is left out, and ssh then uses its default `known_hosts`. A `%` in the path is passed as `%%`, so ssh no longer reads it as the start of a token such as `%u`. A GCP IAP connection hands the path to gcloud through the same helper; gcloud splits each `--ssh-flag` value at spaces, so there a path with a space is left out as well. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/tunnel_manager.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/monitoring/ssh_exec.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_key_setup.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/recording.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)
- **Linux without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`: SFTP through Midnight Commander no longer uses a wrapper directory another user created** — without `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, the `ssh` wrapper that carries a connection's jump host and SSH options to mc (issue #255) lived in `/tmp/rustconn-mc-ssh`, which RustConn reused whoever owned it, so a local user who created it first could replace the wrapper script, which runs as you and now also names the file holding the saved password. The directory is now created owner-only (mode 0700), an older one of yours is made owner-only, and one that is a symbolic link or belongs to another user is not used: mc then opens without the wrapper and without the password, and the log says why. macOS, whose temp directory is per user, and Linux desktop sessions with `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` were not affected. (`rustconn-core/src/mc_ssh.rs`, `rustconn-core/Cargo.toml`)
- **macOS: SFTP through Midnight Commander finds a Homebrew `mc` when RustConn starts from the Dock or Finder (issue #422)** — the check found `mc` in `/opt/homebrew/bin` or `/usr/local/bin`, but the tab then started it with the `PATH` the app inherits from launchd (`/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`), so it reported `'mc' is not installed`; from a terminal it worked. mc, and the `ssh` wrapper it goes through, now get the same `PATH` as every other session: the Homebrew prefixes and the sandbox tool directories in front of the inherited one. (`rustconn-core/src/mc_ssh.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Flatpak: a host `evil-winrm` installed through rbenv, rvm or a gem directory in the profile now starts** — RustConn found the client through a host login shell but started it with the Flatpak session helper's environment, which lacks the profile's `PATH` and `GEM_HOME`, so a client that was found could fail to start. It now starts in the same login shell (`flatpak-spawn --host … -- sh -lc 'exec "$0" "$@"' <client> <arguments>`), which passes the path and every argument through unparsed; the password is still typed only at the client's prompt, never on its command line or in its environment. When the client is missing, the Flatpak message now says it must be on the `PATH` of the host's login shell (set in `~/.profile`, not only in `~/.bashrc`). `rustconn-cli connect` builds a WinRM launch from the WinRM handler itself, so the client is never started with another command's arguments. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/winrm.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/connect.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **Ukrainian: one form for "script" and for WinRM hosts** — the interface mixed the genitive «скрипта» and «скрипту»; every string now uses the dictionary form «скрипту», and «на WinRM-гостах» reads «на гостах WinRM» like the other strings. (`po/uk.po`)
- **A slow `Match exec` in `~/.ssh/config` no longer stalls a connect or a script run** — the `ssh -G` check that decides whether a password may be handed to ssh gives up after 5 seconds and then keeps the password back, as it does when the check fails. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`)
- **Flatpak: a home folder whose path a shell would split no longer breaks jump hosts reached through RustConn's `ProxyCommand`** — the writable `known_hosts` path went into the command unquoted, so a home folder with a space, a `;` or a quote in its path broke the command or ran part of it as another command. Such a path is now left out of the `ProxyCommand`: carrying it would take quoting twice, once for the shell and once for ssh, which is not worth it for so rare a home path. Those jump hosts then use ssh's default `known_hosts`. A jump host already known there connects as before; one that is not is handled by the host key policy as usual, so under `accept-new` it still connects, but its key cannot be recorded in the Flatpak's read-only `~/.ssh` and is accepted anew each time. Sessions, SFTP and tunnels that pass the path straight to `ssh` keep it, quoted when it holds a space. (`rustconn-core/src/jump_route.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`)
- **Ctrl+W closes an open dialog, a playback tab, or the window when no tab is open** — Ctrl+W did nothing on the Welcome page, and a recording playback tab could not be closed with it. With no session tab open it now closes the main window the way its close button does: it asks first when detached or external sessions are still running, and hides to the tray when that is enabled. Pressed in a dialog such as Settings, Ctrl+W closed the session tab behind the dialog, in the main window and in a detached window; it now closes the dialog. Inside a terminal Ctrl+W still goes to the shell (issues #197, #216) and Ctrl+Shift+W closes the tab; the Keyboard Shortcuts window now says so under Close Tab. (`rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/shortcuts.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **Ctrl+, and F10 work in a detached session window** — a session moved into its own window had no Settings action and no menu, so Ctrl+, and F10 did nothing there, and Keyboard Shortcuts (Ctrl+?) and About opened on the main window, which could be behind it or hidden in the tray. The detached window now has a main menu button (F10) with Settings, Keyboard Shortcuts, Help and About; like the main window's, F10 goes to the session while keyboard passthrough is on. Ctrl+, opens Settings on the main window and raises it, since Settings belong to the whole application, and Keyboard Shortcuts and About open on the window you are in. (`rustconn/src/detached_window.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **A screen reader names each session view** — Orca announced every terminal as just "terminal", so the panes of a split sounded the same, and an embedded RDP or VNC view as an unnamed image. A terminal, in a tab, a split pane or a detached window, is now named after its session, and follows a rename; a recording playback is named like its tab. An embedded RDP or VNC view is called "Remote desktop" with the connection's name, and its description says how to get the keyboard back: the Toggle Keyboard Passthrough shortcut as currently bound, and Super+Escape for the desktop shortcuts in GNOME. The status line of an RDP tab whose session runs in an external window is now translated. The terminal highlight and failed-command overlays and the theme preview in the connection editor are hidden from screen readers as decoration. The Jump Host dropdowns of the SSH, RDP, VNC and SPICE pages, the new-connection wizard, the group editor, the tunnel builder and the Global Jump Host in Settings now have names of their own. (`rustconn/src/embedded.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/split_title.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/playback.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/highlight_overlay.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/failed_marker.rs`, `rustconn/src/session/vnc.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/advanced_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/vnc.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/spice.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection_wizard/connection_page.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/network_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/tunnel_builder/step_connection.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_group.rs`, `po/`)
- **The sidebar context menu shows a focus ring** — the focused item had only a 15% accent tint and its focus outline was removed, which left it close to invisible in high contrast, and a mouse click left the tint behind. The tint now appears only for keyboard focus, together with a 2px focus ring in the theme's focus colour, as on other libadwaita buttons. (`rustconn/assets/style.css`)
- **Split-pane colours are visible in light and dark themes** — the pane borders, pane headers, tab stripes and sidebar markers of a split used shades such as `#33d17a` green and `#00b4d8` cyan, which have less than 3:1 contrast against a light window. They now use six GNOME palette shades with at least 3:1 against both white and the dark theme's `#222226`: Blue 3 `#3584e4` (unchanged), Green 5 `#26a269`, Orange 4 `#e66100`, Purple 2 `#c061cb`, Teal `#2190a4` (was cyan) and Red 2 `#ed333b` (was pink). The order is kept, so a saved split layout keeps its colours. (`rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`, `rustconn/assets/style.css`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_types.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/split/color.rs`)

### Changed
- **Update every device that shares or syncs a configuration before using WinRM or a SOCKS5 proxy** — RustConn 0.24.10 and older cannot read a `connections.toml`, a Simple Sync file or a Group Sync `.rcn` file that holds a WinRM connection: the whole file fails to load, not only that connection. They also do not know the new SOCKS5 proxy fields of a Web connection, so they open its page directly instead of through the proxy, and saving there drops the fields, which sync then carries to the other devices. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Shorter software-center summary and a more accurate description** — the AppStream summary reads "Manage remote connections" (it had 36 characters, one over Flathub's limit, and named only three of the protocols), and the description no longer claims that no external dependencies are needed: it says that some features use external command-line tools, such as a cloud provider's CLI for Zero Trust connections or a password manager's client. The Debian, OBS (Debian and RPM), Nix and Snap package descriptions say the same in place of "no external dependencies required". (`rustconn/assets/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.metainfo.xml`, `debian/control`, `packaging/obs/debian.control`, `packaging/obs/rustconn.spec`, `packaging/nixpkgs/package.nix`, `snap/snapcraft.yaml`)
- **F1 opens the user guide; the Keyboard Shortcuts window keeps Ctrl+?** — F1 is Help in the GNOME HIG, but it opened the shortcuts window. It now opens [the user guide](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/blob/main/docs/USER_GUIDE.md) in the browser, as does the new **Help** item in the main menu, and it is remappable under Settings → Keybindings like any other shortcut. A shortcut you bound yourself now wins over a default on the same keys, so if you had given F1 to the shortcuts window, or to anything else, F1 still does what you chose. The Keyboard Shortcuts window and Settings → Keybindings show the keys that are in use, so a default your own binding took is no longer listed next to its old action. (`rustconn-core/src/config/keybindings.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/shortcuts.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/keybindings_tab.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `po/`)
- **Additional license terms for the author's code** — from this release, the material in RustConn written by Anton Isaiev carries additional terms under section 7 of GPL-3.0, set out in the new `NOTICE` file: keep the author attribution "Anton Isaiev, Ukraine" in copyright notices and in the About dialog's copyright line and "Made in Ukraine" section (7(b)); a modified version distributed as a separate project is not named "RustConn" alone, uses neither an application ID beginning with `io.github.totoshko88` nor the RustConn icon, and says that it is modified and by whom (7(c)); the author's name is not used to promote a modified version without permission (7(d)); no trademark rights beyond `TRADEMARKS.md` (7(e)). Packaging by an operating-system distribution that does not change the program's features is not a separate project. The license stays GPL-3.0-or-later; 0.24.10 and earlier releases, and code contributed by other people, are not affected. (`NOTICE`, `README.md`)
- **The copyright line names Ukraine** — the About dialog now reads "© 2024-2026 Anton Isaiev, Ukraine", next to its unchanged "Made in Ukraine" section, and the Debian and OBS copyright files and the RPM spec carry the same line; the two copyright files also point to `NOTICE`. (`rustconn/src/app.rs`, `debian/copyright`, `packaging/obs/debian.copyright`, `packaging/obs/rustconn.spec`)
- **Flatpak — FreeRDP 3.33.0→3.33.1** — the bundled FreeRDP used for the external RDP client; version and `sha256` updated. (`packaging/flatpak/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.yml`, `packaging/flatpak/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.local.yml`)

### Documentation
- **RustConn now follows the GNOME Code of Conduct** — it replaces the Contributor Covenant 2.1 for the project's issues, pull requests, discussions and code review. `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` now links to [conduct.gnome.org](https://conduct.gnome.org/) and says how to report a problem, to the maintainer or to the GNOME Code of Conduct Committee; the README has a Code of Conduct section, and CONTRIBUTING.md points to it. (`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`)
- **Use of the name, icon and application ID** — a new `TRADEMARKS.md` covers the unregistered name "RustConn", the application icon and the application ID prefix `io.github.totoshko88`. Official builds, unmodified redistribution, distribution packaging that does not change the program's features, referring to the project by name and saying "based on RustConn" need no permission; a fork distributed as a separate project uses its own name, application ID and icon. (`TRADEMARKS.md`)
- **How AI tools are used in RustConn** — a new "AI-assisted development" section in CONTRIBUTING.md says that the maintainer works with AI coding agents (Kiro, with Claude models) whose configuration is public in `.kiro/` and the `AGENTS.md` files; that every change is read by the maintainer, passes `scripts/verify.sh --tests` and is tested by hand; that Flathub manifests and submissions are written by hand; and that since 12 November 2026 agent-made commits carry a `Co-Authored-By` trailer (most earlier ones, including most of the 0.24.11 cycle, do not). Contributors may use AI tools if they understand and can explain what they submit. (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **Translation policy written down** — CONTRIBUTING.md now says that every language is welcome except Russian, which the project does not ship, as a product decision explained in the [maintainer's reply on PR #413](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/413#issuecomment-6714428433); new languages of the region such as Bashkir, Tatar and Nogai are welcome like every other. The translation issue form, the README and `po/AGENTS.md` say the same. (`CONTRIBUTING.md`, `po/AGENTS.md`, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/translation.yml`, `README.md`)
- **Official sources listed in the README** — a new "Official sources" section under Installation lists the channels RustConn is published through: GitHub Releases with build-provenance attestations, Flathub, the Snap Store, the openSUSE Build Service, the Homebrew tap, the AUR package `rustconn`, FreeBSD ports and the Nix flake. It says that forks are not affiliated with the project and that their bugs belong in their own trackers, while packages from other distributions are reported here. (`README.md`)
- **NOTICE and TRADEMARKS.md ship with the packages** — the release `.deb`, the release `.rpm` and the OBS packages now install `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` as license files and `TRADEMARKS.md` as documentation, so the section 7 terms travel with the binaries. The release `.rpm` previously shipped no license file at all. (`.github/workflows/release.yml`, `packaging/obs/rustconn.spec`)
- **The bug report form asks where RustConn came from** — the required install-source field now lists the official channels, a build from this repository's source, "Another distribution's package" and "Other / a fork", and says that reports about forks are handled in the fork's own tracker. (`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.yml`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): libz-sys 1.1.32→1.1.33, openh264 0.9.9→0.9.10 (with openh264-sys2), rpassword 7.5.4→7.5.5, rustls-pki-types 1.15.1→1.16.0, zip 9.5.0→9.5.1, and patch releases of bit-vec, bit_field, const-oid, crc32fast and errno. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. GNOME runtime 51, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.7.0, VTE 0.84.1 and the pinned TigerVNC 1.16.2 download were already current. resvg 0.50 is a breaking release and waits for its own change; zbus 6 / oo7 0.7 stay deferred as in 0.24.10.

## [0.24.10] - 2026-11-09
### Fixed
- **macOS: the menu-bar icon follows the system style** — the tray icon was a full-colour image that clashed with the other status items. It is now a monochrome template glyph, so macOS tints it for a light or dark menu bar like any native item. (`rustconn/src/tray.rs`, `rustconn/assets/icons/hicolor/scalable/apps/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn-tray-macos.svg`)
- **macOS: Settings no longer shifts and clips a few seconds after opening** — once client detection finished, the Clients page replaced its rows with full install paths and unbounded version strings, which made the dialog wider than its 600 px content width and cut off every page. The path is now one ellipsised line (full text in a tooltip) and the version label is ellipsised too. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`)
- **macOS: the Dock icon is grey in the Clear and Tinted icon styles** — when RustConn runs without a `.app` bundle, its Dock tile is set at runtime, which bypasses the system's monochrome icon styles, so it stayed full-colour next to every other app. The tile now reads `AppleIconAppearanceTheme` and, for Clear or Tinted, uses a grey tile (dark or light to match). Inside a bundle the `.icns` is still left to macOS. (`rustconn/src/main.rs`, `rustconn/assets/icons/macos-dock/`)
- **A broken CLI wrapper is no longer shown as installed with an error for a version** — Settings → Connection showed Tailscale CLI with a ✓ and `/usr/local/bin/tailscale: line 2: …: No such file or directory` in the version column when the wrapper outlived a removed Tailscale.app. A command that exits 126/127 (the shell's "cannot execute" / "not found") now yields no version and is reported as not found. Applies to every Zero Trust CLI on that page. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`)
### Changed
- **macOS: Compact interface is off by default** — it was switched on for new macOS installs to compensate for scaling problems that are now fixed. A fresh install gets the standard chrome, as on Linux. Existing users keep whatever is saved in their settings; the switch is still in Settings → Interface, the primary menu and `Ctrl+Shift+D`. (`rustconn-core/src/config/settings.rs`)
### Improved
- **Settings no longer spins the main loop while it detects clients** — the Clients and SSH Agent pages polled their background result through an idle source, which never sleeps, so the interface ran flat out for the few seconds detection took each time Settings opened. They now poll every 50 ms, as the Secrets page already did. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ssh_agent_tab.rs`)
- **Development builds optimise third-party dependencies** — `cargo run` compiled GTK, VTE and IronRDP at opt-level 0, which made tab overview, redraws and scrolling feel slower than a release build. Dependencies are now built at opt-level 2 in the dev profile; the workspace crates are unchanged. (`Cargo.toml`)
### Documentation
- **macOS: running with `cargo run`** — `docs/MACOS_BUILD.md` now shows the full `cargo run` command with the macOS feature set and explains why the default features fail on `javascriptcoregtk-6.0`. (`docs/MACOS_BUILD.md`)
### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): arc-swap 1.9.4→1.9.5, itoa 1.0.18→1.0.19, notify-types 2.2.0→2.3.0, syn 3.0.9→3.0.10. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; CLI downloads, FreeRDP 3.33.0, cJSON 1.7.19, GNOME runtime 51 and Snap core26 are current.
- **RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (rsa Marvin Attack) — a fix now exists but is still out of reach** — rsa 0.10.0 (2026-11-08) is the patched release, with constant-time arithmetic. RustConn cannot take it yet: `ironrdp-connector` 0.12.0, the latest, pins `picky =7.0.0-rc.26`, and picky, sspi and winscard pin `rsa =0.10.0-rc.18`; the same pins hold sspi 0.23.1, winscard 0.4.1, picky rc.29 and pkcs1 0.8.0 back. The acceptance rationale in `deny.toml` and `.cargo/audit.toml` is updated to say so; it is not exploitable in a local client that exposes no RSA decryption oracle.
- **Flatpak: picocom is fetched from its maintained GitLab home** — picocom development moved from `github.com/npat-efault/picocom` (no push since 2023) to `gitlab.com/wsakernel/picocom`, the homepage Anitya project 3634 tracks. The source and the `x-checker-data` URL template now point there, as proposed upstream in [flathub PR #552](https://github.com/flathub/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn/pull/552) by razzeee; the release workflow copies this manifest into the Flathub repository, so the change has to live here too. Version 3.1 and its checksum are unchanged — the two archives are byte-identical. (`packaging/flatpak/*.yml`, `packaging/flathub/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.yml`)
- **zbus 6 / oo7 0.7 deferred** — both are breaking releases (zbus 6 removes the blocking API and renames `Value` to `Variant`), and the Linux tray's `ksni` 0.3.7 is still on zbus 5, so moving now would build two zbus versions. Planned for when ksni follows.

## [0.24.9] - 2026-11-09

### Added
- **Azerbaijani (az) added — [PR #414](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/414), contributed by Naim Shafiev** — a full catalogue of this release's strings, with terminology aligned to the GNOME and Firefox Azerbaijani translations. Azerbaijani can be chosen in Settings → Interface → Language (it maps to the `az_AZ.UTF-8` locale), and the translator is credited in the About dialog. (`po/az.po`, `po/LINGUAS`, `rustconn/src/i18n.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `README.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md`)

### Fixed
- **The CodeQL scan runs again** — every job of the in-repo CodeQL workflow failed. Rust was set to the `autobuild` build mode, which CodeQL does not support for Rust (only `none`); the Python and Ruby jobs found no code, because the only Python and Ruby files live under `scripts/` and `packaging/`, which the config excludes; and the Actions job's results were refused because the repository's CodeQL default setup was still enabled. The workflow now scans `actions` and `rust` (build mode `none`), and default setup is to be turned off in Settings → Code security, as the workflow header now explains. The CodeQL change was contributed in PR #414 by Naim Shafiev. (`.github/workflows/codeql.yml`, `.github/codeql/codeql-config.yml`)

### Changed
- **Bitwarden's "Keep the CLI running" process now stops when the screen locks or the computer suspends** — with the switch on, one `bw serve` process holds the unlocked vault in its memory, and until now it stayed running while the user was away. It is now stopped on the same screen-lock and suspend signals that remove RustConn's SSH keys from the agent, on Linux and macOS. Nothing asks for the master password afterwards: the session key stays in RustConn, as it does with the switch off, so the next vault operation starts a fresh `bw serve` and only that first operation is slower. A process busy with an operation at that moment is stopped as soon as the operation finishes, so a lookup in flight is never cut off, and the stop never delays the lock or the suspend. In the Flatpak this needs the same opt-in `flatpak override` as the SSH key removal; in the Snap a screen lock is seen and a suspend is not. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden_serve.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/lock_events/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `docs/BITWARDEN_SETUP.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md`)

### Dependencies
- **resvg 0.48 → 0.49** — the SVG renderer for the tray icon (supersedes Dependabot PR #404). The calls RustConn makes (`usvg::Tree::from_data`, `resvg::render`) are unchanged in this release. (`rustconn/Cargo.toml`)
- **CI — taiki-e/install-action v2.87.25 → v2.88.1** (Dependabot PR #403), pinned by commit SHA as before, in every CI and release job that installs a Rust tool. (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`)
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): crypto-bigint 0.7.10→0.7.11, notify 8.2.1→8.2.2, usvg 0.48.1→0.49.0 (with resvg), uuid 1.29.1→1.29.2, zip 9.4.2→9.5.0. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests.

## [0.24.8] - 2026-11-08

### Added
- **Dynamic Folder scripts can define the full connection, not just host/port/username (issue #410)** — a dynamic-folder entry now accepts an optional `protocol_config` object in the native RustConn shape, so a generated connection can carry anything the connection editor sets: an SSH `startup_command` (e.g. `tmux new-session -A -s my-session`), an `auth_method`, RDP display options, and so on. The object is the same tagged form the config file uses (`{"type": "Ssh", "startup_command": "…", "auth_method": "agent"}`); its `type` tag decides the protocol and default port, and the `protocol` string is then only used to warn on a mismatch. Entries without `protocol_config` behave exactly as before, so existing scripts are unaffected. Because the connection stays read-only, the configuration is re-applied on every refresh instead of being lost when the folder regenerates, which removes the need to hand-edit a generated connection only to have the edit discarded on the next refresh. (`rustconn-core/src/models/dynamic_folder.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/dynamic_folder.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **A KeePass database per connection or group (issue #411)** — with the KeePass backend, a connection or a group can now name its own `.kdbx` file instead of the single global one, so personal and corporate vaults can live side by side. Groups get a **KeePass Database** row on the Identity page (independent of Default Credentials); connections get one beside the Vault password source. A connection reads from its own file, else from the nearest group above it that sets one, else from the global database; an empty field inherits. Only the file changes — the database password, key file and YubiKey slot still come from Settings → Secrets, so a database that needs a different passphrase is not covered yet. The path is device-local: it is stripped from the full sync export, never part of Group Sync, and a remote edit keeps this device's own value. Switching a connection's password source away from Vault drops its path. The unlock prompt for a locked database now names the override file. Every KeePass operation follows the same resolution, not just the connect: saving, loading and testing a password in the connection editor, a group's own password (saved, loaded and deleted in the group's database), copying a connection's password on paste, deleting it with the connection, renaming it on an edit or group move, and the VNC password prompt. A move that changes which database a connection resolves to leaves the entry in the old file and logs it, rather than copying between databases; the password is entered again. The **KeePass Database** rows are hidden when the KeePass backend is not active, and the file picker filters on `*.kdbx` with a named filter. `rustconn-cli` follows the same resolution: `secret get`, `secret set`, `secret delete` and `add --password-stdin` open the connection's database instead of always the global one and print which file they used, and the override is set with `--kdbx-path` on `add`, `update` and `group edit` (an empty value on `update`/`group edit` clears it) and shown by `show` and `group show`. Backend-to-backend credential transfer still uses the global database only. (`rustconn-core/src/models/connection.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/group.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/resolver.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sync/full_export.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/sync.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/widgets.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_group.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/connection_dialogs.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/rdp_vnc.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/`, `po/`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)

### Fixed
- **Embedded RDP on macOS sent the wrong keys (issue #412)** — on the macOS GTK Quartz backend the GDK hardware keycode is an Apple virtual keycode (`NSEvent.keyCode`), not an evdev keycode, but the embedded (IronRDP) client ran it through the Linux evdev→AT scancode table. Every key landed on a neighbouring one: on a German layout `qwertz` arrived as `345687`, `123` as `90ß`, and Backspace as `#`, because e.g. Apple keycode 12 (`q`) is evdev 12 (`3`). A new `macos_keycode_to_scancode` table maps the stable Apple virtual keycode straight to the IBM PC AT (Set 1) scancode the RDP server expects — letters, digits, punctuation, modifiers (Command → Super/Win), the function row (F1–F19), the navigation cluster (Help → Insert) and the numeric keypad (including Keypad `=`), with the `E0` prefix on extended keys. `send_ironrdp_key` now selects the evdev table on Linux/BSD and the Apple table on macOS at compile time; the external FreeRDP client, which was never affected, is unchanged. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/input.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/input.rs`)

- **The tray icon now shows the real number of open sessions, and a failed connection no longer logs a false "Failed to terminate session" warning** — both read the core `SessionManager`, which the GUI never registers a terminal session in, so the tray always said 0 and every connection that failed with its tab still open logged `WARN Failed to terminate session: Session not found`. The tray now uses the same count as the quit confirmation (tabs, detached windows and external viewers), the always-failing terminate calls are gone, and the Session Manager dialog shows one "active session(s)" count instead of a second "tracked" figure that was always 0. Opening the RDP editor without FreeRDP installed (normal on macOS, where the embedded client needs none) is now logged at `info` instead of `warn`; a launch that needs FreeRDP still reports it. (`rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/sessions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/session_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sessions.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/detect.rs`)

- **Closing one of two sessions of the same connection no longer clears its "connected" mark in the sidebar** — the sidebar counted open sessions per connection, and one session ending is reported by several paths (the close-tab action, the notebook's page-closed hook, the terminal's child-exit handler, an embedded client's Disconnected state). Each report took one off the counter, so closing one tab of a connection with two open sessions dropped it to zero and cleared the check mark, and a failure on one session could mark a connection failed while another was still connected. The sidebar now keeps the set of open session ids per connection, so a session is removed once however many paths report it, and a reconnect in the same tab is not counted twice. External viewer sessions pass their session id to the sidebar for the same reason. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_types.rs`, `rustconn/src/external_session.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/`)
- **"System keyring" can be chosen for a saved master password on macOS** — the Secrets tab gated the keyring choice on the Linux `secret-tool` binary, which macOS never has, so picking "System keyring" to store the KeePass, Bitwarden, 1Password, Passbolt or portable-file secret was reverted and every opening of Settings logged `WARN secret-tool not found`. On macOS the choice is now gated on the Keychain probe the backend itself uses. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/detection.rs`)
- **Logs name the KeePass database a lookup used** — the debug line for a KeePass credential lookup now carries the `.kdbx` path, so a per-connection or per-group database (issue #411) can be told apart from the global one, and `rustconn-cli secret get/set/delete` include the path in KeePass errors. The credential-resolve timing line is now `op="connect.resolve"` instead of `ssh.connect.resolve`, because RDP, VNC and the other protocols resolve through the same code. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/tracing/perf.rs`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`)

### Changed
- **Code scanning moved to an in-repo CodeQL configuration** — the repository's CodeQL scan is now defined by a committed workflow (`.github/workflows/codeql.yml`) and config (`.github/codeql/codeql-config.yml`) instead of GitHub's UI-only default setup, so its scope is reviewable in a PR. The language set (actions, python, ruby, rust) and the default query suite are unchanged; the workflow uses `github/codeql-action@v4` (v3 is deprecated in December 2026) and a per-language job-level concurrency group, and `paths-ignore` keeps non-product trees (Rust integration tests and benchmarks, packaging and developer tooling, build output) out of the scan. Thirteen pre-existing high/critical alerts were reviewed and dismissed as false positives: each flagged a `#[cfg(test)]` fixture — a hard-coded test passphrase or a synthetic certificate-mismatch message — not a production secret or log. (`.github/workflows/codeql.yml`, `.github/codeql/codeql-config.yml`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): arc-swap 1.9.3→1.9.4, cc 1.11.0→1.11.1, flate2 1.1.10→1.1.11, inout 0.2.5→0.2.6, libz-sys 1.1.31→1.1.32, lzma-rust2 0.24.0→0.25.0, serde_spanned 1.1.2→1.1.3, smallvec 1.16.4→1.16.5, toml 1.2.0→1.2.1, toml_datetime 1.1.2→1.1.3, toml_edit 0.25.18→0.25.19, uuid 1.29.0→1.29.1, zip 9.4.0→9.4.2. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. Bundled Flatpak FreeRDP (3.33.0) and cJSON (1.7.19) are at their latest releases, every pinned CLI download (TigerVNC 1.16.2) is current, and `ironrdp-rdpdr` 0.9.0 is still the latest release, so the 0.24.7 known issue about embedded RDP shared-folder names still applies.

## [0.24.7] - 2026-11-06

### Added
- **The Kubernetes connection editor can pick a context from the kubeconfig** — the Context field keeps its free-text entry but gains a button that lists the contexts defined in the kubeconfig the connection names (or `$KUBECONFIG` / `~/.kube/config` when the field is empty), with the current-context marked. The list is read with `serde_yaml`, taking only the context names and `current-context` and ignoring every other kubeconfig field; a missing or unreadable file simply offers nothing to pick. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/kubernetes.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/kubernetes.rs`)
- **A crash from a Rust panic is now recorded to a local log, and the next launch says so** — a panic writes its message, location, backtrace and the build version to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/rustconn/crash-<timestamp>.log` (the OS data directory where there is no state directory). The next start shows a banner with an **Open Log** button; once the log has opened the logs are cleared so a handled crash does not reappear (if it cannot be opened, the banner stays and a toast names the file). A panic the program survives (one caught internally, or one that only ends a background thread) is removed again on a clean exit, so the banner appears only when the previous run really did not finish. The log is owner-readable only, stays on the machine and is never sent anywhere — review it before sharing, as a panic message can contain formatted data. This catches Rust panics only; a `SIGSEGV` inside a C library (GTK, VTE) does not come through here. (`rustconn-core/src/crash_report.rs`, `rustconn/src/main.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Run a saved snippet from the Command Palette, and save a terminal selection as a snippet** — the palette's new `!` prefix lists snippets and runs the chosen one; because the palette hangs off the main window, it always asks for confirmation first, so a snippet is never sent to the wrong terminal when a detached window or split pane holds focus (issue #315). The terminal right-click menu gains **Save Selection as Snippet…**, which opens the snippet editor with the selection of the terminal you right-clicked pre-filled (also in a split pane or a detached window) for you to name and trim — nothing is stored without your action. (`rustconn-core/src/search/command_palette.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/command_palette.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/snippet.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/config.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/snippets.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/snippet_actions.rs`)

### Fixed
- **A "Save N Files" RDP download no longer hangs on "Downloading…" when the clipboard channel is gone, and a failed save is now shown as a toast (issue #390)** — if the CLIPRDR channel was unavailable when a file-contents request was made, the request returned silently and the button sat on "Downloading…" forever, because the error was raised through the clipboard backend that no longer existed. The failure is now reported straight through the session event channel, so the batch unwinds. A batch that could not save every file also raises an error toast, not just a 3-second inline status the user could miss. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/commands.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/polling_handlers.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP now locks the remote file clipboard during a copy, so a file copied in the session survives a focus change (issue #390, Fix B)** — the embedded clipboard channel now advertises `CAN_LOCK_CLIPDATA`. When the server also supports it (Windows does), IronRDP sends a Lock PDU after the Format List Response whenever the server announces a file list, carries the `clipDataId` on each File Contents Request, and expires each lock on its own. Without it a server was free to drop the File Stream data the moment its clipboard changed — the "copy a file in the session, switch to another app, come back and Save" race that the earlier Fix A could not cover, because the focus-return format announce replaced the clipboard before the download even began. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/clipboard.rs`)
- **A minimal RDP server that rejects the external client now says which client to use (issue #409)** — against a screen-sharing server that implements only the rdpgfx/AVC (EGFX) capability set, such as KRDP, the external FreeRDP client advertises the full standard RDP capabilities in Demand Active and the server tears the session down during the capabilities exchange (`CAPABILITIES_EXCHANGE` / `STATE_RUN_FAILED`). That failure used to surface as a bare `RDP connection failed (exit status …)`. It now reports that the server only speaks the embedded client's protocol and tells the user to set the connection's Client Mode to Embedded, which negotiates only the EGFX caps and connects to the same server. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`, `po/`)

### Changed
- **Importing connections with passwords no longer freezes the window** — storing the imported credentials in the chosen secret backend now runs on a background task with a toast when it starts and another reporting how many were stored, instead of blocking the UI for the whole batch. The connections and groups still appear at once; only the credential storage, which is several seconds per credential for a CLI-backed backend such as Bitwarden, moves off the main thread. (An earlier fix had already stopped these passwords going to the system keyring regardless of backend; this removes the remaining freeze, W4/FC §6a.) (`rustconn/src/state/sync.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/connection_dialogs.rs`, `po/`)
- **Opening the Passbolt vault and the export output folder now goes through the desktop portal** — both used to spawn `xdg-open` (or `open` on macOS); they now use `gtk4::UriLauncher` / `gtk4::FileLauncher`, so they let the desktop choose the handler and work inside the Flatpak sandbox. The external file manager for an `sftp://` browse is deliberately left spawning directly, because it must inherit RustConn's ssh-agent socket, which a portal launch would not pass on. The now-unused `open` crate dependency is dropped. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/export.rs`, `rustconn/Cargo.toml`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): zip 9.3.0→9.4.0. Dropped with the `open` crate: open 5.4.5, is-docker 0.2.0, is-wsl 0.4.0. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. Bundled Flatpak FreeRDP (3.33.0) and cJSON (1.7.19) are at their latest releases, and TigerVNC, the only pinned CLI download, is current at 1.16.2.

### Known Issues

- **Embedded RDP shared folders show only the first letter of their name, and a folder with a non-ASCII name (e.g. Cyrillic) is refused** — since 0.24.3 the embedded client uses `ironrdp-rdpdr` 0.9, which announces the share name as UTF-16 as MS-RDPEFS asks, but Windows reads it as 8-bit characters. A share named `Downloads` therefore appears as `\\tsclient\D` (reported upstream for Windows 11 and Server 2022/2025), and one named `Загрузки` is refused by Server 2019 and 2025 with `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL` (the session continues and a toast names the folder, issue [#407](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/407)). The external FreeRDP client is not affected and shows both names correctly, so set the connection's Client Mode to External if you need the share name. The ASCII case is fixed upstream in [IronRDP#2342](https://github.com/Devolutions/IronRDP/pull/2342) (not yet released), and a fix for non-ASCII names is proposed in [IronRDP#2383](https://github.com/Devolutions/IronRDP/pull/2383) ([IronRDP#2382](https://github.com/Devolutions/IronRDP/issues/2382)); RustConn picks them up with the next `ironrdp-rdpdr` release that carries them.

## [0.24.6] - 2026-11-05

### Added
- **SSH keys RustConn adds to the agent itself expire after 12 hours and are removed on exit** — when a multi-hop `ProxyJump` connect or an SFTP / Midnight Commander launch needs a key the agent lacks, RustConn runs `ssh-add -t 43200` without prompting (30-second limit), records the key (and its certificate), and removes exactly those keys on exit over the agent socket. Keys already in the agent before RustConn's add, or added in Settings → SSH Agent, are never touched; a key you re-add with `ssh-add` in a terminal after RustConn added it is still removed on lock, suspend or exit. Legacy PEM/PKCS#8 keys are added only to an empty agent and rely on the 12-hour lifetime, as do keys added by `rustconn-cli sftp` when it cannot clean up. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/own_keys.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/wire.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/keyfile.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/sftp.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, and others)
- **The same keys are also removed on screen lock and suspend (Linux)** — a new D-Bus listener (`lock_events`, non-default `lock-events` feature, always on in the GUI) watches login1 `PrepareForSleep` and the freedesktop/GNOME screensaver `ActiveChanged`, holding a sleep delay inhibitor so the removal finishes before suspend. Unreachable sources are logged and skipped. Flatpak and Snap get no new permissions: in the Flatpak lock/suspend removal is an opt-in `flatpak override` (`docs/INSTALL.md`); in the Snap screen lock works, suspend is not detected. (`rustconn-core/src/lock_events/`, `rustconn-core/Cargo.toml`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `docs/INSTALL.md`, `docs/SNAP.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, and others)
- **The same keys are also removed on screen lock and sleep (macOS)** — a new isolated FFI crate, `rustconn-lock-sys`, observes `com.apple.screenIsLocked` and `NSWorkspaceWillSleepNotification` and feeds them into the same `lock_events` subscribers through `lock_events::notify`. Sleep is not delayed for the removal; an unfinished removal completes on wake. (`rustconn-lock-sys/`, `rustconn-core/src/lock_events/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `scripts/verify.sh`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, and others)
- **Encrypted SSH keys are unlocked once per session in RustConn's own dialog** — a connect, reconnect, first jump host, or SFTP / mc launch with a passphrase-protected OpenSSH key the agent lacks now opens an **Unlock SSH Key** dialog (three attempts); the key goes into the agent for 12 hours and is removed on exit, lock and suspend like the other keys RustConn adds. The passphrase is never stored or read from a vault. Cancel, a refusing agent (e.g. 1Password) or a held certificate fall back to `ssh`'s own terminal prompt; a Cancel is remembered for that key and agent socket until RustConn quits, and legacy PEM/PKCS#8 keys or an agent that does not answer get no dialog. Concurrent connects share one dialog, and the SSH editor warns that Agent Forwarding exposes unlocked keys. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/unlock.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/own_keys.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/key_unlock.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/key_unlock.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/ssh.rs`, `po/`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, and others)
- **Vietnamese (vi) added — [PR #408](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/408), contributed by dinhkarate** — a full catalogue brought up to this release's strings, selectable in Settings → Interface → Language, with the translator credited in the About dialog. (`po/vi.po`, `po/LINGUAS`, `rustconn/src/i18n.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)

### Fixed
- **A connection's key sequence is now sent when the connect first waits for the port check** — the Automation key sequence and split-view drag-and-drop registration were skipped for SSH, Telnet and Mosh connects that ran the (default) pre-connect port check. Both now run whenever the session is created, including after the SSH key unlock dialog. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/key_unlock.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/unlock.rs`)
- **A wedged ssh-agent can no longer freeze RustConn or `rustconn-cli`** — `ssh-agent -s`, `ssh-add -l/-d/-L` and `ssh-keygen -lf` calls are now killed after 5 seconds, and the agent socket connect gives up after 3 seconds. Key removal and fingerprint lookup talk to the agent socket directly instead of spawning processes. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/wire.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/keyfile.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`)
- **An embedded RDP session no longer ends when the server refuses a shared folder (issue #407)** — with `ironrdp-rdpdr` 0.9, a Windows Server 2025 refusal of a shared folder ended the session with `[payload error] PDU error`, because RustConn's backend did not implement `remove_drive`. The folder is now released, a toast reports the refusal once per tab, and the session continues; session-ending RDP errors now log their full cause chain. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/rdpdr.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/session.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`, `po/`, and others)
- **Embedded RDP resize against a Linux server (xrdp) now follows the window** — the resize layout now always reports a Landscape primary monitor (IronRDP reported Portrait for tall windows), and because xrdp never reactivates after a resize, **Server OS → Linux** now resizes by reconnecting. On **Auto**, RustConn waits 3 seconds for the server to apply a layout and otherwise switches that session to reconnect-on-resize; **Windows** keeps the seamless path. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/commands.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/resize_watch.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/resize.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, and others)
- **Terminal connection status is now accurate after connects and reconnects** — a reconnected AWS SSM (or other Zero Trust) session no longer stays yellow, a failed reconnect no longer turns green, and a first connect no longer turns green on RustConn's own banner. All status detectors now read only the client's output after the banner and use one rule (shell prompt, including `❯`/`➜`, or a full line of output that is not a password prompt, progress line or known failure), and they disarm on reconnect; reconnects now also restart session recording. (`rustconn/src/terminal/output_start.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/session_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Toasts and banners show names with `&`, `<` or `>` as typed** — names like `R&D` used to break a toast because libadwaita parsed the text as Pango markup. All toasts and banners now go through `toast::plain_toast` / `toast::plain_banner` with markup off, enforced by a clippy `disallowed-methods` rule; the `pass` status now shows `<gpg-id>` instead of `&lt;gpg-id&gt;`. (`rustconn/src/toast.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/detached_window.rs`, `.clippy.toml`, `po/`, and others)
- **Georgian and Uzbek can now be chosen in Settings → Interface → Language** — both catalogues shipped but were missing from the language list and the locale mapping. They are added along with Vietnamese, and a test now checks every code in `po/LINGUAS`. (`rustconn/src/i18n.rs`)

### Security
- **SSH monitoring can no longer accept a host key before you answer the host-key question** — the status detector did not recognise OpenSSH's `(yes/no/[fingerprint])?` prompt, so the monitoring probe could start with `StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new` and write the key to `known_hosts` while you were still deciding. The prompt now holds the session on "connecting", a changed-key warning counts as a failure, and all background ssh runs (monitoring probe and its jump hosts, SFTP probe, recording copy over `scp`) now use `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`. `accept-new` remains only on connects you start yourself where the question cannot be shown: a connect whose stored password goes through askpass (and its password jump hops, issue #203) and Midnight Commander's `sh://` link in the Flatpak; a changed key is still refused there. In `rustconn-core`, `ssh_tunnel::build_nested_proxy_command` now takes a `HopHostKeys` value instead of a bool flag. (`rustconn/src/window/protocols.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/monitoring/ssh_exec.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/recording.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Changed
- **SSH key passphrases are no longer stored in or read from a secret backend** — the unused `key_passphrase` field is gone from `Credentials`, so no backend writes or returns it and SSH login no longer depends on a vault. Passphrases saved by earlier releases are ignored, not deleted; remove them with `secret-tool clear application rustconn key key_passphrase`, `pass rm rustconn/<connection id>/key_passphrase`, or `security delete-generic-password -s rustconn -a '<connection id>/key_passphrase'` on macOS. Encrypted-file entries drop it on their next rewrite. (`rustconn-core/src/models/credentials.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/libsecret.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/pass.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/macos_keychain.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/encrypted_file.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`, and others)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): lzma-rust2 0.23.0→0.24.0, svgtypes 0.16.1→0.16.2, toml 1.1.8→1.2.0, toml_edit 0.25.17→0.25.18, toml_writer 1.1.3→1.2.0, winnow 1.0.4→1.0.5, zip 9.2.2→9.3.0. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests.
- **Flatpak/Flathub — FreeRDP 3.32.2→3.33.0** — the bundled FreeRDP used for the external RDP client. (`packaging/flatpak/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.yml`, `packaging/flatpak/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.local.yml`, `packaging/flathub/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn.yml`)

## [0.24.5] - 2026-11-02

### Added
- **Bitwarden is much faster with an optional persistent CLI** — the Bitwarden backend used to start a fresh `bw` process for every vault operation, and each one paid the Bitwarden CLI's Node.js startup cost (about 2.7 s on a typical machine), so a single credential lookup that runs several searches took on the order of 13 seconds. A new **Keep the CLI running (faster)** switch in Settings → Secrets → Bitwarden keeps one `bw serve` process running for the session instead: the unlocked vault stays in its memory and answers each operation over a local loopback socket, which brought unlock to about 1 second and a root-search retrieve of a 56-item vault to under a second in testing. The switch is off by default; when it is on, the backend still falls back to starting one `bw` per call automatically if the CLI is too old to serve or the running process stops answering, so a lookup never fails for want of the faster path, it only loses the speed-up. The persistent process is shut down when the vault is locked or you log out, so the unlocked vault does not outlive the session. The master password and every saved item now travel to the CLI in a request body rather than on the command line, so they no longer appear in the process list while a save is in flight. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden_serve.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/config/settings.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/mod.rs`, `rustconn/Cargo.toml`)
- **Remote monitoring now supports MikroTik RouterOS** — the monitoring bar previously worked only on Linux hosts, because it reads `/proc/*` and `df`, and a RouterOS device answers every one of those with "bad command name" (its SSH runs the RouterOS console, not a POSIX shell). RustConn now detects RouterOS on first contact and switches to its command set: CPU, memory and storage from `/system resource`, and network throughput from the summed `/interface` byte counters. CPU load is reported directly, so the bar is correct on the first sample; memory, disk and throughput populate as on Linux. Load average is not available on RouterOS and shows as N/A. Detection and the switch are automatic — nothing to configure. (`rustconn-core/src/monitoring/parser.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/monitoring/collector.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/monitoring/metrics.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **The embedded RDP toolbar can hide the Windows-only tools for a Linux server (#366)** — the toolbar's quick-actions menu launches Windows admin consoles (Computer Management, Device Manager, Registry Editor, Services and the rest) and the Scripts menu runs PowerShell or CMD, all by sending Windows key sequences. Against a Linux RDP server (GNOME Remote Desktop, KDE's KRdp, xrdp) they do nothing, so the RDP connection editor gains a **Server OS** choice under Features: set it to **Linux** and both menus are hidden for that connection, leaving Copy, Paste, Ctrl+Alt+Del and Auto Type, which work on any server. **Auto** (the default) and **Windows** keep the tools shown, so existing Windows profiles are unchanged. (`rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Fixed
- **The "Bitwarden vault is locked" banner cleared after unlocking through a connection** — the startup banner that warns the selected password backend cannot store secrets yet was only re-checked at launch and after saving Settings, so unlocking the vault by opening a connection left the banner up even though the vault was then unlocked. A successful credential lookup now re-probes the backend and clears the banner. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **Imported passwords are stored in the password backend you actually chose** — importing connections (Remmina, MobaXterm, `.rdp` and the rest) wrote every imported password to the system keyring, whatever backend was selected. If that backend was Bitwarden, KeePass, 1Password or an encrypted file, the imported connections then came up with no password, because RustConn looked for it in the chosen backend and the keyring is not where it had gone. Imported credentials are now written to the selected backend, so they are found on the next connect. (`rustconn/src/state/sync.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`)
- **Remote monitoring now works on OpenWrt and other BusyBox-based Linux hosts (#405, #406)** — the metrics command used GNU `df -x`, which BusyBox does not implement, so the disk section came back empty and the bar reported 0% for the root filesystem. The command also excluded `overlay`/`squashfs`, exactly the filesystems OpenWrt boots from. The collector now runs plain `df -Pk` and filters pseudo-mounts in the parser, keeping `/` (the writable overlay) and `/overlay` and dropping `/rom`. The IP list falls back to `hostname -i`, and `/etc/openwrt_release` supplies the distro name on releases without `/etc/os-release`. Contributed by [@shafiev](https://github.com/shafiev). (`rustconn-core/src/monitoring/parser.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Documentation
- **Bitwarden setup guide covers the persistent `bw serve` CLI** — a new "Keep the CLI running (optional, faster)" section explains what the **Keep the CLI running (faster)** switch does, compares per-call spawning with the kept-running process, says why the switch is off by default (the unlocked vault stays in that process's memory and is served over a local loopback socket), and lists what holds either way: the automatic fallback to one `bw` per call, shutdown on lock or logout, and secrets sent in the request body rather than on the command line. (`docs/BITWARDEN_SETUP.md`)
- **User guide documents RouterOS monitoring and the RDP Server OS choice** — the Remote Monitoring section now names MikroTik RouterOS as a supported host, gives the RouterOS source for each metric (`/system resource`, summed `/interface` counters), notes that load average shows as N/A there, and states that BusyBox `df` on OpenWrt is supported. The RDP Quick Actions section explains that the tools are Windows-only and that setting **Server OS** to **Linux** under Features hides the Quick Actions and Scripts menus for that connection. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible, transitive): aes 0.9.3→0.9.4, cc 1.10.0→1.11.0, crc32fast 1.5.3→1.5.4, ctutils 0.4.7→0.4.8, openssl 0.10.81→0.10.82, openssl-sys 0.9.117→0.9.118, unicode-script 0.5.8→0.5.9, zip 9.2.0→9.2.2. A `cargo update` refresh for the release; `cargo deny check advisories` is clean. IronRDP stays on the October per-crate releases adopted in 0.24.3.

## [0.24.4] - 2026-11-01

### Fixed
- **Embedded RDP to GNOME Remote Desktop (and over an SSH bridge) no longer stalls on connect (#392, #381)** — a GNOME Remote Desktop server, or a GNOME-RDP server reached through an `ssh -L` forward, completes the TLS upgrade and then never answers the embedded client's NLA/finalize, so the session hung on "Starting RDP" and timed out even though Remmina and GNOME Connections reach the same host. That post-TLS stall already handed the session to the external FreeRDP client, but through the generic path, which kept the default `HYBRID_EX` negotiation and an explicit `/bpp` — the two things these servers reject. The stall now gets the same TLS-only treatment as the KDE Plasma (KRdp) case (#366): the external client is pinned to `/sec:tls` with a negotiated colour depth and a resizable smart-sizing window, which is exactly how a working `xfreerdp … /sec:tls` command connects. The retry fires once; a server that rejects the TLS-only path too surfaces the real "server incompatible" error instead of looping. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **AppImage release build failed on `.DirIcon`** — a newer `appimagetool` continuous build stopped creating `.DirIcon` from `usr/share/icons` on its own, so the AppImage job failed with ".DirIcon is not valid". The release workflow now copies the 256×256 icon into the AppDir root and writes `.DirIcon` explicitly, and no longer silences a missing-icon copy. (`.github/workflows/release.yml`)
- **Embedded RDP showed evenly spaced horizontal stripes across the desktop (#389)** — IronRDP reports each framebuffer damage region as an `InclusiveRectangle`, so the pixel at its `right`/`bottom` edge is part of the region, but the session loop sized the rectangle with a plain subtraction (`right - left`, `bottom - top`), which is one short on each axis. Every update therefore left its last row and last column unpainted. With RemoteFX a server frame arrives as a stack of short full-width bands, so those dropped bottom rows lined up into thin horizontal stripes over the whole desktop (and a one-pixel unpainted column on the right edge); the stripes showed on both Auto and Native display scaling because the gap was in the framebuffer, not in the draw-time scale. The region is now converted with `+ 1` on width and height, and the existing clamp keeps the last band from reading past the framebuffer. This was an xrdp/GNOME-Remote-Desktop session, but it affects any server whose updates do not cover the whole surface. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/session.rs`)

### Security
- **CodeQL `rust/cleartext-logging` alerts triaged — no secret is logged** — the seven high-severity alerts GitHub reported against `rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs` (the `secret set` success lines) and the embedded-RDP broker-routing unit test were confirmed false positives: each `println!` prints only the connection name and username, and the test formats a connector built from a password-less config. A canary test now pins the fact that `ironrdp_connector::Credentials` derives a non-redacting `Debug` (ironrdp-connector 0.12.0), and a `SECURITY` note at the `Credentials` construction site forbids `{:?}`-formatting a connector, config or credentials that can carry a real password, so a future regression is caught at build time rather than by a scanner. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/connection.rs`)

## [0.24.3] - 2026-11-01

> 💜 The embedded Remote Desktop Connection features in this release — the
> graphics pipeline and AVC444, the microphone, automatic reconnection,
> connection broker support, Happy Eyeballs dialling and the wider `.rdp`
> import/export — come from [@pony-svg](https://github.com/pony-svg)'s
> contribution in [#402](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/pull/402).
> Thank you!

### Added
- **Remote working directory on the tab tooltip** — a terminal tab's tooltip now shows the session's current remote directory as its last line, updated live as you `cd` around. It reads VTE's OSC 7 current-directory report (emitted by the same `vte.sh` that drives shell integration), so it fills in on a host that sends OSC 7 and stays absent otherwise. The `file://host/path` URI is parsed directly for its path, so the directory shows for a genuinely remote host rather than only a local one. The line is maintained independently of the host, group and broadcast tooltip lines, so assigning a group, toggling broadcast or renaming the tab all leave it in place. (`rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`)
- **OSC 133 shell integration — prompt navigation and a failed-command marker** — when the remote shell emits the OSC 133 semantic-prompt sequences, RustConn now tracks where each prompt is and makes two things possible. **Previous/Next Prompt** (Ctrl+Shift+[ and Ctrl+Shift+], remappable in Settings → Keybindings) scroll the terminal to the prompt above or below the current view, so you can move between commands without hand-scrolling. A **failed-command marker** — a short red bar in the left margin — marks the prompt of any command that exited non-zero, so failures stand out while scrolling. Both build on the `vte.shell.postexec` termprop already read for the Command-finished monitoring mode: the prompt row is captured at command-end (vte4 0.10 binds only `postexec`) and the exit code attached to it. Like that mode, it needs RustConn built with VTE 0.78 or newer and shell integration sourced on the *remote* side (most modern distributions ship `vte.sh` for bash/zsh/fish); where either is missing the keys are no-ops and no markers appear, with no other change. On a build below VTE 0.78 the whole path compiles to a no-op. The prompt index is per session, lives only in memory, and is dropped with the tab; marks that scroll out of the buffer are discarded so a stale row is never a jump target. (`rustconn/src/terminal/mark_registry.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/failed_marker.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/termprops.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/highlight_overlay.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/tab_lifecycle.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/config/keybindings.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/shortcuts.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `README.md`, `po/*.po`)
- **Auto-capture keyboard shortcuts for remote desktop (Windows App parity)** — automatically inhibits system shortcuts (such as `Alt+Tab` and `Super` key) and disables tab view shortcuts when an embedded RDP/VNC viewer gains focus, forwarding all hotkeys directly to the remote session like Windows App. Controlled by a new toggle in Preferences → Interface → Appearance. (`rustconn-core/src/config/settings.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)
- **Responsive embedded RDP dynamic resizing and crisp rendering** — lowered resize hysteresis and thresholds for responsive dynamic resolution adaptation without jitter, and enforced pixel-direct filtering for sharp text display. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/drawing.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/resize.rs`)
- **RDP microphone, smart card and connection broker settings (Windows Remote Desktop parity)** — the RDP connection editor gains **Microphone** and **Smart Cards** switches (Features) and a **Load Balance Info** field (Connection), the counterparts of Remote Desktop Connection's audio recording, smart card and RD Connection Broker settings. They reach FreeRDP as `/microphone`, `/smartcard` and `/load-balance-info:`, from the GUI launch and from `rustconn-cli connect` alike (`freerdp::freerdp_device_and_broker_args`). The embedded IronRDP client gained the microphone and broker redirection in this release (see the entries below), so only smart cards remain external-client-only. Smart cards stay external-client-only, like FIDO2, and do not force FreeRDP: Remote Desktop Connection writes `redirectsmartcards:i:1` into every `.rdp` file it saves, so every imported Windows profile would otherwise have left the embedded viewer. Smart cards need a FreeRDP built with PC/SC; the Flatpak's bundled FreeRDP is not (`-DWITH_PCSC=OFF`). (`rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/freerdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP sessions reconnect by themselves after a network drop (Windows Remote Desktop parity)** — when the connection of an established embedded session breaks (a read or write on the transport fails), the session now reconnects automatically, as Remote Desktop Connection does: up to 20 attempts (`rdp_client::AUTO_RECONNECT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`), waiting 1, 2, 4 and 8 seconds and then 10 seconds between them (`rdp_client::auto_reconnect_delay`), with the banner counting the attempts. The client reports such a drop as the new structured `RdpClientEvent::ConnectionLost` instead of a generic error string, so a session the server ends (logoff, administrator disconnect) still just ends. An attempt that cannot reach the server waits and tries again; a refused sign-in or a server the embedded client cannot serve ends the attempts and is reported as before. Clicking **Reconnect** connects at once and stops the automatic attempts, and closing the tab cancels a pending one. On by default, like the Windows client; **Reconnect Automatically** (Features) turns it off per connection, and `.rdp` files carry it as `autoreconnection enabled`, which the exporter used to write as a fixed `1`. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/session.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **Per-connection Windows key combinations** — the RDP connection editor gains **Windows Key Combinations**, Remote Desktop Connection's *Apply Windows key combinations*: *Follow the app setting* (the default, which keeps the Settings → Interface auto-capture switch in charge), *On this computer*, *On the remote computer*, or *Only in full screen*, which also follows the window going in and out of full screen while the session has focus. `.rdp` import and export carry it as `keyboardhook`. (`rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`)
- **Leave Fullscreen button** — in fullscreen the header that slides in at the top edge now ends with a Leave Fullscreen button, the counterpart of the Restore button on Remote Desktop Connection's connection bar. (`rustconn/src/window/fullscreen_header.rs`)
- **The `.rdp` importer reads the settings Remote Desktop Connection writes** — `use multimon` and `screen mode id` now set the external window mode (all monitors, full screen, or a custom size when the file carries one), `session bpp` the colour depth, `audiocapturemode` the microphone, `redirectsmartcards` smart cards, `redirectwebauthn` FIDO2 redirection, `enablecredsspsupport:i:0` turns NLA off and `loadbalanceinfo` the broker routing value. `remoteapplicationmode:i:0` is honoured, so a desktop profile with leftover RemoteApp fields no longer imports as a RemoteApp. `authentication level` is deliberately not read: its `0` would let a file someone sent switch certificate checking off. The exporter writes `audiocapturemode`, `redirectsmartcards`, `redirectwebauthn` and `loadbalanceinfo` from the connection instead of fixed values. (`rustconn-core/src/import/rdp_file.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/export/rdp_file.rs`)
- **The embedded RDP client gets the graphics pipeline, AVC444 and the microphone (IronRDP October 2026 releases)** — RustConn now depends on IronRDP crate by crate on the 27/28 October release train (`ironrdp-connector` 0.12, `ironrdp-session` 0.13, `ironrdp-egfx` 0.5, `ironrdp-dvc` 0.10, …) instead of the `ironrdp` 0.17 meta crate, which still pins the July stack. Three things follow. The connector announces the graphics pipeline in the early capability flags (`support_dyn_vc_gfx_protocol`, MS-RDPEGFX 1.5.1), which a Windows host needs before it opens the channel — without it such a host kept the session on bitmap updates, soft with RemoteFX and heavy without. `ironrdp-egfx` decodes AVC444, AVC444v2, ClearCodec, Planar and RFX Progressive and composites surfaces and caches itself, and `ironrdp-session` paints the result like any other update, so RustConn's own surface compositor in `gfx_handler.rs` — written when ironrdp-egfx decoded but did not composite (issue #262) — is gone, the AVC420-only capability override with it, and the pipeline no longer needs OpenH264 to be usable. And the microphone runs in the embedded viewer over the MS-RDPEAI `AUDIO_INPUT` channel (`ironrdp-rdpeai`): the core reports the format the server opened (`RdpClientEvent::MicrophoneOpened`), the GUI captures the default input device with cpal (`audio::RdpMicrophone`) and returns 16-bit PCM packets (`RdpClientCommand::MicrophoneData`), so switching the microphone on no longer moves the session into a FreeRDP window. A connection broker's Server Redirection now arrives as a structured outcome the embedded client follows to the session host (see the broker entry below). (`rustconn-core/Cargo.toml`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/`, `rustconn/src/audio.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **Connection brokers, shared-folder refresh and bulk compression in the embedded RDP client** — three things the October IronRDP releases made possible. A connection with **Load Balance Info** no longer moves into a FreeRDP window: the embedded client sends the value as the X.224 routing token and follows the broker's Server Redirection to the session host, with the session ID, routing token and password cookie the broker returns (`connection::follow_redirection`; the cookie is zeroized and never printed), up to two hops. The PDU parsing and the first-hop `REDIRECTION_SUPPORTED` advertisement are unit-tested and a normal connection is unaffected, but the full redirection has not been exercised against a live RD Connection Broker farm. Shared folders tell Windows when a file changes on this computer — the directory watcher already saw the changes, but the notify response it needed (`ClientDriveNotifyChangeDirectoryResponse`) only arrived in `ironrdp-rdpdr` 0.9, and nothing polled the watcher; the session loop now does, once a second — so Explorer refreshes by itself. And XCRUSH bulk compression is negotiated, which shrinks every PDU the server sends; it was off because a resize used to lose the decompressor's history (issue #200), which `ironrdp-session` 0.12 fixed. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/connection.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/session.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/rdpdr.rs`)
- **CLI usability for headless provisioning — six improvements to `rustconn-cli`** — found while scripting test connections. (1) `add --group NAME` and `update --group NAME` now report whether the group was **created** or already **existing**, with its resolved name and ID (`Added connection '…' to group 'ops' (created, ID …)`), so a typo no longer silently spawns a second group; the group ID is printed too, matching `group create`. (2) An explicit `--format table` is now honoured verbatim even when stdout is piped — the terminal-detection downgrade to JSON applies only when no `--format` was given, so `… | grep` on a table keeps working while a bare piped command still defaults to JSON (clig.dev).
