(3) `add --password-stdin` reads a password from stdin into a `SecretString` and stores it in the configured secret backend — the same vault the GUI uses — never in the connections file, argv, output or logs (requires the `secret-management` feature). (4) `add --from-file PATH` (or `-` for stdin) bulk-adds connection definitions from a YAML or JSON document (a `connections`/`groups` object or a bare array, parsed through the existing `Connection`/`ConnectionGroup` serde models), reporting how many were added and naming each skipped entry with its reason. (5) `add --provider aws_ssm` now validates at `add` time that `--host` is an EC2 instance ID (`i-` followed by hex) and rejects a plain hostname with a non-zero exit code, instead of failing deep inside the AWS CLI at connect time. (`rustconn-cli/src/cli.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/add.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/mod.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/error.rs`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)

### Fixed
- **Embedded RDP and VNC sessions timed out on hosts with an unreachable IPv6 address** — the embedded clients connected with `TcpStream::connect("host:port")`, which tries the resolved addresses one after another. When a name resolves to both an AAAA and an A record and the IPv6 address never answers, as on a VPN that hands out IPv6 addresses it does not route, the first attempt used up the whole connect timeout and the session failed with `Operation timed out` without ever trying the IPv4 address that works. Connecting now races the addresses (RFC 8305, "Happy Eyeballs"): families alternate, each attempt gets a 250 ms head start, and a refused address hands over at once, so such a host connects over IPv4 in about a quarter of a second. The same dialler is used by the embedded RDP and VNC clients, by a broker's redirect to its session host, by *Check if online*, and by *Test Connection*. The MPTCP setting now also applies to direct embedded RDP connections in builds with RD Gateway support, which used plain TCP regardless. Connections through an RD Gateway are dialled by `ironrdp-mstsgu` and are unchanged. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/dial.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/connection.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/vnc_client/client.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/host_check.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/testing/mod.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP sessions played no sound unless a folder or printer was shared** — with audio set to *Play on this computer* and nothing shared, the embedded client joined the sound channel (RDPSND) without the device redirection channel (RDPDR). A Windows host starts sound only once RDPDR is joined too, so the server never sent a format list or a single sample, and nothing reported a problem. FreeRDP registers RDPDR whenever sound is on for the same reason; the embedded client now does as well. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/connection.rs`)
- **The Quality performance mode no longer offers the server a lossy codec** — Quality asked for lossless bitmaps but also advertised the RemoteFX codec, which is lossy and which a server prefers when it is offered, so the request for lossless output was undone. That is what an embedded session shows whenever the server does not open the GFX pipeline, as some Windows hosts and policies never do, and text arrived soft and smeared. Quality now offers no codec, so the server sends lossless 32-bit bitmaps, which take noticeably more bandwidth; Balanced still offers RemoteFX for links where that matters, and Speed is unchanged. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/connection.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP text was uneven on fractional-scaling displays** — with Display Scale *Auto* the remote desktop is requested at the widget's logical size and stretched to the display, and on a 125% or 150% display that stretch used Nearest filtering, which repeats every fourth or every second row and column, so strokes came out with uneven widths. The framebuffer is now drawn with Nearest only when each remote pixel lands on a whole number of device pixels (Native, or Auto at 100% or 200%), and with bilinear filtering otherwise. For pixel-exact text on a fractional display, Display Scale *Native (full HiDPI)* still avoids the stretch altogether. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/drawing.rs`)
- **`.rdp` files saved by Remote Desktop Connection could not be imported or opened** — `mstsc` saves `.rdp` files as UTF-16LE with a byte-order mark, and the importer read them as UTF-8, so importing one, or double-clicking it, failed with an invalid-UTF-8 error. A UTF-8 file with a byte-order mark lost its first key the same way, which refused the file outright when that key was `full address`. The byte-order mark now decides the encoding (UTF-16LE, UTF-16BE or UTF-8), and a file without one is read as UTF-8. (`rustconn-core/src/import/rdp_file.rs`)
- **Touchpad smooth scroll and high-velocity mouse wheel no longer panic in RDP sessions** — kinetic scrolling and high-resolution wheel events can emit large deltas that exceed RDP's 9-bit two's-complement limit `[-256, 255]`. Large deltas are now chunked into safe increments of at most 120 (standard Windows `WHEEL_DELTA`), and horizontal/touchpad scrolling is scaled smoothly, preventing connection drops from `ironrdp-pdu` panics. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/commands.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/input.rs`)

### Documentation
- **Microphone, smart card and connection broker settings documented** — the user guide describes the new RDP switches and the Load Balance Info field, corrects the FIDO2 note that `.rdp` export did not carry it, and lists every `.rdp` field the importer now reads. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Automatic reconnection, Windows key combinations and Leave Fullscreen documented** — new user-guide sections under RDP, and the fullscreen notes mention the new button. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Graphics pipeline and embedded microphone documented** — a new user-guide section explains Automatic graphics mode, AVC444 and why a Windows host needs the pipeline announced, and the microphone notes no longer send the session to FreeRDP. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Broker redirection and shared-folder refresh documented** — the user guide's Load Balance Info section describes the embedded client following the broker, and the shared folders notes mention the automatic refresh. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Translations for the new strings** — the RDP microphone, smart card, broker, automatic-reconnection, Windows-key-combination and Leave Fullscreen strings, and the two remote-desktop shortcut-capture strings the POT already carried, are translated in all 17 catalogues. (`po/*.po`)

### Groundwork
- **tmux control-mode parser and session model (core only, no UI)** — the GUI-free foundation for a future `tmux -CC` integration: a streaming parser for the control-mode line protocol (`%begin`/`%end`/`%error` blocks, `%output` with octal un-escaping, and every `%window-*`/`%session-*`/`%pane-*`/flow-control notification) and an in-memory window/pane/session model that folds those events, keyed by tmux IDs. It lives in `rustconn-core` with no GTK/VTE dependency and is covered by golden-stream tests from the tmux wiki examples, including chunk-boundary robustness (every byte offset) and non-UTF-8 payloads. No tabs, splits, reconnect or UI yet — that is the 0.25 epic; this only de-risks the protocol layer. (`rustconn-core/src/tmux_control/`)

### Dependencies
- **IronRDP moved to the October 2026 per-crate releases** — the embedded RDP client now depends on `ironrdp-connector` 0.12, `ironrdp-session` 0.13, `ironrdp-egfx` 0.5, `ironrdp-pdu` 0.11, `ironrdp-rdpdr` 0.9, `ironrdp-rdpeai` 0.2 and the rest of the 27/28 October train, crate by crate, instead of the `ironrdp` 0.17 meta crate that still pinned the July stack. This is what unlocks the graphics pipeline, AVC444 and the microphone (see Added). (`rustconn-core/Cargo.toml`)
- **Updated** (semver-compatible, transitive): js-sys 0.3.106→0.3.107, log 0.4.34→0.4.35, wasm-bindgen 0.2.129→0.2.130 (with js-sys/web-sys/futures/macro), yuv 0.8.19→0.8.20, zip 9.1.3→9.2.0. A `cargo update` refresh for the release; `cargo deny check advisories` clean. `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests.

## [0.24.2] - 2026-10-30

### Added
- **RDP clipboard direction control (users reported the one-way clipboard workaround)** — the RDP connection editor's "Clipboard Sharing" switch is now a four-way selector: Off, Remote to local only, Local to remote only, or Both directions. One-way sync previously needed a hand-written `/clipboard:direction-to:` custom argument. The external FreeRDP client maps the choice to `/clipboard:direction-to:…,files-to:…` and the embedded IronRDP backend enforces it in-app. Backward compatible: a profile written before this feature loads as Both (clipboard was on) or Off, and the old on/off state is kept in sync so a downgrade still reads correctly. (`rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/freerdp.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/`)
- **QUIC (quicssh) preset on the SSH ProxyCommand row — SSH over QUIC via [moul/quicssh](https://github.com/moul/quicssh)** — the ProxyCommand row gains a QUIC button that fills in `quicssh client --addr %h:%p`, giving an ordinary SSH connection a QUIC transport with connection migration (roaming between networks) and UDP NAT traversal for ISP-NATed hosts. Because it is a preset and not a new protocol type, jump hosts, exports, vault keys, connection testing and reconnect all apply unchanged. The tooltip reports whether `quicssh` was found and notes that the Flatpak/Snap builds only see binaries reachable inside the sandbox. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/ssh.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/protocol/detection.rs`)
- **Auto-hide the sidebar on connect (issue #401)** — a new opt-in switch in Settings → Interface (Connections group). When enabled, opening a session collapses the connection sidebar to free space; it reappears when you reveal it with the sidebar toggle or when the last open session closes. Off by default, so existing behaviour is unchanged. (`rustconn-core/src/config/`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)
- **MobaXterm import and export round-trip more session types** — the `.mxtsessions` importer and exporter previously handled only SSH, RDP, VNC and Telnet and silently dropped the rest. Both now cover Serial, MOSH and Web (Browser) sessions, the importer additionally maps Rsh/Rlogin (to Telnet) and FTP (to SFTP), and every session type RustConn has no equivalent for (XDMCP, the file browser, the local shell, AWS S3, WSL) is now recorded as a named skipped-entry warning instead of vanishing. FTP-imported-as-SFTP carries a warning that the transport, port and authentication differ. A Serial session preserves baud rate, data bits, parity, stop bits and flow control and carries the COM/`/dev/tty*` device verbatim for the user to retarget. Round-trip tests confirm a Serial, Web or MOSH connection survives export and re-import. (`rustconn-core/src/import/mobaxterm.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/export/mobaxterm.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/import/traits.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/import/mod.rs`)

### Fixed
- **An imported MobaXterm SFTP session no longer round-trips back out as SSH** — the importer mapped MobaXterm session type 7 (SFTP) and type 0 (SSH) through the same path, always producing an SSH connection, so exporting an SFTP connection and re-importing it silently downgraded it. Type 7 now imports as `ProtocolConfig::Sftp` while type 0 stays `Ssh`. (`rustconn-core/src/import/mobaxterm.rs`)

### Documentation
- **New feature coverage for 0.24.2** — the user guide, README and CLI reference document the quicssh ProxyCommand preset, the RDP clipboard direction selector (replacing the old `/clipboard:direction-to:` custom-argument workaround) and the opt-in auto-hide-sidebar-on-connect setting. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)
- **All 17 translation catalogues completed for the release** — the new quicssh, RDP clipboard direction, sidebar and MobaXterm strings were translated across every catalogue, and the Ukrainian catalogue was reviewed for DSTU terminology and the «з'єднання» connection noun. (`po/*.po`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible, transitive): ctutils 0.4.6→0.4.7, libbz2-rs-sys 0.2.5→0.2.6, utf8_iter 1.0.4→1.1.0, zcheapstr 1.1.0→1.2.0, utf_types added 1.0.0. A `cargo update` refresh for the release; `cargo deny check advisories` is clean. `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests.

## [0.24.1] - 2026-10-28

### Added
- **On/off switch for multi-line paste confirmation (issue #399)** — the `confirm_multiline_paste` safe-paste guard, which shows a preview and asks before pasting text that spans multiple lines as a pastejacking defence, was configurable only by hand-editing `settings.toml`. The Terminal tab of Settings now has a "Confirm multi-line paste" switch next to the other paste controls; it reflects the stored setting, and toggling it takes effect live on save through `configure_terminal_with_settings`. No behaviour change to the guard itself. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/terminal_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/mod.rs`)

### Fixed
- **The AppImage now uses its bundled translations, so Settings → Interface → Language takes effect (issue #400)** — the saved language was persisted and applied at startup, but the AppImage never found its own translation catalogues: an AppImage is mounted at a random temporary path exported as `$APPDIR`, and `locale_dir()` only looked in Flatpak, Snap, macOS bundle, user-local, XDG and system locations, so gettext was bound to the host's `/usr/share/locale`, which has no `rustconn.mo`, and every language fell back to English even after a restart. Locale lookup now resolves `$APPDIR/usr/share/locale` (with a fallback to `../share/locale` next to the executable's `usr/bin` directory) ahead of the user-local and system directories, and only when it actually holds a `rustconn.mo`, so a normal install is never misrouted. The OBS AppImage recipe shipped no catalogues at all; it now compiles `po/*.po` into the bundle as the GitHub release AppImage already did. Flatpak, Snap, deb, RPM, Nix and macOS were not affected. (`rustconn/src/i18n.rs`, `packaging/obs/AppImageBuilder.yml`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated** (semver-compatible, transitive): aws-lc-rs 1.18.2 → 1.19.0, aws-lc-sys 0.46.0 → 0.47.0, libz-sys 1.1.30 → 1.1.31. A `cargo update` refresh for the release; `cargo deny check advisories` is clean before and after. `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests.

## [0.24.0] - 2026-10-28

### Fixed
- **Switching the preferred secret backend drops the old backend's unlock state (W1)** — rebuilding the secret-manager chain on a settings save cleared the per-connection credential cache but left Bitwarden's process-global unlock marks (the session key, the verify timestamp, the last-sync timestamp) and the global-secret cache in place. After switching the preferred backend away from Bitwarden, a stale session key kept answering the fast path and a global secret resolved from the old backend could be served for up to its cache TTL. The switch now clears the Bitwarden session state and the global-secret cache — but only when the preferred backend actually changes, so flipping an unrelated read-only or root-search toggle still keeps an unlocked session (no re-lock, no speed regression). (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`)
- **Tunnel askpass helper script is created atomically with 0700 (W4, review SEC-4)** — `write_askpass_script` created the helper with `File::create` and only chmodded it to 0700 afterwards, so for a brief window it existed world-readable and a symlink planted at its (random) path could have redirected the write. It now opens the file with `create_new` (O_EXCL) and mode 0700 set at open time, matching the tunnel secret file. The script holds no secret (only the name of the env var to read); this is defence-in-depth. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`)
- **A mouse selection in a terminal no longer disappears while it is being dragged when the remote side redraws on a timer (contributed by Christian Petri, @Cyb0rk)** — with byobu (or any tmux status bar with a clock) on the remote host, the selection was dropped every second, so selecting more than a few characters was nearly impossible. VTE deselects when output arrives before the drag ends, and protects its own PTY by not reading from it during a drag. RustConn owns the PTY and feeds VTE directly, which bypassed that pause. The output loop now waits while the primary button is down and VTE has a selection, and resumes on release or when the terminal loses focus. Nothing is dropped: the relay's bounded queue fills and the child is held back, as with VTE's own PTY. A click that goes to a mouse-tracking application (tmux with mouse on, vim) creates no selection and does not pause output. Closing the tab in the middle of a drag no longer leaves the output loop waiting for a release that never comes, and closing the tab or reconnecting in the middle of a drag no longer stalls the window while the session's PTY threads are stopped. (`rustconn/src/terminal/selection_hold.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/pty_relay.rs`, `rustconn/src/terminal/mod.rs`)
- **The current prompt line is no longer held back on prompts that use `❯` and similar glyphs** — on a host with an oh-my-bash `powerline-multiline` prompt the last line (`❯ ` and the cursor) did not appear in a tab until more output arrived, although a separate terminal showed it at once. Since 0.23.6 the OSC 52 filter that sits in front of VTE treated a bare `0x9d` byte as the 8-bit OSC introducer, but in UTF-8 that is a continuation byte inside ordinary characters (`❯` is `e2 9d af`, as are box-drawing characters such as `╝` and many Nerd Font glyphs), so the rest of the line was buffered as an OSC string; it happened with the OSC 52 setting off too. The filter now follows VTE, which decodes UTF-8 before parsing: the 8-bit controls are recognised only as the encoded code points `c2 9d` and `c2 9c`, and `CAN`/`SUB` and the other C1 controls abandon an open string. What VTE is fed is equivalent to the stream it would have read: the same text and controls, the same OSC strings, minus the OSC 52 offers the filter takes. Each sequence the filter removes (an offer, a query, an over-long string, or an OSC string left open when the session ends) is replaced by a single `CAN`, which VTE ignores on screen and which ends whatever the filter let through just before it, so a lone `ESC`, a UTF-8 lead byte or an abandoned OSC string cannot pair with the bytes that follow the gap, and a removed offer can no longer turn the text after it into an OSC 52 that VTE would act on. Offers VTE would also read as OSC 52 are no longer missed when control characters it ignores sit inside the string or between `ESC` and `]`, and an OSC string still open when the session ends is no longer handed to VTE, where the next `BEL` shown in the tab could complete it. Only a genuinely incomplete escape or a lone `c2` is held at the end of a read. (`rustconn/src/terminal/osc52.rs`)

- **The browser SOCKS tunnel listens on loopback only, whatever `GatewayPorts` says** — the tunnel was started with `-D <port>` and no bind address, so a `GatewayPorts yes` in the user's `~/.ssh/config` made the SOCKS proxy listen on every network interface and anyone on the local network could route traffic through the user's SSH or Tailscale host without authenticating. It is now `-D 127.0.0.1:<port>` for both OpenSSH and Tailscale SSH, the same address the readiness probe and the browsers already use. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`)
- **"Open Browser via Tunnel" ignores repeat clicks while its tunnel starts** — the action raises the tunnel in the background (up to 10 seconds) with no sign that anything was happening, so a second click started a second tunnel and a second browser. It now shows a "Starting the tunnel…" toast on the click and ignores further clicks for the same connection until the tunnel is up or has failed. The in-flight mark is cleared on success, on failure and when the worker dies, and other connections are not affected. Quitting while the tunnel starts still leaves its `ssh -N` running (unchanged, deferred). (`rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)
- **Jump-host tunnels for RDP, VNC and SPICE listen on loopback only (security)** — the tunnel was started with `-L <port>:<host>:<port>` and no bind address, so `GatewayPorts yes` in the user's `~/.ssh/config` made the forwarded port listen on every network interface and anyone on the local network could reach the RDP/VNC/SPICE target through it. It is now `-L 127.0.0.1:<port>:<host>:<port>` for both OpenSSH and Tailscale SSH, built by the same helper as the SOCKS bind; every consumer already connects to `127.0.0.1`. Forwards the user writes themselves (per-connection port forwarding, the SSH Tunnel Manager) are unchanged on purpose and keep following `GatewayPorts`. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **"Open Browser via Tunnel" is offered only where it can work (issue #397 follow-up)** — the sidebar decided whether to show the item from a Zero Trust connection's `provider`, while the code that starts the tunnel checked its `provider_config`, so an imported or hand-edited connection whose two disagreed showed the item and then did nothing. Both now use the same core predicate, `rustconn_core::ssh_tunnel::supports_browser_tunnel`, which requires the provider and its configuration to agree; the menu reads it from the row, and the Web connection "Tunnel Through SSH" path refuses a connection that cannot host a tunnel instead of running `ssh` against it. If the action is still reached for such a connection, it shows the "Could not open the SSH tunnel" toast. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)
- **The CPU/RAM monitoring bar no longer stays washed out after Reconnect (issue #398)** — when an SSH session dropped, the monitoring collector stopped after three consecutive execution errors and dimmed the bar (the `monitoring-stale` style, 50% opacity) with a warning icon to mark its metrics as stale. Reconnect then skipped starting monitoring, because its guard treated any bar already in the session map as live, so the dimmed bar stayed in place and its metrics never updated again — including the race where Reconnect was pressed before the collector had even noticed the drop. Reconnect now explicitly discards the previous connection's bar and collector before reconnecting, and starting monitoring replaces a bar whose collector has already stopped instead of skipping it, so the bar comes back exactly as on a first connect: one bar, no stale class, no warning icon. (`rustconn/src/monitoring.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP hands a session that stalls after TLS to the external client (issue #392)** — against GNOME Remote Desktop's Remote Login mode, IronRDP completed the TLS upgrade and the TOFU certificate check, then waited the full handshake budget (60s+) inside `connect_finalize` (NLA → MCS → licensing → capabilities) with no error. That timeout was indistinguishable from an unreachable host (`Operation timed out`, a `NON_FALLBACK_MARKERS` entry), so the session closed with no fallback even though Remmina and GNOME Connections — both FreeRDP-based — connect to the same server. A timeout after the TLS upgrade has already succeeded now classifies as `ProtocolIncompatible` and hands the session to the external `xfreerdp3` client, reusing the same fallback path issue #199 added; a timeout before TLS, a refused connection and an unknown host are unaffected and still fail without a fallback. The root cause is upstream: GNOME Remote Desktop's Remote Login mode most likely expects an RDP Server Redirection to one-time RDSTLS credentials, which `ironrdp-connector` 0.10 does not implement (`cluster: None`, upstream TODO #139); full embedded support needs that upstream work. Until then, selecting **Client mode: External** for such a connection skips the ~minute wait. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/client/connection.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **F11 in a popped-out session window now gives a true, chrome-free full screen (issue #388)** — pressing F11 on a detached window (right-click an embedded tab → "Pop Out") fullscreened the window but left its header bar ("Move to Main Window" button and title) on screen, unlike the main window, which hides its header bar in full screen. The detached window now follows its own `fullscreened` property, the same mechanism the main window's fullscreen chrome uses: the header bar hides on entry and is restored exactly on exit, while the "Session disconnected" banner stays visible (issue #236). This covers every path into full screen — F11, the window manager, and detaching straight to a monitor — and the `win.toggle-fullscreen` action's checkmark state now follows the same property instead of going stale after a window-manager-driven change. (`rustconn/src/detached_window.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/detach_actions.rs`)

### Added
- **Performance instrumentation for vault and SSH operations (`rustconn::perf`, W0)** — 0.24 is a performance release, so its targets are measured rather than felt. A new `PerfSpan` helper times each vault operation (`vault.unlock`, `vault.retrieve`/`store`/`delete`) and each SSH-connect phase (`ssh.connect.resolve`, `ssh.connect.agent`, `ssh.connect.args`) and, on drop, emits one structured line to the `rustconn::perf` tracing target with `op`, `transport`, `elapsed_ms` and `calls` fields. The target is off unless you ask for it (`RUST_LOG=rustconn::perf=info`), and a line never carries a secret, a credential, a vault item name, a host name or a user name — only fixed labels. This is the instrument the release's timing targets are read from; see docs/ARCHITECTURE.md. (`rustconn-core/src/tracing/perf.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)
- **Bitwarden `bw` spawn-count and argv-safety harness (W0, dev test)** — an ignored integration test puts a fake `bw` on the backend's resolved command path and runs real `BitwardenBackend` operations against it, so the number of child processes per vault operation can be measured in CI as the W2 `bw serve` work drives it toward one. It also pins the 0.23.21 fix that the item JSON (which carries the plaintext password) reaches `bw` over stdin and never as a base64 argument visible in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`. Run it with `cargo test -p rustconn-core --test bitwarden_spawn_counter -- --ignored`. (`rustconn-core/tests/bitwarden_spawn_counter.rs`)
- **Containerized sshd testbed for live SSH integration tests (W3 K0)** — a throwaway rootless-podman OpenSSH server (`scripts/sshd-testbed.sh`) with named profiles (password, bastion, StrictModes, publickey+keyboard-interactive, ForceCommand, unknown host key), bound to a random loopback port and torn down with the container, so the SSH primitives that need a real server answering on a real socket can be exercised without touching the developer's own sshd or `~/.ssh`. A new ignored test module drives it against the pre-connect port check (`check_port`/`check_port_async`), the ControlMaster socket detection behind the P4 fast path (`control_socket_present`) and the interactive keepalive, and self-skips cleanly when no testbed env is present. No new dependency and no production code — pure shell plus `#[ignore]` tests. Run with `scripts/sshd-testbed.sh up <profile>`, `source target/sshd-testbed.env`, then `cargo test -p rustconn-core --test ssh_testbed -- --ignored`. (`scripts/sshd-testbed.sh`, `rustconn-core/tests/ssh_testbed.rs`)
- **Headless core of "Set Up Key Authentication…" (W3 K2)** — a new `rustconn_core::ssh_key_setup` module does everything the "Set Up Key Authentication…" dialog (next entry) needs without a widget: it generates a key with `ssh-keygen` (Ed25519 by default, RSA 4096 as an option, no passphrase by default) into `<ssh dir>/rustconn/` (mode 0700, `~/snap/rustconn/current/.ssh/rustconn/` in the snap) under `<connection>-<id8>_<type>` with the comment `rustconn@<host> <date>` and no connection name; installs the public key with its own idempotent script fed through stdin (creates `~/.ssh` 0700 and `authorized_keys` 0600, appends only when the key is absent, runs `restorecon`) so a hostile connection name, host or comment has nothing to inject into; and verifies the result by logging in with the new key alone (no ControlMaster, no agent, `publickey` only) and counting it a success only when the server's `-v` output names the new key's fingerprint. The control-channel `ssh` always runs with `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes`, placed ahead of — and removing the conflicting entries from — the connection's custom options, so `StrictHostKeyChecking=no` there cannot weaken it; an unknown or changed host key is reported as its own error so the dialog can offer to connect normally first. A password goes to `ssh` through the askpass helper (a file path in the environment, never argv); a key passphrase is used only to generate or verify and is never stored, and during verification it is released only for the passphrase prompt of the new key, so a bastion's password prompt in a jump chain never receives it. Clustered short flags in a connection's own `ssh` arguments (`-vA`, `-vo …`) are split before filtering, so they cannot re-enable agent forwarding or a weaker host-key setting, and a host name with shell metacharacters is refused. A repeat run reuses the pair it generated before, and a generated key can be deleted without ever touching a key the user brought. `proc::wait_bounded_cancellable` lets a Cancel button stop the child. Nothing in the UI changes yet. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_key_setup.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/proc.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/tests/ssh_testbed.rs`)
- **"Set Up Key Authentication…" in the sidebar context menu (W3 K3)** — right-click an SSH connection in the sidebar and choose "Set Up Key Authentication…" to stop typing a password for it. RustConn creates a key (Ed25519 by default, RSA 4096 as an option), installs it on the server, signs in with it to check that it works, and then switches the connection to key authentication, with an Undo button on the confirmation toast. The sidebar menu is the only entry point for now; a button in the connection editor comes later. It reaches the server exactly the way a normal session does, including jump hosts, `ProxyCommand` and the Flatpak `known_hosts`, and it hands the stored account password to `ssh` only when a normal connect would. The stored password stays in your password store, so you can switch back. If the connection has no stored password, the dialog asks for it once and does not save it. An optional passphrase protects the new key; it is used only to create and check that key and is never stored. Nothing is sent to a server whose host key RustConn has not seen: the dialog says so and offers "Connect" to open an ordinary session so the key gets recorded (when a stored password signs that session in, the key is recorded without a question, as in any normal connect), after which you run the setup again; a changed host key only shows a warning. If the server rejects a stored password, the dialog shows the password field so you can type the right one for this run. Other outcomes have their own messages: wrong or expired password, wrong passphrase, a server that does not allow password sign-in, a server with no POSIX shell, the key being installed but refused by the server (`StrictModes`, a custom `AuthorizedKeysFile`, SELinux), and network errors. If the key is installed but the check fails, it is kept so that "Try Again" reuses it, and "Delete Generated Key" removes it. A server that accepts the key but also asks for a one-time code lets you decide whether to use the key anyway. Stop cancels a run in progress and ends the `ssh` process. In the snap, the dialog warns that the key lives in the snap's own folder. This also tightens the K2 key check: behind a jump host, the bastion's own debug output no longer turns a wrong passphrase into "the server does not accept the key" or its partial success into a second-factor warning. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/key_setup.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/key_setup.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_key_setup.rs`, `po/*.po`)
- **"Use an Existing Key" in Set Up Key Authentication (W3 K5)** — the "Set Up Key Authentication…" dialog (K3) gained a "Key" row: "Create a New Key" (unchanged) or "Use an Existing Key". The existing-key list is read from `~/.ssh` and `~/.ssh/rustconn` (the same directory generated keys live in), with a "Choose file…" button that opens the portal file chooser for a key kept elsewhere; in Flatpak a portal path is copied to the stable sandbox `.ssh` directory first, as the connection editor's own key picker already does. A connection that already points at a key file has it preselected. The public half is read from a matching `.pub` file when one parses and agrees with the private key's fingerprint, otherwise derived straight from the OpenSSH private key itself — which works even for an encrypted key, since OpenSSH keeps the public key in the clear; a legacy PEM/PKCS#8 key (not parsed by this module) needs a `.pub` file next to it. Only Ed25519 and RSA are supported, matching what key setup can generate. Deploy, verify and the outcome mapping (including "Connect" for an unknown host key) are exactly K3's, run against the chosen key instead of a generated one; on success the connection switches to that key's path with an Undo toast, and the stored account password is untouched. If the chosen key is encrypted, its passphrase is asked for in the dialog and used only to verify the key signs in — never saved, never vaulted. The one rule this adds: an existing key the user brought is never offered for deletion on a failed run, unlike a key RustConn generated — `KeyOrigin` tracks where each on-disk key came from, and `delete_offered`/`deletable_key` are the only two places "Delete Generated Key" or the deletion itself may fire from. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/key_setup.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/key_setup.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_key_setup.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/keyfile.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs`, `po/*.po`)
- **"Set Up Key Authentication…" in the connection editor (W3 K4)** — the Authentication group on an SSH connection's page now has a "Set Up Key Authentication…" row, the same item and the same dialog as the sidebar menu's (K3/K5): the sidebar menu is no longer the only entry point. The row is enabled only for an already-saved SSH connection (disabled with a "Available after the connection is saved" hint otherwise, and absent for SFTP/MOSH/every other protocol, which cannot host the flow). It runs against the connection as it is currently stored, so an unsaved edit to Host is not silently swapped in. Unlike the sidebar menu, a result from the editor's row is never persisted directly: on success the dialog closes and the key goes into the editor's own Method, Key Source, Key File and "Use Only Specified Key" fields, exactly what the sidebar menu would have written, and it only takes effect when you press Save — Cancel discards it like any other unsaved edit (the key itself stays installed on the server and on disk either way). No Undo toast in this path, since the editor's own Cancel already fills that role. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/ssh.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/build.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/construction.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/key_setup.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_dialogs.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/copy_field_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `po/*.po`)
- **In-process SSH key-file inspection and fingerprinting (W3 A2)** — a new `ssh_agent::keyfile` module reads the public half of a private OpenSSH key, computes its `SHA256:` fingerprint and classifies the file (OpenSSH vs legacy PEM vs PKCS#8, encrypted vs not) without spawning `ssh-keygen` and without a passphrase — the public key sits in the clear even in an encrypted OpenSSH key, so a key can be identified before anyone is asked to unlock it. The fingerprint is computed with the same `ring` + `data-encoding` helper the agent read path uses, so a file key and an agent key compare directly (the basis for an "is this key already loaded?" check). It adds no dependency: the full A2 plan to decrypt in-process via the `ssh-key` crate is blocked by a transitive `aes-gcm` RC-pin conflict with IronRDP, so an *encrypted* key still routes to `ssh-add` for loading (`KeyFileInfo::needs_ssh_add_fallback`). (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/keyfile.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/wire.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs`)
- **Embedded web browser in the snap (issue #244)** — the snap now builds the default feature set plus `adw-1-8`, so Web connections can open in an embedded WebKitGTK 6.0 tab, using the WebKitGTK 2.52 of the gnome-core26 platform and the `webkitgtk-6.0` layout the core26 `gnome` extension adds. Inside a snap WebKit does not use its bubblewrap sandbox, so embedded web content is confined by the snap's strict confinement only and has the same access as RustConn itself (home, SSH keys, cloud credentials, keyring); docs/SNAP.md describes the trade-off. The embedded browser is the default in the snap, the same as in the Flatpak and the native packages: new Web connections, imports (mRemoteNG, CSV) and the connection dialog's Mode list default to Embedded, and *Open Browser via Tunnel* defaults to the in-app browser. **System** and **Custom** remain available in the Mode list for a connection where the missing sandbox matters, and the tunnelled-browser switch still lets you pick an external Chromium-based browser instead; docs/SNAP.md spells out when to reach for either. *Open Browser via Tunnel* with no usable Chromium opens the embedded browser instead of the system one (which cannot take the SOCKS proxy), with a toast explaining the fallback; a Web connection tunnelled through SSH needs Embedded or a Chromium Custom command, since System opens it without the tunnel. Flatpak, native and macOS behaviour is unchanged. The feature list no longer repeats `default` by hand, so a new default feature cannot silently go missing from the snap. (`snap/snapcraft.yaml`, `docs/SNAP.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **"Open Browser via Tunnel" for Tailscale SSH connections (issue #397)** — the sidebar action that raises a SOCKS proxy to a host and opens a browser through it was offered only for SSH/SFTP connections, so a Tailscale SSH user had to keep a duplicate plain-SSH connection just to browse through a tailnet host. A Zero Trust connection of the Tailscale SSH type now offers it too. The proxy is started with the same `tailscale ssh [user@]host` the session runs, with `-N -D <port>` passed through to the system `ssh`, so authentication, ACLs, MagicDNS and Tailscale's host-key check are exactly the session's; the connection's port, custom arguments and stored password are not used, as the session does not use them either. A host or user name that is not a plain name (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, `:`) is refused before anything is spawned, since `tailscale ssh` passes it to `ssh` where a leading `-` would read as an option. Embedded and external (Chromium) browsers both work, as for SSH. Other Zero Trust providers are unchanged. One shared gate, `rustconn_core::ssh_tunnel::socks_tunnel_route`, now decides which connections can host the tunnel, and the tunnel builder gained a `TunnelLauncher` instead of a second copy. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/models/protocol.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar_ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/types.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Changed
- **Listing ssh-agent identities no longer spawns a process (W1 A1)** — reading the keys an agent holds ran `ssh-add -l` and, for the public-key path, a `ssh-keygen` per key, so a connect that consulted the agent cost one process plus one per identity on the way in. RustConn now speaks the ssh-agent protocol directly over `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` (`SSH_AGENTC_REQUEST_IDENTITIES`) in one round trip and computes each key's SHA256 fingerprint in-process with `ring` — no process, under a millisecond. The old `ssh-add -l` path remains as a fallback for an agent the native client cannot talk to, so behaviour is never worse than before. Adding and removing keys still go through `ssh-add` for now. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/wire.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs`)
- **One `ssh-add` path for the whole app (W1 A3)** — loading a connection's SSH key into the agent before an mc/file-manager launch was five near-identical `ssh-add` spawns (four in the GUI, one in core), each re-deriving the same stdin/stderr wiring and Flatpak `SSH_ASKPASS` strip, so a fix to one could miss the others. They now call a single `rustconn_core::sftp::ensure_identity_loaded`, which returns the agent's stderr on failure so each caller keeps only its own result handling (a toast, a sidebar status, a log line). No behaviour change: a missing or absent key still proceeds without blocking the launch. (`rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)
- **Secret global variables are read from the vault once per session window, not once per connect phase (W1 P1)** — a connection that substitutes a secret global variable re-read it from the vault every time, and a single SSH connect reads the globals two to three times (the ASK scan, the launch substitution, the reconnect path), each a blocking vault lookup. Resolved secret globals are now held in a 300-second session cache (the same window as the per-connection credential cache), so the repeated reads of one connect and a quick reconnect cost nothing; a vault miss is not cached, and saving the variables clears the cache so an edited secret is never served stale. (`rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/history_actions.rs`)
- **`ssh -G` no longer spawns on the GTK main thread during SSH connect (W1 P3)** — deciding whether OpenSSH's own config routes a host through a proxy (which gates whether the target password may be delivered by askpass) ran `ssh -G` synchronously inside the connect-args build on the UI thread. On the initial connect it now runs in the same background phase as the port check and the answer is handed to the build, so the main thread spawns no process. The reconnect path and a connect with the port check disabled still compute it synchronously as a fallback. No behaviour change: a config that routes through a proxy still blocks target-password askpass, keeping the credential off the bastion. (`rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)
- **FreeRDP version detection is warmed in the background at startup (W4)** — the first RDP connect of a session probed each candidate FreeRDP client (`… --version`) synchronously on the GTK main thread to pick and configure the right binary — up to seven short spawns the user waited through while the window was already up (one connect in the field showed five). A fire-and-forget background thread at startup now fills the same process-wide version cache the connect path reads (by the same keys, local `PATH` and the Flatpak host's `host:` form), so by the time a connection opens the lookups are cache hits and the main thread spawns nothing. It changes no detection logic and caches nothing a probe would not: a client missing, slow or without a parseable banner at warm-up time is simply re-probed on first use, so behaviour is never worse than before. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/detect.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **Loading an SSH key into the agent skips `ssh-add` when the key is already there (W3 A3)** — the single `ensure_identity_loaded` path added in W1 always spawned `ssh-add`, even before an mc or SFTP launch on a key already loaded at connect time, where the add is a no-op that still costs a process. It now reads the key's public fingerprint without a passphrase (A2, the public half of an OpenSSH key is in the clear even when encrypted; a sibling `.pub` is the fallback) and compares it against the agent's identities over the native wire protocol (A1, one Unix-socket round trip, no process). An already-loaded key returns immediately with zero spawns. The agent query is bounded by a 3-second timeout so a broken or launchd-throttled agent cannot hang a main-thread caller, and any uncertainty (no fingerprint, no agent, a timeout) falls through to the existing `ssh-add` path — so behaviour is never worse than before and an encrypted key not yet loaded still goes through `ssh-add`. (`rustconn-core/src/sftp.rs`)
- **A live ControlMaster socket skips the pre-connect port check (W1 P4)** — when a master connection to the host is already open, a new SSH session multiplexes over it, so the TCP connection is already up and the pre-connect port check is redundant. RustConn now detects an existing ControlMaster socket for the host and port (a real Unix socket sharing the `ControlPath` prefix, not a stale regular file) and skips the port check, saving a round trip. It does not run `ssh -O check` — that would spawn the process the fast path exists to avoid — and a socket that is present but dead is simply re-established by OpenSSH's `ControlMaster=auto`, so the only cost of a rare false positive is a skipped port check that proxy/bypass connections already skip safely. (`rustconn-core/src/monitoring/ssh_exec.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)
- **Multi-bastion hop-password resolution extracted into one tested function (W1 P2a)** — the per-hop password logic for a jump-host chain (issue #203) was inline in the connect-args build, interleaved with the chain walk. It now lives in `assemble_hop_passwords`, which keeps the first reference hop's already-resolved password in place, resolves each deeper hop through an injected resolver (cache then vault, warming the cache), and keeps the result index-aligned with the jump-host list so a string-proxy gap or an unresolvable hop never shifts the others. The vault read is still synchronous — this is the extraction and dedup step; moving it off the main thread is a separate change. (`rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)

- **Bitwarden unlock no longer blocks on `bw sync` (W1)** — unlocking the vault ran `bw sync` synchronously before handing back a ready backend, and on a snap/Flatpak `bw` that one call cost around six seconds on the critical path to a usable vault at startup (issue #353 trace: `bw status` + `bw unlock` + `bw sync` back to back, ~15 s total). The sync is now deferred rather than removed: the unlock path returns immediately, the startup thread runs the sync in the background after signalling the UI that the vault is unlocked, and the first credential read or write after an unlock carries a one-shot `sync_due()` gate so the local copy is still refreshed before it is used (freshness for items edited in another client, issue #351, is preserved). A sync that ran within the last ten minutes is not repeated, so reconnects and batch lookups keep skipping the round trip. The background and first-retrieve syncs are serialized process-wide, so a connect made the instant the background sync starts waits for it rather than firing a second `bw sync` beside it. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **SSH askpass helper moved from the GUI into `rustconn-core` (W3 K1)** — the prompt-aware `SSH_ASKPASS` helper that releases a stored target or bastion password only for an account-password prompt (and stays silent for key passphrases, host-key questions, OTP and password-change prompts), its per-hop script cache, the owner-only secret files and the per-child askpass environment lived in `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`, out of reach of the core consumers that need them next (key setup, the CLI, monitoring). They now live in a new `rustconn_core::ssh_askpass` module that the GUI calls, and the SSH tunnel's secret file is written by the same function, so one place decides where an askpass secret may live (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `$TMPDIR` on macOS, an O_EXCL 0600 random name in the temp dir otherwise). No behaviour change: the helper script is byte-for-byte the same (pinned by a golden test), as are the env var names, file names, modes and the cleanup on ssh exit. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_askpass.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)

### Removed
- **Dead embedded-RDP file-transfer progress/complete callbacks (W4, issue #390)** — `connect_file_progress` and `connect_file_complete`, their backing `on_file_progress`/`on_file_complete` fields and the whole chain that carried them (the `FileTransferContext` fields, the clones through the connect setup and the Save-Files button, and the three call sites that invoked them) had no caller: nothing ever connected a callback, so every invocation saw `None` and the "Downloaded {}/{} files" / completion branches were dead. The user-facing progress and summary come from the status label and the button, which are untouched; this only removes machinery that never ran. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/polling_handlers.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/thread.rs`)
- **Dead `RdpBackendSelector` backend-selection module (W4, FC §6c)** — `rdp_client/backend.rs` (the `RdpBackend` enum, `BackendDetectionResult` and `RdpBackendSelector` with its detection caching) was a relic of an older backend-selection design and had no production caller: the live FreeRDP-client choice goes through `embedded_rdp::detect` and `protocol::select_freerdp_client`. Removed the module, its re-exports and its property tests (~380 lines with tests). No behaviour change — nothing outside the module and its own tests referenced it. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/backend.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/tests/properties/rdp_backend_tests.rs`)

### Improved
- **"Open Browser via Tunnel" no longer freezes the window while the SSH tunnel comes up (W4)** — the sidebar action started the SOCKS tunnel and waited for its port on the GTK thread, up to 10 seconds with a dead or slow host, while the Web connection `tunnel_via` path already did the same off-thread. The action now raises the tunnel through the same background path, so the window stays responsive; the success and failure toasts, the choice between the embedded and the external browser, and who owns the tunnel afterwards (the tab for the embedded browser, the external-session registry for Chromium) are unchanged. (`rustconn/src/window/edit_actions.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **Snap base core24 → core26, GNOME platform `gnome-46-2404` → `gnome-core26` (issue #174)** — Snapcraft 9.2 added core26 support to the `gnome` extension, which was the blocker that reverted the 0.23.2 attempt. The snap now builds on Ubuntu 26.04 against the `gnome-core26` platform (GTK 4.24, libadwaita 1.10) with GPU drivers from `mesa-2604`, and is compiled with `adw-1-8` like the Flatpak, so it gets the full libadwaita 1.8 widget tier (`AdwSpinner`, `AdwToggleGroup`, `AdwShortcutsDialog`) instead of the 1.5 fallbacks. Existing snap users download the new `gnome-core26` and `mesa-2604` platform snaps on the first update. Because core26 ships no Python, the pip-installed Azure and Oracle Zero Trust CLIs in the Components dialog run on the Python 3.14 of the gnome-core26 platform (the extension's library cleanup empties a separately staged Python), and because the extension no longer adds the `gsettings` plug the snap declares it explicitly. The bundled FreeRDP client stays `freerdp3-x11`; the embedded WebKit browser is enabled separately (see Added, issue #244). Building the snap needs Snapcraft 9.2 or newer. (`snap/snapcraft.yaml`, `.github/workflows/snap.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `scripts/check-snap-runtime-libs.sh`)
- **crypto-bigint 0.7.6 (yanked) → 0.7.10** — a `cargo update` cleared a yanked-crate warning (`cargo deny check advisories` flagged crypto-bigint 0.7.6, pulled transitively through the IronRDP → picky → rsa-rc → crypto-primes chain); advisories are otherwise clean before and after.
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): ctutils 0.4.5 → 0.4.6, der 0.8.2 → 0.8.3, elliptic-curve 0.14.1 → 0.14.3, ironrdp-error 0.2.0 → 0.2.1, lzma-rust2 0.22.0 → 0.23.0, notify 8.2.0 → 8.2.1, notify-types 2.1.0 → 2.2.0, openssl-probe 0.2.1 → 0.2.2, picky-asn1 0.10.1 → 0.10.2, picky-asn1-x509 0.15.5 → 0.15.6, roxmltree 0.21.1 → 0.21.2, rustix 1.1.5 → 1.1.6, typed-path 0.12.4 → 0.13.0, xattr 1.6.1 → 1.7.0, zip 9.0.5 → 9.1.3. The update also pruned ten stale `windows-*` lock entries; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. The Flatpak stack was already current: GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.2 (latest upstream), cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.7.0, VTE 0.84.1; every downloadable CLI tool resolves current (kubectl 1.37.2, Tailscale 1.104.4, Teleport 18.10.0).

## [0.23.21] - 2026-10-23

### Fixed
- **Embedded RDP "Save N Files" no longer times out without saving after the folder dialog closes (issue #390)** — when the folder picker was a portal window (Flatpak, Snap), returning focus to RustConn made GTK report a clipboard change, and the local-clipboard monitor announced text to the server. That announcement replaces the server's clipboard, so the files being fetched were no longer offered: the request went unanswered, IronRDP gave up after 60 s and the button went back to "Save N Files" with nothing written. The monitor now holds local announcements from the moment Save is pressed until the batch settles, and a change seen meanwhile is announced once afterwards; a cancelled or failed folder dialog is now logged and releases the hold. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/thread.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/polling_handlers.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **Bitwarden: item contents are passed to bw via stdin instead of command-line arguments** — saving a credential ran `bw create item <base64>` / `bw edit item <id> <base64>`, and that base64 item holds the plaintext password, so any local user could read it from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` while `bw` ran. `BitwardenBackend::store` now writes the encoded item to `bw`'s stdin (the CLI reads it there when the argument is absent), on the create, edit and "out of date" retry paths alike; the buffer is still wiped on drop. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)
- **No more UI freeze after the startup Bitwarden unlock and after every Settings save (issue #353)** — `AppState::refresh_secret_backend_cache` ran two availability probes (up to 5 s each) with `block_on` on the GTK main thread, about 7 s with `bw` and a stall for every CLI backend, to fill a cache nothing read. The dead cache and its four call sites are removed; the startup banner and the sidebar status already come from `detect_secret_backends` on a background thread. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)
- **Bitwarden "Search from vault root" makes one `bw` call fewer per lookup (issue #353)** — with root search on, the scoped `RustConn: <id>` search ran first although the search by the connection id that follows always returns a superset of it, so a lookup cost four `bw list items` calls (4–4.5 s each in the reporter's log). The scoped search is now skipped, giving three calls with the same match rules: RustConn's own item still wins over a bare title match. `BitwardenBackend::is_available` also trusts a held session instead of running `bw --version` and `bw status`. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)
- **Settings → Secrets no longer unlocks the Bitwarden vault again when a session exists (issue #353)** — `bw status` on CLI 2026.4+ always reports `locked`, so opening Settings or switching the backend to Bitwarden ran a new `bw unlock` every time, outside the unlock lock, which produced the parallel unlocks in the #353 logs. `check_bitwarden_status_sync` now reports Unlocked at once when RustConn holds a session, and the blocking and async unlock paths share one `BW_UNLOCK` lock, so a manual Unlock waits for a startup or connect unlock instead of racing it. A held session that was locked outside RustConn (`bw lock` in a terminal) is no longer replaced silently on the next Settings open; press Unlock there or restart RustConn. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/secrets_tab/detection.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)
- **Repeated credential fetches are served from the session cache (issue #353)** — Copy Username, Copy Password and the editor's **Load password from vault** asked the vault every time, and only a Connect filled the cache, so fetching the same credentials twice in a row ran the full vault lookup (several seconds with `bw`) twice. Every vault read through `resolve_credentials_gtk` now caches the answer; the editor's Load uses the cache only while the editor's lookup fields match the saved Vault connection. A password-only answer is not cached for RDP and Web, which need a user name, and a failed connection still drops the entry (issue #351). Editing or deleting a connection drops its entry too, so a password changed in the editor is not replayed from the cache, and expired entries are removed from memory instead of only being skipped. (`rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/connections.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/copy_field_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/passwords.rs`)
- **Serial Auto-Detect finds macOS ports and offers USB adapters first** — the scan only knew Linux node names, so on macOS it found nothing. It now offers `/dev/cu.*` call-out devices (not the `tty.*` dial-in nodes, which block on carrier detect) and ranks the results `ttyUSB`, `ttyACM`, `cu.usb*`, other `cu.*`, then the built-in `ttyS` UARTs, numbered nodes in numeric order (`ttyUSB2` before `ttyUSB10`). Before, the list was sorted alphabetically, so the built-in `ttyS*` ports came ahead of a USB adapter and `ttyUSB10` ahead of `ttyUSB2`. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/serial.rs`)
- **Remmina import keeps leading and trailing spaces in passwords** — the password read back from the keyring with `secret-tool lookup` was trimmed, so a password that starts or ends with a space was imported wrong. Only the one trailing newline `secret-tool` adds is removed now, and the value is held as `SecretString` with its buffers wiped. Each lookup also has a 30 s limit, so a keyring unlock prompt left open no longer holds the import indefinitely; past it the connection is imported without its password. (`rustconn-core/src/import/remmina.rs`)
- **Group Sync no longer spreads a local SSH key file path** — `strip_ssh_key_path` cleared `key_path` but left an `SshKeySource::File` key source in the exported group, so another device received a path to a key file that only exists on the exporting machine. A `File` key source is exported as `Default` (let ssh choose), for SSH and SFTP alike; `Agent` and `Inherit` are kept. (`rustconn-core/src/sync/group_export.rs`)
- **The SSH key passphrase is no longer passed to `ssh-add` in the environment** — `SshAgentManager::add_key` gave the passphrase to its askpass helper in `RUSTCONN_ASKPASS_SECRET`, readable through `/proc/<pid>/environ` while `ssh-add` ran. It now uses the owner-only (0600) secret file in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` the SSH tunnels already use; the environment carries only the file's path, and the file is zeroized and removed when `ssh-add` exits. macOS has no runtime dir, so there the file sits in the per-user `$TMPDIR`, as the tunnel's already does. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`)
- **A wrong SSH key passphrase no longer freezes RustConn** — after a wrong passphrase `ssh-add` asks again until its askpass helper gives an empty answer, and the helper repeated the same passphrase forever, so adding a key from Settings → SSH Agent hung the window. The helper now answers once and empties its file after the read, so `ssh-add` stops and the dialog reports an incorrect passphrase; `ssh-add` also runs under a 30 s limit after which it is killed and the passphrase file is still removed. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_agent/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`)

### Changed
- **`bw sync` runs at most every 10 minutes while a Bitwarden session is held (issue #353)** — each time the 120 s session check expired, `auto_unlock` also ran `bw sync`, a few seconds per connect. A separate 10-minute sync TTL now gates that step. A fresh unlock still syncs every time, a successful sync from any path (including the "out of date" retry in `store`) resets the timer, and `sync_on_next_unlock` after a rejected password still forces a sync (issue #351). The 10 minutes are wall-clock time, so a suspend counts towards them. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **ctutils 0.4.3 → 0.4.5 (RUSTSEC-2026-0385)** — fixes non-constant-time mask generation and `u128` comparisons in `ctutils::Choice`, which could branch on a secret value on common platforms and leak it through a covert timing channel; pulled in transitively under the IronRDP/`sspi`/`rsa`/`picky` smartcard and crypto chain. `cargo deny check advisories` is clean again.
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): cc 1.9.0 → 1.10.0, crypto-bigint 0.7.5 → 0.7.6, smallvec 1.16.3 → 1.16.4, uuid 1.28.0 → 1.29.0; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests.
- **Flatpak — OpenH264 2.6.0 → 2.7.0** — the bundled embedded-RDP H.264 decoder/encoder picks up a batch of hardening fixes (several out-of-bounds, use-after-free and integer-overflow guards in the decoder's threaded and rate-control paths); version and `sha256` updated in both the local and Flathub manifests. GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.2, cJSON 1.7.19 and the bundled VTE 0.84.1 were already current; every downloadable CLI tool resolves current (TigerVNC pinned 1.16.2). The Snap stays on `core24` + `gnome-46-2404`: Snapcraft 9.2 just added core26 support to the `gnome` extension, but moving the base is a multi-step migration (libadwaita 1.8 features, re-checked staged package names, a full strict-confinement smoke test) that needs its own change rather than a version bump here — tracked as a proposal for 0.24 (issue #174).

## [0.23.20] - 2026-10-23

### Added
- **Session scripting with `.rsc` scripts (PR #387, contributed by Naim Shafiev)** — Ásbrú-CM / SecureCRT-style automation of a live terminal: send keys, wait for and capture output, ask the user (`ask … hide` for masked input), run a local command, and open, split and close sessions, with variables, labels and `goto`/`if_goto` branching. **Tools → Scripts…** edits, validates, runs and stops scripts from the library at `<config_dir>/scripts/` and opens the language reference; bundled examples cover shell and OpenWrt uptime, a local command and a first-run wizard. Connections and groups can auto-run scripts when the session starts, inherited down the group chain. The parser and event-driven engine live in `rustconn-core`; no new dependency was added. (`rustconn-core/src/automation/script.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/script/manager.rs`, `rustconn/src/script_runner.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/script.rs`)
- **Scripts are safe against the remote host and other local users** — a variable substituted into `run` must pass the connection-task check (no shell metacharacters, newlines or control characters), so text captured from a session cannot become a local command, and an `ask … hide` answer is never put on a command line. `run` gets no stdin, runs without session-token and cloud-credential variables, and is killed at its timeout (60 s when none is set). Deleting a script asks first; script ids are validated on every read and delete; a script missing from the local library is kept on save rather than replaced; engine `Debug` shows variable names only and values are zeroized.
- **The vault's user name and domain are used with its password, for every backend and protocol** — a non-empty vault value replaces the connection's for that session, an empty one keeps it, `DOMAIN\user` is split, and an e-mail-style login is kept whole. **Load credentials from vault** in the editor fills password, user name and domain. Before, a Bitwarden user name reached RDP but not SSH, Telnet or Serial. KeePassXC does not supply them yet. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/vault_identity.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)

### Fixed
- **Bitwarden "Search from vault root" finds entries by URL and full host name (issue #353)** — the search returned items whose title was not exactly the connection's name or host, and they were dropped. An exact title still wins; otherwise a login URL host or an FQDN/short-name match is used when exactly one item matches, and ambiguity resolves to none with a warning. A URL-shaped host also searches by its host, RustConn's own item is accepted when a save was still in flight, and the debug log lists what each search returned. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)
- **Bitwarden item notes no longer become the RDP domain** — the domain now comes from a custom `domain` field, or from the notes of RustConn's own `RustConn: …` item only; an implausible value is ignored. Saving to Bitwarden also no longer leaves the password in unwiped buffers. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **tokio 1.54.0 → 1.54.1 (RUSTSEC-2026-0384)** — fixes a data race and use-after-free in `tokio::time::Interval` on the multi-threaded runtime; `cargo deny check advisories` is clean.
- **Updated** (semver-compatible): icu_collections, icu_locale_core, icu_normalizer, icu_normalizer_data, icu_properties, icu_properties_data 2.3.0 → 2.4.0, icu_provider 2.3.1 → 2.4.0, memmap2 0.9.11 → 0.9.12, open 5.4.4 → 5.4.5, system-deps 9.0.0 → 9.0.1, tinystr 0.8.4 → 0.8.5, yoke 0.8.3 → 0.8.4, zerovec 0.11.8 → 0.11.9, zerovec-derive 0.11.6 → 0.11.7; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.2, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0 and every downloadable CLI tool were already current; the Snap stays on `core24` + `gnome-46-2404` (issue #174).

## [0.23.19] - 2026-10-21

### Added
- **About → Credits now names the people who contributed** — the About dialog lists code contributors alongside the maintainer (Felipe Schneider, Naim Shafiev, dwetscher, Henrik, cocide, Austin Eschweiler) in Developers, and names every translator (Albano Battistella — Italian, Ekaterine Papava — Georgian, GaaChun — Simplified Chinese, Jürgen Hörmann — German, Mykola Zubkov — Ukrainian, p-bo — Czech) in the Translated-by section. The translator list is a fixed, language-independent set rather than the per-locale `translator-credits` string, so every translator is shown at once and the running language's translator is no longer both listed twice and the only one visible; its heading is one of GTK's own strings and stays localised in every language with nothing added to our catalogues. Every name is drawn from the git history, a merged or adopted pull request, or a translation catalogue's `Last-Translator` header — no name is invented, and catalogues credited only to "RustConn Team" name no individual. Where a public profile or e-mail exists the entry links to it. (`rustconn/src/app.rs`)

### Fixed
- **Sidebar sort modes now re-apply when switched — "Sort alphabetically" after "Sort by recent" no longer did nothing** — `ConnectionManager::sort_all` short-circuits on an `is_sorted` optimisation flag, but `sort_by_recent` and `sort_group` reordered connections without clearing that flag. After sorting by recency (or a group), the flag stayed `true`, so the next `sort_all` returned early and the sidebar kept its previous order; alternating A–Z and by-recent appeared to do nothing. Both `sort_by_recent` and `sort_group` now reset `is_sorted = false` after reordering, so every mode switch re-sorts while a repeated same-mode click stays a cheap no-op. A regression test (`alternating_sort_modes_each_reapply_their_order`) drives A–Z → recent → A–Z and fails against the old code. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`)
- **The same `is_sorted` invalidation gap was closed in every other reorder/reparent path** — an audit of the sidebar and main menus found four more mutation paths that rewrote `sort_order`/`parent_id`/`group_id` but left the `is_sorted` guard set, so "Sort alphabetically" after a drag-drop reorder or a move-to-folder also silently did nothing: `reorder_connection`, `reorder_group`, `move_connection_to_group` and `move_group`. All four now clear the flag through a shared private `mark_unsorted()` helper, placed next to `sort_all`, so the "mutate ⇒ invalidate" convention has one named home instead of a scattered inline assignment. Three deterministic regression tests (`reorder_then_sort_all_reapplies_alphabetical`, `reorder_group_then_sort_all_reapplies`, `move_to_group_then_sort_all_reapplies`) reorder/reparent into a non-alphabetical order and assert the next `sort_all` re-alphabetises; each fails against the old code. Separately, the search-cache invalidation that only `reload_sidebar` performed is now centralised at the single rebuild choke point `rebuild_sidebar_sorted`, so the direct-rebuild paths (`sort_connections`, `sort_recent`, `handle_drag_drop`) no longer leave a stale search filter after a re-sort. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sorting.rs`)
- **Homebrew: `brew info --json=v2 rustconn` no longer prints post-install command lines before the JSON, which broke JSON consumers (issue #386)** — v0.23.16 renamed the formula's `def post_install` to `def post_install_steps` to silence Homebrew's new `FormulaAudit/InstallSteps` cop, but `post_install_steps` is a *declarative* DSL registrar, not a hook for arbitrary Ruby. Defining a method of that name shadowed the registrar, and Homebrew invokes it while serialising the formula to a hash (`Formula#to_hash`) — exactly what `brew info --json=v2` does. The three `system` calls (LaunchServices `lsregister`, `glib-compile-schemas`, `gtk4-update-icon-cache`) then ran during a read-only metadata query and wrote their `==>` command lines to stdout before the JSON document, so `brew info … --json=v2` for the formula and for `--installed` enumeration produced invalid JSON and tools like Brew Browser failed with `json_parse`. The declarative form cannot express an arbitrary command like `lsregister` (its runner only knows a fixed set of named actions), so the formula reverts to the `post_install` hook, which Homebrew runs only in the real install phase inside a sandboxed, logged child process and never during `brew info`. Registration at `brew install` is therefore unchanged. `post_install` is `odeprecated` in favour of the declarative form it cannot use, so `FormulaAudit/InstallSteps` is waived in the CI `brew style` step alongside the existing `FormulaAudit/Text` waiver; the deprecation only becomes a hard error under `HOMEBREW_DEVELOPER=1`, which neither a normal `brew install` nor the informational build step sets. (`packaging/macos/rustconn.rb`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: ppmd-rust 1.5.1 → 1.5.2 (transitive, under the archive import/export path). Semver-compatible patch bump; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. No security advisories affected. Every direct dependency and the bundled Flatpak stack were already current: GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.2, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0 (each confirmed latest upstream), and all downloadable CLI tools resolve current (TigerVNC pinned 1.16.2, the rest auto-latest). The Snap stays on `core24` + `gnome-46-2404` (no `core26` GNOME extension yet, issue #174).

## [0.23.18] - 2026-10-21

### Improved
- **`rustconn-cli secret get/set/delete` build one Tokio runtime per command, not one per backend branch** — each backend arm created its own `tokio::runtime::Runtime::new()` before its single `block_on`, so a command spun up (and tore down) a fresh multi-thread runtime for one call. The three commands now share one lazily-created current-thread runtime through a `secret_runtime()` helper (22 call sites), which is all a one-shot CLI needs. Behaviour is unchanged. (`rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs`)
- **Tag filtering no longer allocates a `String` per tag** — `ConnectionManager::filter_by_tag` and `filter_by_tags` lower-cased both the query and every connection's every tag into fresh `String`s on each call (`filter_by_tags` even rebuilt a `Vec<String>` of lower-cased tags per connection). They now compare through an allocation-free, Unicode-aware `eq_ignore_case` helper that folds case lazily, so filtering the sidebar allocates nothing per tag. Matching stays case-insensitive with the same Unicode semantics as the old `to_lowercase` (so `Ä` still matches `ä`), unlike an ASCII-only comparison. (`rustconn-core/src/connection/manager.rs`)
- **CSV export no longer duplicates its field mapper and resolves group paths in linear time** — the bulk `export_to_string` and the single-connection `export_connection` carried two near-identical `match field { … }` blocks that could drift apart, and the bulk path re-scanned the whole group slice for every connection to build each group path (O(connections × groups)). The per-field mapping is now one shared `field_value` helper, and the group lookup goes through a `HashMap` built once per export so each hierarchy hop is O(1). Output is byte-for-byte unchanged; a regression is guarded by the existing round-trip tests. (`rustconn-core/src/export/csv_export.rs`)
- **M-PUBLIC-DEBUG guard tests for five more password-bearing types** — `SshTunnelParams`, `VncClientConfig`, `VerifiedCredentials`, `KdbxEntry` and `CredentialUpdate` each derive `Debug` while holding a `pub password: Option<SecretString>` but had no test proving the derived output does not leak the plaintext. `secrecy` redacts itself today, so there is no leak now, but a future field or a switch to a manual `Debug` could silently start leaking; these tests make that a compile-and-test guarantee, matching the guards `RdpClientConfig`/`SpiceClientConfig` and the secret backends already carry. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/kdbx.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/verification.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/manager.rs`, `rustconn-core/tests/properties/vnc_client_tests.rs`)

### Documentation
- **CLI reference: `delete` in non-interactive mode now documents the real behaviour** — the reference said that with piped stdin "confirmation is automatically assumed", implying the connection is deleted. The code does the opposite: `connection delete` without `--force` and with no terminal aborts and deletes nothing, the safe default scripts rely on. A script trusting the old wording would silently fail to delete. The line now states that it aborts without `--force` and that `--force` is required to delete unattended. (`docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`)

### Fixed
- **Ásbrú export never writes a dead entry for an unsupported protocol, whichever entry point is used** — the `ExportTarget` wrapper skips SPICE, Serial, Kubernetes, Mosh and Web (no round-trippable Ásbrú `method:`), but the raw `AsbruExporter::export` builder and the public `connection_to_entry` sat below that guard and would still emit a `method: "SPICE"` entry that neither Ásbrú-CM nor RustConn's own importer can read back. The raw builder now skips the same unsupported protocols itself, so a direct call (or a future caller) cannot bypass the guard. The protocol list is now a single `protocol_round_trips` source of truth that `supports_protocol` delegates to, and the stale comment claiming those protocols "don't have additional fields" is corrected. (`rustconn-core/src/export/asbru.rs`)
- **SSH jump-host passwords no longer travel in the process environment** — three single-hop SSH paths (the embedded RDP/VNC/SPICE/Telnet jump-host tunnel in `ssh_tunnel`, the standalone Tunnel Manager, and the background monitoring SSH client) gave the password to their `SSH_ASKPASS` helper through an environment variable (`_RC_TUN_PW`/`_RC_MON_PW`). A process environment is readable by the same user through `/proc/<pid>/environ` for the life of the process, a weaker channel than the file-path indirection the multi-hop path (issue #203) already uses. All three now write the password to an owner-only (mode 0600) file in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and pass only that file's *path* in the environment; the helper script reads the file, and the password value never enters the SSH process environment. The files are zeroized and removed when the tunnel or monitoring session ends. The Tunnel Manager's helper also moves off world-readable `/tmp` onto the same runtime-dir resolver, and the three duplicate askpass implementations collapse into one shared pair of helpers in `ssh_tunnel`. (`rustconn-core/src/ssh_tunnel.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/tunnel_manager.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/monitoring/ssh_exec.rs`)
- **Custom/extra RDP, VNC, Telnet, Serial and Zero Trust arguments now survive quotes and spaces** — the connection editor and the template dialog split every custom-args field with a bare `split_whitespace()`, so a quoted argument or a value containing a space (a FreeRDP drive mapping such as `/drive:docs,"/home/u/My Docs"`, or any flag whose value has a space) was silently torn into separate argv tokens and reached the client malformed. All six sites now route through the shared, quote-aware `rustconn_core::dialog_utils::parse_args` (built on `shell_words::split`, already used elsewhere), which keeps a quoted value as one argument and falls back to a plain whitespace split only on an unbalanced quote. Removing the five duplicate parsers also leaves one place to change this behaviour. (`rustconn-core/src/dialog_utils.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/builders.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/template.rs`)
- **Ásbrú import now holds passwords as `SecretString`, not plain `String`** — the `AsbruEntry.pass`/`password` fields and the public `AsbruImporter::extract_password` returned a bare `String`, so a password read from an Ásbrú YAML file lingered in a plain heap allocation that was neither redacted in `Debug` nor zeroised on drop, diverging from the RDM importer (`rdm.rs`), which already wraps the same field in `SecretString` per `secrets-guide.md` rule #6. Both fields and the extractor return value are now `Option<secrecy::SecretString>`; `secrecy` deserialises straight from the YAML, so the plaintext never materialises as a long-lived `String`. Password import stays off by default and still only marks the connection `PasswordSource::Prompt`. (`rustconn-core/src/import/asbru.rs`)

### Added
