- **RDP Color Depth can now be set to "Negotiate (server default)", which stops RustConn from sending `/bpp:` to FreeRDP (issue #381)** — the colour-depth dropdown in the connection editor and the template dialog always resolved to an explicit value (index 0 was 32-bit), so every GUI-configured RDP connection stored `color_depth = Some(..)` and the external/embedded FreeRDP launcher always emitted `/bpp:`. Some servers answer an explicit `/bpp:` by tearing the session down — KRdp replies to `/bpp:32` with `PDU_TYPE_DEACTIVATE_ALL` (already worked around internally for #366), and the hosts in #381 connect from `wlfreerdp3`/`sdl-freerdp3` only when `/bpp` is omitted. A new first dropdown entry, **Negotiate (server default)**, stores `color_depth = None`, so no `/bpp:` reaches FreeRDP and it negotiates the depth with the server — matching what a bare `xfreerdp /v:… ` does. The save and load mappings in both dialogs shift accordingly (None→index 0, 32→1, 24→2, 16→3, 15→4, 8→5), and because a new connection's model default is already `None`, new connections now default to Negotiate. The native IronRDP path is unaffected (it still drives its own 32-bit framebuffer for AWS EC2 compatibility). (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/rdp.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/builders.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/populate.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/template.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: ksni 0.3.6 → 0.3.7 (system-tray backend; pulls in arc-swap 1.9.3 and crossbeam-queue 0.3.14 transitively). Semver-compatible; `cargo-sources.json` regenerated for the Flatpak/Flathub manifests. No security advisories affected.
- **Documented five accepted *unmaintained* transitive advisories in `deny.toml`** — `cargo audit` reports `unsafe-libyaml` (via `serde_yaml_ng`), `zbus_names`/`zvariant`/`zvariant_derive` (via the `zbus` 5.19 desktop-integration stack — ashpd/ksni/oo7) and `atomic-polyfill` (deep under the ironrdp→sspi smartcard chain through `heapless` 0.7) as unmaintained. None is a vulnerability and each is pinned by an upstream RustConn does not control, so the fix is an upstream release in every case. `cargo-deny` already passes (`unmaintained = "workspace"` fails only on workspace-direct crates, so none is in `ignore`); the provenance and upgrade path of each are now recorded in `deny.toml` so a future audit recognises them as tracked rather than new. (`deny.toml`)

## [0.23.17] - 2026-10-20

### Fixed
- **"Search from vault root" now works for Bitwarden, 1Password, Passbolt and pass, not only KeePass (issue #353)** — with root search on, the whole-vault fallback is meant to match an entry titled by the connection's name or host. On these four backends it never did: the connect path, and the connection editor's **Load password from vault** and **Test credential resolution** buttons, all read through a plain `retrieve(key)`, which hands the backend no name or host. The fallback therefore searched for one title only, the internal `rustconn/{name}` key, which no user writes. The reporter's log shows it: `bw list items --search rustconn/hanfile03` came back empty and the lookup ended. `dispatch_vault_op` now has a `VaultOp::RetrieveFor { name, host }` that calls `retrieve_identity`, and the connect path and both editor buttons use it. A backend with root search off ignores the aliases, so its reads are unchanged. The editor's load button also stops building each backend itself and goes through `dispatch_vault_op`, as the test button already did. Its Bitwarden unlock now runs under the shared vault lock, and its retrieve gets the per-backend budget the rest of the app uses (45 s for the CLI-backed vaults, 10 s for local ones) instead of a flat 10 s. (`rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/passwords.rs`)
- **Root search no longer lets a write touch an entry RustConn did not create (issue #353)** — on Bitwarden, 1Password and Passbolt, `store` and `delete` located the existing entry through the same lookup reads use, which with root search on also matches a bare title anywhere in the vault. So a save could overwrite and rename an entry the user kept outside RustConn, and a delete could remove it (Passbolt permanently). Writes now look only for RustConn's own `RustConn: {key}` entry; root search widens reads only, as its setting says. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/onepassword.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/passbolt.rs`)
- **KeePass: the connection editor's Load password from vault and Test credential resolution buttons now search from root as a connect does (issue #353)** — the reporter's KeePass connection resolved on connect through root search, but both buttons said the password was missing. They read only the `RustConn/` scoped entry and never checked `kdbx_root_search`. A new `KeePassStatus::get_password_from_kdbx_scoped_then_root` does the scoped read, then the root search with the same candidates the connect path uses (lookup key, host, name); a database that cannot be read is reported, not treated as a miss. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/status.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/passwords.rs`)
- **Group passwords: the group dialogs' Load password from vault button and an inherited connect now search from root too (issue #353)** — the New Group dialog built each backend itself with a plain `retrieve` on `group:{name}`, a key nothing writes. The Edit Group dialog, the GUI's non-KeePass Inherit path and the core `CredentialResolver` Inherit path passed the group's UUID with no name. With root search on, none of them could match an entry titled by the group's name. The KeePass Inherit connect path already could. All of them now pass the group name. The New Group dialog goes through `dispatch_vault_op`. KeePass reads in both dialogs use `get_password_from_kdbx_scoped_then_root`. The two timeout constants only the removed inline dispatch blocks used are gone. (`rustconn/src/window/connection_dialogs.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/edit_group.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/resolver.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`)
- **`rustconn-cli secret get/set/delete` now use the vault keys the GUI uses (issue #353)** — for every backend except KeePass, the CLI read and wrote `{name} ({protocol})`, and the connection UUID for a 1Password store and for Passbolt. The GUI saves and resolves under `rustconn/{name}`, or `RustConn/{group path}/{name} ({protocol})` for the system keyring. So `secret get` never found a password saved from the GUI, and a connect never found one saved with `secret set`. The key functions (`generate_store_key*`, `vault_keys_for_connection`) moved from the GUI into `rustconn-core`, and the CLI uses them. `get` reads the GUI's key first, root search included, then the GUI's older keys and the old CLI key; `delete` removes all of them. Because `rustconn/{name}` carries no group or protocol, same-named connections share it in the GUI as well; `set` and `delete` now name the other connections they affect on stderr (not for KeePass, whose entry path carries the group and protocol). A root-search match on an entry RustConn did not create is now logged at info level, naming the entry, so a wrong match can be traced. `secret get` also passes the connection's name and host, so it honours root search on these backends as it already did for KeePass. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/store_key.rs`, `rustconn/src/vault_ops.rs`, `rustconn-cli/src/commands/secret.rs`)
- **Bitwarden no longer unlocks twice when a connect starts during the startup unlock (issue #353)** — the startup unlock runs on its own thread, outside the vault-op lock. A connect made while it was still running started a second `bw status` / `bw unlock` / `bw sync` alongside it. Every step appeared twice in the reporter's logs, and each `bw` call takes seconds. `auto_unlock` now runs one caller at a time, so the second one waits; if the first stored a session, the second takes the fast path, otherwise it repeats the checks on its own. The startup unlock is now bounded to 30 s, like the connect path's, so it cannot hold the connects behind it indefinitely. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)

### Documentation
- **CLI reference and user guide** — `docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md` describes the keys `secret get/set/delete` use and that `secret get` honours root search on every backend that has it; `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` gives the system keyring's hierarchical key format. (`docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: tokio 1.53.3 → 1.54.0, rustls-webpki 0.103.15 → 0.103.16, crc32fast 1.5.2 → 1.5.3, typed-path 0.12.3 → 0.12.4. All semver-compatible; tokio is a direct dependency (minor bump), the rest are transitive patch bumps. No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean), and `cargo outdated --root-deps-only` reports every direct dependency current. CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2, confirmed latest upstream; kubectl/Tailscale/Teleport/Boundary/Hoop.dev/Bitwarden/1Password auto-resolve and were reachable); the RustCrypto release-candidate family and the out-of-range majors remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.16. The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync and current (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.2, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0, each confirmed as the latest upstream release).

## [0.23.16] - 2026-10-19

### Fixed
- **Embedded RDP to a Linux server that offers only Standard RDP Security no longer dies silently — it falls back to the external FreeRDP client (issue #366)** — a server such as xrdp with its default `security_layer=negotiate` down-selects the connection to Standard RDP Security (RC4) after the embedded IronRDP client advertised SSL/HYBRID/HYBRID_EX. `ironrdp-connector` 0.10 rejects that with `"client advertised SSL | HYBRID | HYBRID_EX, but server selected STANDARD_RDP_SECURITY"`. The failure classifier matched the prose spelling `"standard rdp security"` (from the #235 negotiation error) but not `STANDARD_RDP_SECURITY` — the `SecurityProtocol` enum-variant name `ironrdp-connector` prints verbatim, which lowercases to `standard_rdp_security` with underscores, not spaces. The message therefore fell through to `RdpFailureClass::Other`, which warrants no fallback, so the session was stranded with no handover even though external FreeRDP speaks the RC4 standard security IronRDP does not implement. The underscore spelling is added to `SECURITY_MARKERS`, so the case now classifies as `SecurityUnsupported` and hands the session to the external client exactly like the #235 prose form. Confirmed live against xrdp 0.9.24 on Ubuntu 24.04: the classification moved from `Other` (silent death) to `SecurityUnsupported` (external FreeRDP launched). A regression test pins the verbatim connector-0.10 string. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`)
- **A wrong password to an NLA-only RDP server now says so, instead of reporting a raw `exit status: 161` (issue #366)** — the #366 TLS-only fallback hands the session to external FreeRDP pinned to `/sec:tls` when the embedded client's CredSSP is rejected with `STATUS_LOGON_FAILURE` (`0xc000006d`), on the hunch that the server is a KRdp/FreeRDP-3.32 box advertising `HYBRID_EX` it cannot complete. But that status is byte-for-byte identical to a genuinely wrong password, so against a server that legitimately *requires* NLA the `/sec:tls` retry is refused with `ERRCONNECT_HYBRID_REQUIRED_BY_SERVER` ("The server requires Network Level Authentication, but it is not enabled"). `classify_client_output` had no branch for that code and fell through to the generic `"RDP connection failed (exit status: 161)"`, which told the user nothing. A `HYBRID_REQUIRED_BY_SERVER` branch now reports that the server requires NLA and the credentials are likely wrong — the actionable truth, since reaching this point means the `0xc000006d` really was a credential failure rather than the HYBRID_EX regression. Confirmed live against a FreeRDP 3.32.1 NLA server. A regression test pins the verbatim external-client output. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)

### Removed
- **Removed 59 stale editor backup files from the source tree** — numbered backup snapshots (`automation.rs.bak2`…`.bak42`, `terminal/osc52.rs.bak18`…`.bak67`, `terminal/detach.rs.bak9`…`.bak37`, `window/group_broadcast.rs.bak27`…`.bak40`, `external_session.rs.bak*`, `utils.rs.bak*`, `dialogs/tunnel.rs.bak26`, `dialogs/widgets.rs.bak15`…`.bak17`, `window/detach_actions.rs.bak29`) were still tracked. The 0.23.15 cleanup removed only the `embedded_rdp`/`embedded_vnc` batch and recorded that `.gitignore` would stop new ones — but the `*.bak` pattern does not match the numbered suffixes editors actually produce (`git check-ignore` confirms `foo.rs.bak30` was not ignored). The files are deleted and the ignore pattern is widened to `*.bak*`, so numbered backups are now both untracked and ignored. (`.gitignore`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: cc 1.8.0 → 1.9.0, find-msvc-tools 0.1.14 → 0.1.15, inout 0.2.4 → 0.2.5, ppmd-rust 1.5.0 → 1.5.1, zip 9.0.4 → 9.0.5. All semver-compatible patch/minor bumps (zip is the one direct dependency, used by the archive import/export path; the rest are build-time or transitive). No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2, confirmed latest upstream; kubectl/Tailscale/Teleport/Boundary/Hoop.dev/Bitwarden/1Password auto-resolve and were reachable); the RustCrypto release-candidate family and the out-of-range majors remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.15.
- **Flatpak/Flathub: FreeRDP 3.32.1 → 3.32.2** — a FreeRDP upstream **security and bugfix** release that hardens the code base against malicious client/server messages (a large batch of CVE/GHSA advisories), plus `[server,ntlm] fix missing UNICODE flag that causes authentication to fail`. The bundled source module and its `sha256` are updated in all three manifests (local, Flathub, and the `.local` dev manifest). The rest of the Flatpak stack is confirmed current and unchanged: GNOME runtime 51 (GNOME 52 is not due until spring 2027), cJSON 1.7.19 (latest), OpenH264 2.6.0 (latest). The Snap stays on `core24` + the `gnome-46-2404` extension — the `core26` GNOME extension still does not exist (issue #174).

## [0.23.15] - 2026-10-17

### Fixed
- **Mouse pointer no longer vanishes on the "Session disconnected" screen after a remote RDP box reboots (issue #383)** — the embedded IronRDP viewer mirrors the server's cursor: a `CursorHidden` event sets the drawing area's pointer to `"none"`. A rebooting Windows host hides its cursor on the "Restarting…" screen (and holds it there, longest during Windows Updates) just before it drops the link, so the last cursor state RustConn received was "hidden". When the session then disconnected, nothing restored the local pointer, so the "Session disconnected" / Reconnect overlay was presented with an invisible cursor and the user had to hunt for the Reconnect button. On the `Disconnected` transition the viewer now forces the default arrow (`set_cursor_from_name("default")`) unconditionally — there is no remote session left to own the pointer, so `show_local_cursor` does not apply — making the Reconnect overlay usable regardless of the cursor state the session ended in. (The reporter's hunch about OpenH264 being disabled is unrelated: this is pointer state, not video decoding.) (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **Same invisible-pointer fix applied to the embedded VNC viewer** — the VNC viewer mirrors the server cursor identically (an empty server `CursorUpdate` bitmap sets the pointer to `"none"`), and its reconnect banner is shown in exactly the `Disconnected`/`Error` states, so a VNC server that left the cursor hidden before dropping the link reproduced the #383 symptom on the VNC reconnect banner. The default arrow is now restored centrally in the state-change handler whenever the reconnect banner is shown, so every banner-showing transition is covered by one invariant (banner visible ⇒ arrow visible): clean disconnect, "unexpected end of file" (EOF-as-disconnect), a generic session error, and the failure path of the external-viewer encryption fallback. (`rustconn/src/embedded_vnc/mod.rs`)

### Changed
- **Removed stale editor backup files from the source tree** — six `launcher.rs.bak*` snapshots in `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/` and one `mod.rs.bak14` in `rustconn/src/embedded_vnc/` had been committed before `*.bak` was added to `.gitignore` (which does not untrack already-tracked files). They are now deleted; the `.gitignore` rule already prevents new ones.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: inout 0.2.3 → 0.2.4, zip 9.0.3 → 9.0.4, openh264 0.9.8 → 0.9.9, openh264-sys2 0.9.8 → 0.9.9, lzma-rust2 0.21.0 → 0.22.0, zstd 0.14.2 → 0.14.3, zstd-safe 8.1.0 → 8.2.0, zstd-sys 2.1.1 → 2.1.2, libc 0.2.190 → 0.2.191, schannel 0.1.29 → 0.1.30, combine 4.6.8 → 4.6.9, font-types 0.12.6 → 0.12.7, zbus_names 4.3.4 → 4.3.5, zvariant 5.15.0 → 5.15.1, zvariant_derive 5.15.0 → 5.15.1. All semver-compatible patch/minor bumps. The inout bump clears a yanked-version warning: 0.2.3 was yanked upstream (`cargo deny check advisories` flagged it as a yanked crate, pulled transitively through the aes-gcm/picky stack), and 0.2.4 is the drop-in replacement; advisories are otherwise clean. The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).

## [0.23.14] - 2026-10-15

### Fixed
- **Password login no longer fails on servers that offer both keyboard-interactive and password (issue #382)** — when a server advertised `keyboard-interactive` *and* `password` authentication, a password-based SSH connection would fail with `Permission denied` even though the password was correct. OpenSSH counts `NumberOfPasswordPrompts` per *method*, so it invoked the `SSH_ASKPASS` helper **twice** — once per method — against the same secret-file path, but the generated helper script deleted the secret file (`rm -f`) immediately after its first read. The second invocation then found no file and emitted an empty password. This is a regression surfaced by the keyboard-interactive support added for ESXi in 0.23.13 (#364), which made the two-method case reachable. The in-script delete is removed: the helper now only reads the file, and cleanup is already owned by the child-exit watcher (`watch_child_exit` removes every registered secret path when the ssh process exits) with the file living in a user-private `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (tmpfs, mode 0600) — so dropping the `rm -f` costs no security. The fix is in the shared `askpass_script_contents`, so it covers the target-account helper and every jump-host (bastion) helper identically; a new regression test runs the generated helper twice against one secret file (for the target and jump-hop env vars) and asserts both invocations emit the password and the file survives. (`rustconn/src/window/protocols_ssh.rs`)

### Changed
- **Development toolchain pinned to Rust 1.99.0 (was 1.97.1)** — the `rust-toolchain.toml` channel and the `RUST_TOOLCHAIN` env in both CI workflows (`ci.yml`, `release.yml`) move together, kept in step by the existing `hygiene` drift gate. MSRV is unchanged and still 1.95: the `msrv` CI job, the MSRV-built RPM and the snap each keep their deliberate `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.95` override. OBS compiles with the `stable` toolchain its build VM vendors (`scripts/obs-publish.sh` copies `rustc --print sysroot`), so it picks up 1.99 as the OBS stable repos reach it — no pin to move there. The bump surfaced two newly-stabilised clippy lints: `needless_borrows_for_generic_args` (5 sites in `rustconn-core/src/import/csv_import.rs`, fixed by dropping a redundant `&` on a `Copy` closure passed to `Option::and_then`) and `assert_is_empty` (225 sites, almost all test assertions). The latter is a style lint whose rewrite (`assert_eq!(x, [] as [T; 0])`) is type-specific noise that would force `Debug`/`PartialEq` onto types for test cosmetics only, so it is allowed workspace-wide with a reason in `[workspace.lints.clippy]`, consistent with the other pedantic/nursery lints the project already suppresses. (`rust-toolchain.toml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `Cargo.toml`, `rustconn-core/src/import/csv_import.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: inout 0.2.2 → 0.2.3. A semver-compatible transitive patch bump; no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2, confirmed latest upstream); the RustCrypto release-candidate family and the out-of-range majors remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.13. The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).

## [0.23.13] - 2026-10-14

### Added
- **Expand and collapse sidebar groups with the arrow keys (issue #380)** — the connection tree is a GTK4 `ListView` + `TreeListModel` + `TreeExpander`, and that modern list stack (unlike the deprecated `GtkTreeView`) binds no bare Left/Right to the expander — only Ctrl+Space — so a keyboard user could not open or close a group without Enter or the mouse. Right now expands the focused collapsed group; Left collapses an expanded group, and on a leaf or an already-collapsed group moves the selection up to its parent group (the behaviour users notice missing most). In right-to-left layouts the two keys swap, so the arrow that points into the tree expands and the opposite one collapses. The handling lives in the sidebar's existing key controller, acts on the single focused row in multi-selection mode, and leaves Enter untouched (it still toggles a group or connects a leaf via `activate`). Both shortcuts are listed in the Ctrl+? shortcuts dialog — which also feeds the keybinding-conflict checker — and in the user guide. (`rustconn/src/sidebar/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/shortcuts.rs`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Optional drag-to-resize for the sidebar (issue #3)** — by default the sidebar keeps the fixed width of libadwaita's adaptive sidebar pattern: one **Sidebar width** value in Settings ▸ Interface (180–500, default 320), exactly as in 0.23.12. A new **Resize sidebar by dragging** toggle (off by default) lets you drag the sidebar's inner edge instead. The drag rewrites that same Sidebar width within the same 180–500 range, narrowed to what the sidebar can actually show — no narrower than its own buttons allow and no wider than the window leaves room for — so the edge stays under the pointer, the saved width is the width on screen, the spin row and the drag never disagree, and a dragged width survives a restart; it is saved when the drag ends. The edge follows the pointer under text scaling (Large Text) and in right-to-left layouts. The handle is hidden entirely — no separator, no resize cursor, no extra width — while the option is off and on narrow windows where the sidebar is a swipe-driven overlay, and toggling the option applies as soon as Settings closes. Screen readers announce the handle as a separator labelled "Resize sidebar"; the keyboard way to change the width remains the Sidebar width spin row. The option is opt-in because a draggable edge is not part of that pattern: GNOME Files lost its draggable sidebar in GNOME 43 when it moved to libadwaita's adaptive sidebar. The sidebar stays an `AdwOverlaySplitView` (not a `GtkPaned`), so adaptive collapse, overlay and swipe behaviour are untouched. (`rustconn-core/src/config/settings.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/sidebar_resize.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/state/mod.rs`, `rustconn/assets/style.css`)

### Fixed
- **Bitwarden root-search now finds an entry titled by the leaf of a slash-separated connection name (issue #353)** — with "search entire vault" enabled, a connection named by a path such as `AME/DE/11/hanfile03` still failed to resolve against a Bitwarden vault entry simply titled `hanfile03`, while the same vault resolved correctly under KeePass. The two backends had diverged: KeePass's root matcher already extracts the basename of each candidate (`candidate.rsplit('/')`), but the Bitwarden path only ever offered the *full* name/host as candidates and matched entry titles exactly, so the bare leaf was never searched for. The fix moves the leaf extraction into the shared `LookupIdentity::aliases` (`rustconn-core/src/secret/backend.rs`), so every root-search backend — Bitwarden, 1Password, pass, and the KDBX wrapper — now offers the slash-leaf as an additional candidate, placed right after the full alias so an exact full-title match still wins first. Matching stays exact (not substring), so a bare `db` can never steal a `database-prod` entry. Verified: the settings toggle already reaches the live backend correctly (`SecretManager::rebuild_from_settings` runs on every Secrets-tab save), so this was purely the missing leaf candidate, not a stale-settings bug. New unit tests cover the slash-leaf alias extraction and its edges (plain name, trailing slash, absent fields) and a Bitwarden lookup resolving a slash-named connection to a leaf-titled entry. (`rustconn-core/src/secret/backend.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/secret/bitwarden.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: rustls 0.23.45 → 0.23.46, sha2 0.11.0 → 0.11.1, tokio 1.53.2 → 1.53.3, zip 9.0.2 → 9.0.3, zerocopy 0.8.62 → 0.8.63, zerocopy-derive 0.8.62 → 0.8.63, deranged 0.5.8 → 0.5.9, equivalent 1.0.2 → 1.0.3. All semver-compatible patch bumps; no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2, confirmed latest upstream); the RustCrypto release-candidate family and the out-of-range majors remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.12. The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).

## [0.23.12] - 2026-10-13

### Added
- **Sidebar row spacing and text size are now adjustable, independent of Compact interface (#379)** — a user with 200+ hosts asked to tighten the gap between connections and groups and to control the sidebar font, because Compact interface appeared to change nothing in the list. It genuinely did not: the per-row vertical rhythm was a GTK *widget* margin (`set_margin_top/bottom(7)` on each row's content box in `sidebar/view.rs`), and a GTK4 widget margin is added outside the CSS box and cannot be overridden by a stylesheet — so Compact's `window.compact listview > row` rule was dead code with no visible effect. Density now lives where CSS can reach it. Two new Settings ▸ Interface ▸ Appearance controls: **Sidebar row spacing**, a 0–12px slider (default 7, reproducing the old spacing exactly, so existing configs look unchanged until moved) that drives `.navigation-sidebar > row` padding through a dedicated live CSS provider; and **Sidebar text size** (Small / Medium / Large), a class scoped to the row label alone (`.sidebar-row-label`) so icons, group arrows and status indicators keep their metrics — a label-only class rather than a `window` font-size, which cascades unpredictably into VTE and em-based metrics in GTK4. Both apply instantly (no restart), are remembered across restarts and are applied from the first frame without opening Preferences. The two controls are fully independent of **Compact interface**, which now tightens window chrome only; the superseded (and ineffective) compact list-row CSS was removed. (`rustconn-core/src/config/settings.rs`, `rustconn/src/sidebar/view.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/mod.rs`, `rustconn/assets/style.css`)

### Changed
- **Quick Connect uses idiomatic Adwaita rows** — the Host, Username, Password and Port fields were bare GTK widgets dropped into action-row suffixes, which read differently from the connection editor and name dialog. They are now `AdwEntryRow` / `AdwPasswordEntryRow` / `AdwSpinRow` with floating title labels, so the Quick Connect form matches the rest of the app. (`rustconn/src/window/edit_dialogs.rs`)

### Fixed
- **Quick Connect is now keyboard-first** — the dialog opened with focus on nothing, and because Connect is a header button, pressing Enter in a field did nothing, so a connection needed the mouse. The Host field is now focused on open, and the three text fields activate the Connect button as the dialog default, so Enter connects from any field (the empty-host guard still no-ops when nothing is typed). (`rustconn/src/window/edit_dialogs.rs`)
- **Split-pane color 1 (green) and 5 (red/pink) now match across the panel border, tab stripe and sidebar marker** — the three surfaces that identify the same split pane drew from two slightly different palettes: the panel left-border and its header tint used `#2ec27e` / `#e01b24` while the tab stripe and sidebar marker followed the authoritative `SPLIT_COLOR_VALUES` (`#33d17a` / `#f66151`). The panel colors are aligned to the shared palette so one pane reads as one color everywhere. (`rustconn/assets/style.css`)
- **The sidebar "connecting" status icon has a single, correct color** — the `.status-connecting` (and `.status-connected` / `.status-failed`) rules were defined in both `style.css` (at application priority) and `sidebar_types.rs` (at a higher user priority that always won), so the `style.css` "connecting = accent + opacity pulse" rule was dead and the state actually rendered as the warning color. The dead duplicates were removed, leaving `sidebar_types.rs` as the single source of truth; no visible change. (`rustconn/assets/style.css`)
- **Monitoring: the activity timer spins grey out when they do nothing** — in Settings ▸ Monitoring, the Default Quiet Period and Default Silence Timeout spins stayed active even for the Off and Command modes, which ignore both values. They now follow the Default Mode (sensitive only for Activity / Silence), gated by the same `MonitorMode::uses_timers` predicate the activity coordinator uses, applied on load and live on mode change. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/monitoring_tab.rs`)
- **Three user actions that failed silently now report the failure** — a code audit found the same swallowed-error pattern the 0.23.8–0.23.10 releases fixed piecemeal: a user-triggered save or delete that only logged via `tracing` and left the user with no feedback (the project's error-feedback rule requires a modal dialog for a save/delete failure, never logs alone). Three live sites were fixed to the canonical pattern already used by the Tunnels dialog delete path (`alert::show_error`) and the smart-folder actions. (1) **Saving a tunnel in the Visual Tunnel Builder** — `save_tunnel_to_state` wrote the tunnel and then dropped any `save_settings` error into `tracing::error!`, closing the wizard as if it had succeeded, so the tunnel would silently vanish on next launch; it now returns the error to both callers (the normal Save and the "Save Anyway" running-tunnel path), which show a modal *Could Not Save Tunnel* and leave the wizard open so the user can retry. This is the exact bug the 0.23.8 release fixed for the tunnel *delete* path but never for the sibling *save* path — a root-cause fix across both call sites. (2) **Deleting a snippet** from the Snippets manager logged a failed delete and then rebuilt the menu and list as if it had worked, so a failed delete looked successful (the snippet stayed/returned); it now shows a modal *Could Not Delete Snippet* and skips the rebuild when nothing changed. (3) **Toggling hardware video acceleration (OpenH264)** in Settings ▸ Clients persisted the switch with a log-only failure path while its two sibling actions in the same handler already surfaced errors; it now shows an error toast when the setting cannot be saved. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/tunnel_builder/mod.rs`, `rustconn/src/window/snippets.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/clients_tab.rs`)

### Documentation
- **Corrected two user-guide inaccuracies and documented the new controls** — the guide stated the Sidebar width spin ranges 260–500px when the code allows 180–500; it now matches. The Appearance section documents the new Sidebar row spacing and Sidebar text size controls, and the Quick Connect section documents its focus-on-open and Enter-to-connect behaviour. A stale "34px" header-icon-button size in code comments was corrected to the real 32px. (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `rustconn/src/window/ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/app.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: zip 8.6.0 → 9.0.2 (a direct dependency, the one held-back major that is ours alone to move; it pulled constant_time_eq 0.4.2 → 0.6.1, lzma-rust2 0.16.5 → 0.21.0, zstd 0.13.3 → 0.14.2 and zstd-safe 7.3.0 → 8.1.0), plus the semver-compatible aws-lc-rs 1.18.1 → 1.18.2, aws-lc-sys 0.45.0 → 0.46.0, libz-sys 1.1.29 → 1.1.30 and syn 3.0.8 → 3.0.9. zip 9's one breaking change that touched us is `ZipFile::name()` now returning `Result<Cow<str>, ZipError>` instead of `&str` (a non-UTF-8 entry name is surfaced rather than lossily decoded); the single affected call site in the Royal TS importer was updated to default an undecodable name to empty. No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2); the RustCrypto release-candidate family (aes-gcm, ecdsa, rsa) and the out-of-range majors (x509-cert 0.2 → 0.3, picky-krb 0.12.4 → 0.12.5) remain held back — all three are pinned transitively by the ironrdp/sspi stack (x509-cert by `ironrdp-pdu`, picky-krb and the RustCrypto RCs by `sspi 0.21.3` via `picky`), so they cannot move without bumping ironrdp itself; picky-krb 0.12.5 also still breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3 (its `GssApiMessageError` variant), exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.11. The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).

## [0.23.11] - 2026-10-12

### Fixed
- **Opening a Local Shell as the second pane of a fresh RDP split no longer crashes** — splitting a connected RDP (or SSH) session and then choosing **Local Shell** for the empty pane crashed the process with SIGSEGV. The Local Shell path is the only one that *spawns* a new VTE terminal and *reparents* it into the pane in a single gesture (`move_session_to_panel` → `set_panel_content`, which `unparent`s the widget and re-`append`s it under a fresh overlay); the Select Tab paths move an already-settled widget. Immediately after that move, the handler restored the host tab and grabbed focus on the just-reparented terminal **in the same main-loop tick**, racing GTK's own teardown of the old parent's surface: GTK was left dispatching a Wayland event against a `wl_proxy` whose surface had already been destroyed, and `wl_proxy_get_version` on it faulted. A gdb backtrace confirmed the crash is entirely inside `libgtk-4`/`libwayland-client` (`wl_proxy_get_version` ← GTK ← `wl_display_dispatch_queue_pending` ← `g_application_run`) with no RustConn frame — a use-after-free our reparent-then-focus sequence triggered, not our own memory bug, which is also why it reproduced identically under the GL, Cairo and default renderers (it is not the GPU/Vulkan path). The tab restore and focus grab are now deferred to the next idle, after GTK has finished the reparent and dropped the stale proxy, which breaks the race; the widgets are re-resolved inside the idle so a session closed in the meantime is a no-op. (`rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`)

### Documentation
- **Documented how to run ChromaTerm (and other output filters) from a sandboxed build (#343)** — the reporter who wanted coloured *text* rather than the highlight overlay's underline followed the docs to ChromaTerm as an Output Filter, then hit the real wall: a Flatpak/Snap RustConn cannot reach a host `chromaterm`, and feeding it its own `PYTHONPATH` still fails because the sandbox's Python cannot see ChromaTerm's `pyyaml` dependency. The Output Filter section of the user guide now spells out the working path — install ChromaTerm on the host and set the filter command to `flatpak-spawn --host chromaterm`, which runs it with the host's Python so `yaml` resolves. (The underline-vs-coloured-text limit, the multibyte-colour crash, the tab/CJK/scrollback positioning and the toggle for the built-in ERROR/WARNING/CRITICAL/FATAL rules were all already addressed in 0.22.6–0.23.x.) (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`)
- **Clarified the snap's external FreeRDP client and documented the core26 migration (#342)** — the snap has bundled FreeRDP's X11 client (`xfreerdp3`, from `freerdp3-x11`) since 0.22.7 and gained the `network-status` plug (fixing the portal `NetworkMonitor` warning) in 0.22.11, so the reported gaps are already closed. The `snap/snapcraft.yaml` base comment now carries an actionable, numbered checklist for the eventual `base: core24 → core26` move (enabling `--features adw-1-8`, re-checking the VTE and `freerdp3-x11`/`sdl-freerdp3` package names on 26.04, and re-running the strict-confinement smoke), tied to the gnome extension's core26 support tracked in [snapcraft#6185](https://github.com/canonical/snapcraft/issues/6185) and RustConn#174. `docs/SNAP.md` notes that an `sdl-freerdp3` client should be preferred over the X11 one once the snap is on 26.04. No build or runtime change. (`snap/snapcraft.yaml`, `docs/SNAP.md`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: cc 1.7.0 → 1.8.0, serde_json 1.0.152 → 1.0.154, syn 3.0.7 → 3.0.8, unicode-ident 1.0.26 → 1.0.27, zopfli 0.8.3 → 0.8.4. All semver-compatible patch/minor bumps (serde_json plus the build-time proc-macro and compression families); no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2); the RustCrypto release-candidate family and the out-of-range majors (zip 8→9, x509-cert 0.2→0.3) remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.10; picky-krb 0.12.5 stays held at 0.12.4 for the same reason (its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3). The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).

## [0.23.10] - 2026-10-11

### Added
- **"Move to New Window" for a split-view pane** — a split pane could be returned to its own tab ("Remove from Split") but not sent straight to its own window; the only route was Remove from Split, then detach the resulting tab. A pane now offers **Move to New Window** directly, both as a corner button (stacked above Remove-from-Split and Close) and as a context-menu item (Shift+F10). It composes the two existing, tested paths — the pane is removed from the split exactly as "Remove from Split" does, then handed to the window layer's `detach-session`, which re-checks detach eligibility and explains any refusal with a toast — so no new reparenting path is introduced. (`rustconn/src/window/split_view_actions.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`, `rustconn/src/split_view/bridge.rs`)

### Changed
- **The split-pane and session-toolbar reveal handles rest a little more transparently** — the hover-reveal arrows sat at 0.3 / 0.4 opacity when idle; they are now 0.22 / 0.3 (with a correspondingly lighter background), so they intrude a few percent less on the session content at rest. The hover state is unchanged, so they still come fully forward under the pointer. (`rustconn/assets/style.css`)
- **Clearer wording for the two floating-control settings** — the Interface tab's "Open session toolbar on hover" and "Hide hover controls in split panes" subtitles now spell out the two independent controls so the layout is self-explanatory: the session toolbar is a handle on the top edge of RDP/VNC/embedded-browser viewers (not terminals), while a reveal arrow at the middle of the pane's right edge carries close/detach/reconnect on every split pane of any protocol, with close/detach also reachable from the pane menu (Shift+F10) and the sidebar. No behaviour change. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)

### Fixed
- **The split-pane controls no longer sit on top of the RDP/VNC session toolbar** — the split adapter's per-pane reveal controls (close / detach / reconnect) were anchored at the top-right corner, directly over the right end of the viewer's floating session toolbar — exactly where its most important actions live (RDP Ctrl+Alt+Del and the "⋯" overflow menu). In a split pane the two piled into one unreadable cluster, and because every pane — including a lone fullscreen one — carries this overlay, the same controls blocked the toolbar's overflow menu in fullscreen too. The pane controls now live at the **vertical centre of the pane's right edge**, off the top edge entirely, so the top edge belongs to the session toolbar alone (its handle is centred there) and the two overlaid control sets never compete, in split or fullscreen. The hover arrow keeps its 44×44 HIG touch target, and the revealed Remove-from-Split / Close buttons are stacked **vertically** (one under the other) to hug the right edge instead of reaching across the pane, HIG order kept (non-destructive on top, Close at the bottom). (`rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_toolbar_overflow.rs`, `rustconn/assets/style.css`)
- **Snippet variable prompts now list fields in template order** — two separate `extract_variables` implementations had drifted apart: the GUI's returned variable names in first-seen (template) order while `SnippetManager::extract_variables` sorted them alphabetically, so the same template could present its fill-in fields in two different orders depending on the path. Both now delegate to one canonical `rustconn_core::variables::extract_variable_names`, which preserves template order — the order the user wrote the variables in, which reads more naturally in the prompt form than an alphabetical sort. Variable detection and de-duplication are unchanged (the snippet property tests compare as sets and still pass). (`rustconn-core/src/variables/mod.rs`, `rustconn-core/src/snippet/manager.rs`, `rustconn/src/utils.rs`)
- **The split-pane reveal arrow now meets the HIG touch-target minimum** — the hover/click arrow that reveals a pane's corner actions was 28×28px, below the GNOME HIG 44×44 pointer/touch-target floor the sibling embedded-toolbar reveal handle already honours. Both its `set_size_request` and its `.panel-reveal-arrow` CSS `min-width/height` are now 44px; the 16px symbolic icon is unchanged, so only the clickable/visible frame grows. (`rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`, `rustconn/assets/style.css`)
- **Starting a session recording that fails is no longer silent** — the sidebar **Start recording** action discarded the `bool` returned by `start_recording`, so when the recordings folder could not be resolved or was not writable the only trace was a `tracing::error!`: no toast, and the `●REC` tab indicator never appeared, leaving the user with no feedback that their action failed. The action now shows an error toast (`Could not start recording for '…' — the recordings folder is missing or not writable`), per the project's error-feedback rule that a failed user-triggered action is never swallowed into logs. (`rustconn/src/window/terminal_actions.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP/VNC status now carries a shape, not colour alone (GNOME HIG, a11y)** — the "connecting / connected / failed / disconnected" line on the embedded RDP and VNC placeholder screens was distinguished only by its text colour (green / yellow / red / grey). Colour alone is ambiguous in grayscale, under a high-contrast theme, and for users with colour-vision deficiency — a direct HIG violation, and the one accessibility gap a 0.23.10 code audit found (the sidebar already uses colour-independent silhouettes, R6.1–6.5). Each state now prefixes a distinct glyph to the status line — `✓` connected, `…` connecting, `⚠` failed, `○`/`✕` disconnected/ended — so the state reads with no colour at all; the accent colour stays as reinforcement. The glyph is a bare symbol composed outside `i18n()`, so no translatable strings changed. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_vnc/ui.rs`)

## [0.23.9] - 2026-10-11

### Fixed
- **RDP now connects to KRdp / NLA-less Linux hosts that reject the embedded client's CredSSP (#366)** — a KRdp 6.6.x server built on FreeRDP 3.32.x advertises `HYBRID_EX` ("Ext") security it cannot actually complete: the embedded IronRDP client lets the server pick `HYBRID_EX`, the server's NTLM/SAM path fails internally, and the client sees `STATUS_LOGON_FAILURE` (`0xc000006d`) even with correct credentials — byte-for-byte identical to a wrong password, which is why #366 was reported as "invalid username or password". This is a server-side regression (FreeRDP issue [#13567](https://github.com/FreeRDP/FreeRDP/issues/13567); KRdp fixed it in 6.7.90). Remmina reaches the same server because it negotiates **plain TLS**, which never offers `HYBRID_EX` — confirmed live, with `/sec:tls` the FreeRDP negotiation drops to `nego_enable_ext: FALSE`. RustConn now does the same: on that rejection it hands the session once to external FreeRDP with the security layer pinned to TLS (`/sec:tls`), matching Remmina. The decision is a pure, unit-tested `warrants_tls_only_fallback` in `rustconn-core::rdp_client::failure` — it fires only for the ambiguous `InvalidToken`/logon-failure shape (never for account-state statuses TLS cannot fix) and only once, so if the TLS-only path is rejected too the real error surfaces with actionable advice (update KRdp to 6.7.90+, set `ExtSecurity=0`, or pin FreeRDP 3.31.0) instead of looping. Two further tweaks were needed for KRdp, both bisected live against a real server: the explicit colour depth is dropped (an explicit `/bpp:32` made KRdp answer the capabilities exchange with `PDU_TYPE_DEACTIVATE_ALL`; `None` lets FreeRDP negotiate it), and the external window opens resizable with content scaling (`/smart-sizing` + a 1280×800 start size) instead of the fixed monitor-sized `/size:100%`. (`rustconn-core/src/rdp_client/failure.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **An RDP tab no longer hangs on "Starting RDP…" / "running in external window" after the external client closes (#366)** — two bugs kept the tab stuck once the external FreeRDP window ended: `notify_ironrdp_state` changed the state but never repainted the placeholder (so the Cairo surface kept the previous label), and the external-exit watchdog both stopped polling after 30s and stood down silently on a post-window exit instead of moving the GUI to Disconnected. The placeholder now repaints on every state change, the watchdog polls for the whole session (not a 30s cap) and transitions to Disconnected on exit, and it also detects a session that dropped without the client process exiting (the SDL client against KRdp logs `Network disconnect` / `CONNECT_CANCELLED` but stays alive) and frees the tab then too. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/connection.rs`)
- **The embedded Wayland FreeRDP fallback now honours the security layer and NLA switches** — the embedded `wlfreerdp3` subsurface path built its argument vector by hand and omitted `/sec` entirely, so it always ran FreeRDP's own default (Negotiate → NLA) regardless of the connection's **Security layer** or **Disable NLA** settings. It now emits `/sec:<layer>` and `/sec:nla:off` exactly as the external launcher's `push_security_args` does, so a user who pins TLS or disables NLA on a server where FreeRDP's TLS is compatible is actually honoured on the embedded path. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/thread.rs`)
- **The deprecated `wlfreerdp` client is now tried last in a Wayland session, after XWayland** — upstream FreeRDP deprecated `wlfreerdp` in favour of the SDL3 client (issue #340), and it `SIGSEGV`s in `freerdp_disconnect` against KDE KRdp (seen while diagnosing #366). The external-launch order in a Wayland session is now `sdl-freerdp3 > sdl-freerdp > xfreerdp3 > xfreerdp > wlfreerdp3 > wlfreerdp`, so when the preferred SDL3 client is not installed a working XWayland client is chosen over the crashing `wlfreerdp`. Installing `sdl-freerdp3` remains the best option. (`rustconn-core/src/protocol/detection.rs`)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: serde_json 1.0.151 → 1.0.152, proptest 1.11.0 → 1.12.0, bit-set 0.8.0 → 0.11.1, bit-vec 0.8.0 → 0.10.1, rand_xorshift 0.4.0 → 0.5.0. All semver-compatible patch/minor bumps (serde_json plus the proptest dev-dependency family); no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2); the RustCrypto release-candidate family and the out-of-range majors (zip 8→9, x509-cert 0.2→0.3) remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.8; picky-krb 0.12.5 stays held at 0.12.4 for the same reason (its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3). The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).


## [0.23.8] - 2026-10-10

### Fixed
- **Recording manager failures are surfaced instead of silently swallowed** — in the Recordings dialog, a failure to **rename** or **delete** a recording returned early after only a `tracing::warn!`, so the dialog closed, nothing changed on disk, and the user was given no reason. Both now raise a modal `adw::AlertDialog` (`Could Not Rename Recording` / `Could Not Delete Recording`) with the underlying error, per the project's own error-feedback decision rule (a failed user-triggered data operation belongs in a dialog, never in logs only). A failure to **list** the recordings folder likewise returned into an empty list indistinguishable from "no recordings"; it now swaps the list placeholder to a `dialog-warning-symbolic` status page (`Could Not Load Recordings`) naming the read error, so a load failure is visibly different from an empty folder. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/recording.rs`)
- **Tunnel stop and delete-save failures are no longer silent** — stopping a tunnel from the Tunnels dialog logged a failure only to `tracing`; it now shows an error toast (`Could not stop tunnel '…'`), consistent with the start path which already surfaced a dialog. More importantly, a failure to **save settings after deleting** a standalone tunnel was swallowed, so the deletion silently reappeared on next launch; it now raises a modal `Could Not Save Changes` dialog warning that the change may return. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/tunnel.rs`)
- **Adding an SSH key reports failure in a dialog, not a toast** — a failed `ssh-add` in **Settings → SSH Agent** showed a dismissable error toast while its two sibling failure paths in the same tab used a modal `alert::show_error`. A security action that did not take effect is now reported through the modal dialog (`Could Not Add Key`), matching the siblings and the GNOME HIG error-feedback rule. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ssh_agent_tab.rs`)
- **The "Enable Cloud Sync?" confirmation now has a default response** — the dialog set a close response (Escape → Cancel) but no `default_response`, so Enter did nothing and the initial focus was undefined, unlike every sibling confirmation. Cancel is now the default (the safe action), per the GNOME HIG. (`rustconn/src/window/edit_group.rs`)
- **The new-theme name prompt can no longer be dismissed into a silent no-op** — entering an empty name and pressing **Create** (or Enter) quietly closed the terminal-theme prompt doing nothing. The Create response now stays disabled until the name is non-empty, so the action can never be silently discarded. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/terminal_tab.rs`)
- **The Theme Editor dialog is wide enough to read its colour rows** — the editor's `adw::AlertDialog` grew only to its natural width, leaving the colour rows cramped. `AlertDialog` exposes no `content_width`, so the width is now set through the embedded `ScrolledWindow`'s `min`/`max_content_width` (420–480), the documented GNOME HIG technique for widening a message dialog whose `extra_child` is meant to be read. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/terminal_tab.rs`)
- **The On-startup "open a connection" picker is now type-to-search** — the Interface tab's startup-action `ComboRow` lists one entry per saved connection and can get long, so it now carries the same type-to-search the jump-host picker uses (a `PropertyExpression` over the `StringList` plus `set_enable_search`), instead of a scroll-only popup. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/ui_tab.rs`)

### Changed
- **Settings rows migrated to native libadwaita rows** — the Terminal tab's font-size and scrollback spinners are now `adw::SpinRow`s, and the theme / language / startup-action selectors in the Terminal and Interface tabs are now `adw::ComboRow`s, replacing hand-built `SpinButton`/`DropDown` rows. The result sizes, aligns and themes exactly like every other row in the preferences window, follows the GNOME HIG, and drops bespoke row-assembly code. No behaviour change beyond the native look and the searchable startup picker noted above. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/{mod,terminal_tab,ui_tab}.rs`)
- **Password fields now use revealable native password rows** — the connection editor's password field is now an `adw::PasswordEntryRow` and the Variables dialog's secret-value field a `gtk4::PasswordEntry`, each with the standard reveal (eye) toggle, in place of hand-built masked entries. The value stays masked by default and is handled as a `SecretString` as before; the only change is the native widget and its built-in show/hide control. (`rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/dialog/passwords.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/connection/general_tab.rs`, `rustconn/src/dialogs/variables.rs`)
- **Embedded RDP/VNC status screens follow the light/dark theme** — the "no connection / connecting / external window" placeholder screens of the embedded RDP and VNC viewers were cairo-painted with a fixed dark `rgb(0.12, 0.12, 0.14)` background and near-white text, ignoring the GTK theme, so on a light desktop they rendered as a black rectangle (and the `.vnc-display` CSS background was painted over, making it dead). The placeholder background and body text now derive from the active theme via a new `utils::placeholder_surface_colors()` helper keyed on `adw::StyleManager::is_dark()`; the status accent colours (green/yellow/red/blue state dots) stay fixed because they encode meaning. The dark letterbox around live video and the stale-frame dim (#248) stay fixed-dark on purpose, since a dark border/wash behind actual video is standard. (`rustconn/src/embedded_rdp/{ui,drawing}.rs`, `rustconn/src/embedded_vnc/ui.rs`, `rustconn/src/utils.rs`)
- **Split-view pane actions have a keyboard and hidden-header route (GNOME HIG)** — the occupied-pane Close / Remove-from-Split actions lived only inside the hover-reveal overlay and the pane-header context menu. With no keyboard path, and with the header hidden when connection-name labels are off, a keyboard-only user — or a user who had turned off both the labels and the hover controls — could be left with no way to dismantle a pane. The pane context menu's action group is now mirrored onto the always-present, focusable pane **container**, and **Shift+F10** / the **Menu** key open the same menu anchored to the pane, so Reconnect / Remove from Split / Remove Split / Close Connection are reachable from the keyboard regardless of header or hover-control visibility. The pointer right-click and touch long-press on the header are unchanged, so VTE's native Copy/Paste menu is still intact (#84). (`rustconn/src/split_view/adapter.rs`)
- **Window minimum height lowered to the HIG phone-portrait floor** — the main window requested a 400px minimum height while its width floor was already refined down to the measured narrow-tier minimum (≥360). The height request is now 294, so the window can shrink to the GNOME HIG adaptive 360×294 phone-portrait size. (`rustconn/src/window/mod.rs`)
- **Writing-style polish in Settings** — the Monitoring tab's "System info" switch is now "System information" (HIG: avoid abbreviations), and the Interface tab's startup-action list uses consistent header caps ("Do Nothing" alongside "Local Shell"). (`rustconn/src/dialogs/settings/{monitoring_tab,ui_tab}.rs`)

### Documentation
- Internal UI/UX HIG audit of all interface components for the 0.23.8 release; the actionable findings above were applied, and the audit confirmed the rest of the surface (header bars, toolbars, adaptive layout, mandatory shortcuts, icon-button accessibility, AlertDialog safe-defaults, the CSS `@media`-free tab-bar seam fix) is HIG-compliant.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: cc 1.6.0 → 1.7.0, smallvec 1.16.2 → 1.16.3, syn 3.0.6 → 3.0.7, uuid 1.27.0 → 1.28.0. All semver-compatible patch/minor bumps; no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2); the RustCrypto release-candidate family (aes-gcm, ecdsa, rsa, picky, x25519-dalek, …) and the out-of-range majors (zip 8→9, x509-cert 0.2→0.3) remain held back, pinned transitively by the sspi/picky-krb stack exactly as in 0.23.1–0.23.7; picky-krb 0.12.5 stays held at 0.12.4 for the same reason (its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3). The Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0).

## [0.23.7] - 2026-10-10

### Added
- **Setting to hide the split-pane hover controls (#374)** — a new **Settings → Interface → Window → "Hide hover controls in split panes"** switch hides the top-right hover-reveal arrow (with its close/detach buttons) on split-view panes for users who find it distracting. **Off by default**, so the hover controls stay shown. It is fully independent of "Show connection name in split panes": each has its own switch and neither hides the other, and it applies live to every open split without a restart. With the arrow hidden, the pane header context menu and the sidebar connection menu still offer close/detach.

### Fixed
- **The sidebar's per-session controls are hidden when a connection has several live sessions (#374)** — "Close", "Remove from Split" and "Move to New Window" each act on *one* session, resolved as the *first* session found for the connection. When a connection had two or more live sessions — common with **"Open a new session on every double-click"** enabled, but also reachable via the menu's own "Open new session" or opening the same entry from Favorites — the sidebar would silently act on an arbitrary one of them, which the user could not predict. The sidebar row represents the *connection*, not a single session, so these three controls are now **hidden when the connection has ≥2 live sessions**; they remain per-tab, where each targets its own session unambiguously. Connection-level items (Connect, Open new session, Edit, Delete, …) are unaffected. The count comes from a new `multi_session_checker` wired from the window off the authoritative notebook session list, mirroring the existing recording checker; it is threaded through every menu entry point (right-click, the KDE deep-row fallback, keyboard Menu/Shift+F10, and touch long-press). An external-viewer session is not in the notebook, so a terminal-plus-external pairing is not miscounted — the per-session resolver only ever targets the single notebook session anyway.
- **Sidebar "Close" is now offered for any connected session, and only Delete stays red (#374)** — the sidebar connection menu showed **Close** only while the connection was in a split view; an ordinary connected session had no way to be closed from the sidebar, an inconsistency with the split case (and with the tab's own Close). Close is now offered for every connected session and routes through the same `close-tab-by-id` teardown that handles both a split pane and a plain tab. It is also **no longer styled destructive (red)**: per the GNOME HIG — and the project's own steering — the red `destructive-action` is reserved for irreversible, data-losing operations. Ending a session is recoverable by reconnecting, so Close is a normal item; only **Delete** (which permanently removes the connection) stays red, leaving a single destructive action in the menu rather than two.
- **The password-generator strength test is no longer flaky** — `test_password_strength` generated a length-4 password with the default `require_all_sets` on, which forces one character from each of the four sets into just four slots. A random draw satisfies that only ~11% of the time, so across the 100 generation attempts the test hit `GenerationFailed` roughly once in 70,000 runs — which is what turned the post-tag CI Test job red. The test now turns `require_all_sets` off for the short-password case (the strength assertion is about length, not composition), making generation deterministic. Test-only; the generator itself is unchanged.
- **A re-split after closing a split's owner tab no longer leaves a dead, actionless pane** — closing the tab that *owns* a split returned its guest sessions to their home tabs but left each guest's entry in the per-session bridge map pointing at the owner's torn-down layout. That layout's model still listed the guest's old panels and the now-killed owner session, so the next split on a returned guest **reused** the stale bridge (`get_or_create_session_bridge: REUSING…`) and `restore_panel_contents` walked a panel whose session had no content widget (`no content widget for session …`). The pane was left with no content and no placeholder — no header actions, no hover close/pop buttons, and no empty-pane Select Tab / close X — a "Loading…"-looking dead pane the user could neither fill nor close (it also emitted `gtk_widget_size_allocate … width -38` and `measure … height 0` warnings). Fixed on two levels: **(1)** `on_split_cleanup` now drops each returned guest's bridge entry as well as the owner's, so a later split takes the create-a-fresh-bridge path with a clean single-panel model; **(2)** defensively, `restore_panel_contents` now falls back to the empty placeholder for any panel whose session has no resolvable content widget, so even an unforeseen stale-layout path yields an actionable pane rather than a dead one. Regression-tested (`reset_after_split_restores_a_clean_single_panel_model`). Pre-existing, unrelated to the #374 context-menu work in this release.

### Changed
- **Sidebar action toolbars restyled to match GNOME Files (#374)** — three visual refinements so the sidebar's bottom action row and its "Select Connections" bar read like a native GNOME sidebar rather than a boxed-off panel:
  - **No divider above the bottom toolbar.** Two conflicting `.sidebar toolbarview > .bottom-bar` rules left a `border-top` drawn on the bottom action row, visually splitting it off from the connection list; the border is removed so the row merges into the list the way GNOME Files' sidebar does.
  - **Bottom icons spread evenly across the width.** The row (History / A-Z / Recent / Vault / Smart Folders) was centre-clustered with empty space to its right; it now fills the full sidebar width with homogeneous cells, so the icons distribute evenly like Files' sidebar actions.
  - **"Select Connections" buttons are flat.** The bulk-action buttons carried `.pill`, giving each a resting rounded background; they are now flat symbolic icons (hover-only feedback), visually identical to the bottom toolbar, and the bar uses the same full-width even distribution. The destructive "Delete Selected" keeps its red hover only (no permanent red), per the GNOME HIG.
  - **Multi-select moved to the bottom toolbar; the header-menu checkbox is gone.** "Select Connections" was a checkable item in the sidebar header menu, which widened that popover. It is now a toggle button in the bottom action row (bound to the same `win.group-operations` state, so it shows the active mode and reveals the bulk-actions bar) — more discoverable, and the header menu no longer carries the checkable item. Adding this sixth icon also tightens the bottom row's even spread so it no longer looks sparse. Connection History stays the leftmost quick-access icon, with the Select toggle second; the final order is History / Select / A-Z / Recent / Vault / Smart Folders.
  - **"Create Cluster" dropped from the "Select Connections" bar.** Cluster creation is a niche action next to the everyday bulk operations (New Group, Move, Batch Edit, Select All, Clear, Delete); it was the odd one out in the row. The bar is now six focused buttons. The capability is unchanged — clusters are still created and managed from **Tools ▸ Manage Clusters** (and the `cluster-from-selection` action remains registered).
- **Split-pane close / detach controls follow the GNOME HIG instead of a hover-only reveal (#374)** — a reporter hit three things at once: the pane close "X" was reachable only through a hover-reveal arrow (a touch screen has no hover, which the project's own HIG steering forbids relying on), the right-click context menu did not open over a terminal or remote-desktop pane, and a disabled "Move to New Window" sat inert on split tabs. The fixes:
  - **Pane context menu moved onto the pane header.** With connection-name headers on (the default), right-click — or long-press on touch — the pane header for Reconnect / Remove from Split / Remove Split / Close Connection. It is attached to the header rather than the pane body on purpose: a VTE terminal and an RDP/VNC `DrawingArea` claim button-press in the bubble phase, so a gesture on the container never fired over the content, and moving it to the capture phase to beat them would have swallowed VTE's own native Copy/Paste menu (the #84 regression). The header has no competing input, so the menu is reliable there and the terminal keeps its own right-click menu. A `GestureLongPress` mirrors the right-click for touch, per the HIG's long-press-with-every-context-menu rule.
  - **Hover reveal arrow stays an independent fallback.** The small corner arrow and its close/pop buttons remain available whether or not the connection-name headers are shown — the labels and the hover buttons each have their own switch and do not hide one another (a header label is small and uncluttering, so suppressing the arrow when it is on bought nothing). A user who still finds the arrow distracting can turn it off outright with the new "Hide hover controls in split panes" setting (see Added). Because the hover arrow is a single easy-to-mis-click gesture, its **Close** now asks for confirmation before disconnecting a live session (an `AdwAlertDialog`), while the two-step header menu and sidebar paths are not second-guessed.
  - **Sidebar connection menu gained split/window actions.** Right-click a connected connection in the sidebar for **Remove from Split** and **Close** (shown only while it is in a split) and **Move to New Window**. The last pops a split pane out and detaches it in one step, and also detaches an ordinary background tab — so a split session can go straight to its own window without first dismantling the split by hand.
  - **The dead "Move to New Window" is gone from split tabs.** The tab menu used to keep the item enabled on a split tab only to answer a click with a "remove the split first" toast, which reads as a broken control; it is now hidden there (`offers_detach` accepts only an `Allowed` verdict). The split is dismantled via Remove Split, or detached in one step from the sidebar.

### Documentation
- **User guide: Split View and Detached Session Windows updated** for the header context menu, the long-press equivalent, the conditional close confirmation on the hover close, the hover arrow staying available independently of the name labels, the new sidebar split/window actions, and the removal of the inert detach item from split tabs.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: tokio-util 0.7.19 → 0.7.20, toml 1.1.7 → 1.1.8, toml_edit 0.25.16 → 0.25.17, toml_parser 1.1.4 → 1.1.5, zstd-sys 2.1.0 → 2.1.1. All semver-compatible patch bumps (the TOML parser family plus tokio-util and the zstd FFI shim); no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). All other in-range cargo dependencies were already at their latest compatible versions. CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2); the Flatpak/Flathub manifests are in sync (GNOME runtime 51, FreeRDP 3.32.1, cJSON 1.7.19, OpenH264 2.6.0), and picky-krb 0.12.5 remains held back for the same reason as 0.23.1–0.23.6: its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3, which does not match it exhaustively, so picky-krb stays at 0.12.4.

## [0.23.6] - 2026-10-09

### Added
- **OSC 52: a remote yank can reach the local clipboard (opt-in)** — VTE parses `OSC 52 ; c ; <base64>` and then drops it: through 0.84 its `XTERM_SET_XSELECTION` handler is a no-op, so a yank in a remote Neovim — or in tmux with `set-clipboard external` — put the text nowhere. GNOME Terminal and xfce4-terminal inherit the same hole, so this was a missing capability rather than a RustConn bug, and RustConn needs its own because VTE has no API to switch the handler on. A new `terminal::osc52::Osc52Filter` sits on the session output path (`TerminalNotebook::deliver_output_to`), lifts the offers out of the PTY stream before `Terminal::feed` sees them, and writes them through the same `display().clipboard().set_text()` call copy-on-select uses. It is a byte filter rather than a parser for its own sake: every other byte is passed through in order — `OSC 0` titles, `OSC 133` shell-integration marks, plain `ESC` sequences — and a string the stream ends inside is handed back by `finish()` instead of being kept, because an unterminated OSC left in the stream would make VTE swallow the rest of the session as body. A sequence can straddle PTY reads (8 KiB `READ_CHUNK`), so the filter keeps state between chunks; one test feeds the same offer at every possible split point and one byte at a time and requires identical output. All three terminators (`ESC \`, `BEL`, C1 `ST`) and both introducers (7-bit and C1) are recognised. **Security**: the switch is **Settings → Terminal → "Remote clipboard (OSC 52)"** and defaults to **off**, since a host that can write the clipboard can aim a pastejacking payload at the next terminal the user pastes into. Reads are never served in any configuration — an `OSC 52 ; c ; ?` query is dropped unanswered, so the local clipboard cannot be exfiltrated this way — only the `c` (CLIPBOARD) selection is honoured, with `p` (primary) and `s` (select) passed through untouched. A payload is capped at 1 MiB, is base64-decoded into a `Zeroizing` buffer so the plaintext is wiped on drop, must decode to valid UTF-8 to reach the clipboard, and the decoded text is never logged. The output filter runs before session-transcript observers too, so an enabled offer is lifted out of the saved log as well as the display; with the setting off the sequence reaches both untouched. Remote tmux needs `set-clipboard external` (and `allow-passthrough on`) to forward the sequence; a DCS-wrapped passthrough is not unwrapped yet. (PR #372, thanks @shafiev)
- **Save a terminal session's output to a text file** — a new **Save Output…** entry in a terminal tab's right-click menu writes the session's full scrollback to a plain-text file through VTE's `write_contents_sync` (requested in #371). A file chooser opens with a name derived from the session; the whole buffer is saved (its length is bounded by the Scrollback lines setting). The item is offered only for VTE terminal tabs — SSH, local shell, Telnet, Serial — gated on the session having a terminal widget, so embedded RDP/VNC/Web tabs and the Welcome tab, which have no text buffer, do not show it. In a split tab the **focused pane** is saved — the window wires a focused-session resolver into the notebook (keyed by the tab owner, mirroring the existing split-pane-box provider) so the save targets the pane the user clicked, not always the first one. A save failure is reported in a modal error dialog and a success in a toast, per the project's error-feedback rule; a cancelled chooser is silent. `session_output_filename` sanitises the default name (every non-alphanumeric character becomes `_`, with a `terminal-output` fallback) and is unit-tested. (#371)

### Fixed
- **RDP Performance Mode now reaches the external FreeRDP client** — the per-connection **Performance Mode** (Quality/Balanced/Speed) was applied by the embedded IronRDP client and written into exported `.rdp` files, but the external FreeRDP launch path dropped it entirely: a Speed profile reached `xfreerdp` with wallpaper, themes and full-window drag all on, visually identical to Quality (reported on #370). The user's choice was stored but never consumed on that path. `FreeRdpConfig` now carries `performance_mode`, threaded from the persisted model through both external launch sites (the direct External client and the embedded→external fallback), and `build_freerdp_args` emits the matching experience flags — `+window-drag +menu-anims` for Quality, nothing for Balanced (FreeRDP's own default), `-wallpaper -themes` for Speed. The mapping mirrors the embedded client's `build_performance_flags` and the `.rdp` exporter's `write_performance_settings` so the three paths cannot drift, pinned by a new `freerdp_experience_args_match_mode` test. The remote server still has the final say (a group policy such as *Enforce Removal of Remote Desktop Wallpaper* overrides the client request). The editor's Performance Mode row subtitle now reads "…for image rendering and visual effects" to match the widened reach. (#370)
- **KeePass "Search from vault root" no longer crashes on a vault with non-ASCII entry names** — the from-root matcher tested whether a listing line ended with `/<candidate>` by byte-slicing the line at `line.len() - candidate.len() - 1`. On a database holding non-ASCII paths — a Cyrillic recycle-bin group (`Смітник/…`), a database titled `Паролі` — that index can fall inside a multi-byte UTF-8 character, so the slice panicked and took the whole root search (and the connect) down with it. The same byte-boundary class of bug as the hex-colour crash in #343. The tail test now uses `ends_with` on the whole string with no byte indexing, so a credential kept outside the RustConn group is found whatever script the surrounding entries are named in. A regression test feeds the matcher a Cyrillic-laden listing. (#353)
- **The misleading "Bitwarden: CLI command path stored" log line no longer reads like a vault lookup** — a KeePass user who turned on debug logging to diagnose a credential miss saw this line and reasonably concluded RustConn was querying Bitwarden for their secret (reported while investigating #353). It is nothing of the sort: it is the Settings page's backend-detection caching the resolved `bw` binary name, and with KeePass selected no Bitwarden backend is ever part of credential resolution. The line now says so explicitly ("cached CLI binary path from backend detection (not a vault lookup)"). (#353)
- **`rustconn-cli secret set` now marks the connection vault-backed** — storing a vault secret for a connection created by `rustconn-cli add` left its password source at the `None` default, so a connect never consulted the vault and the password just stored was never used. `secret set` now promotes a `None` source to `Vault` (an explicit Prompt/Variable/Inherit/Script is left as the user's choice), so the add → set flow produces a connection that actually resolves its credential. (#353)

### Changed
- **`rustconn-cli secret get` mirrors the GUI resolver's from-root search** — with "Search from vault root" enabled, `secret get --backend keepass` now applies the same whole-database fallback (keyed by the connection's scoped key, host and name) the connect path uses, instead of only the scoped `RustConn/…` lookup. The CLI can now diagnose exactly what a connect would find. (#353)
- **Added diagnostics for the KeePass "Search from vault root" path** — when a scoped KeePass lookup misses, RustConn now logs the `kdbx_root_search` flag value and the preferred backend right before the from-root search gate, and `collect_secret_settings` logs the read-only / root-search toggle values it collected on save. This turns a "root search never ran" report into a one-line diagnosis of whether the flag reached the connect, while the underlying toggle-persistence issue is tracked. (#353)

### Documentation
- **API docs no longer point at code that is gone, and a gate keeps it that way** — nothing ran `cargo doc`, so rustdoc's own lints never fired and 106 warnings had piled up across `rustconn-core`, `rustconn`, `rustconn-cli` and `rustconn-dock-sys`; 67 of them were links to items that had been renamed, moved or removed. Where the prose had drifted with them it now matches the code: the KeePassXC helpers in `secret/status.rs` documented a `SecretError::Backend` variant that does not exist (they return `SecretError::KeePassXC`), and `CairoBackedBuffer::copy_rect` claimed to mirror a `VncPixelBuffer` that is no longer in the tree. Doc examples in the GUI binary used `rustconn::` paths, which a binary crate does not have, and now use `crate::`. A new `[workspace.lints.rustdoc]` table denies broken intra-doc links and allows public-to-private links, since these docs are read with private items. `scripts/verify.sh` (default mode) and the CI clippy job now run `cargo doc --no-deps --document-private-items` with `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings"`.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: dirs 6.0→7.0, tray-icon 0.25.1→0.26.1, muda 0.20.0→0.21.2. `dirs` 7.0's only breaking change is `preference_dir` on Windows (RoamingAppData instead of LocalAppData) — RustConn never calls `preference_dir` and does not target Windows, so the upgrade is a no-op here. `tray-icon`/`muda` are macOS-only (behind the `tray-macos` feature, not built by CI); the deprecated `TrayIconBuilder::with_icon_as_template(false)` call was dropped in favour of the builder's non-template default (tray-icon 0.26 renamed it to the `with_icon_templated` opt-in), and `muda` 0.21's removed `TextStyle`/`libxdo` APIs are not used here. All in-range cargo dependencies were already at their latest compatible versions (`cargo update` changed nothing); `cargo deny check advisories` is clean.

## [0.23.5] - 2026-10-08

### Fixed
- **Default `~/.ssh/config` import now actually imports under Flatpak** — the "SSH Config" import source (which reads `~/.ssh/config` automatically) reported success but added nothing, while picking the *same* file by hand worked (reported on #368). Root cause is the Flatpak sandbox: the manifest grants only `--filesystem=home/.ssh:ro` (not full `--filesystem=home`), so the host `~/.ssh` is reachable at its real path, but `$HOME` — and therefore `dirs::home_dir()` — points at the per-app sandbox home `~/.var/app/io.github.totoshko88.RustConn`. `SshConfigImporter::default_paths()` resolved `~/.ssh/config` through that sandbox home and found nothing, whereas the manual file picker goes through the document portal, which grants the exact file the user chose. The importer now probes **both** roots — the sandbox `$HOME/.ssh` and the real host `~/.ssh` recovered via a new `flatpak::host_home_dir()` helper (which strips a trailing `.var/app/<app-id>` segment from the sandbox home). Outside Flatpak behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged. The two code paths' differing group names ("SSH Config Import" vs the manual "config Import") were only cosmetic and are untouched. (#368)
