- **Import no longer claims success when it found nothing** — an import that produced zero connections, zero groups and no errors or warnings still showed "Successfully imported 0 connection(s)", which is what made the broken default SSH-config import read as working. That case now says "Nothing was imported — no connections were found in the source." so an empty import is never mistaken for a successful one. (#368)
- **Header bar height now matches other GNOME apps** — the top bar was visibly taller than every other GNOME application and out of step with the platform (reported on #369). The icon-only header buttons carried a `min-height: 44px` floor — the HIG Pointer & Touch *touch* tap-target — forcing the whole `AdwHeaderBar` taller than native, since the bar sizes to its tallest child and libadwaita's own flat header buttons are 34px. GTK CSS cannot distinguish a pointer session from a touch one, so the 44px floor regressed the common desktop case. The `.header-icon-button` floor is now 34px (libadwaita's native size), a comfortable pointer target that matches the surrounding window chrome; deliberate compact (`window.compact-manual`) still trims to 28px and macOS keeps 28px. CSS-only plus comment updates; `check-css.sh` green and a GUI smoke confirmed a clean start. (#369)
- **The Shell button stays as an icon on a narrow window instead of disappearing** — a UI/UX pass on #369 found the local-shell button was dropped entirely by the narrow (≤ 600sp) breakpoint, even though there was room for its icon. Launching a local shell is a primary action and should not vanish just because the window is narrow. `win.local-shell` is removed from the narrow breakpoint's hidden set, and the button now shrinks to **icon-only** there: its "Shell" text label collapses while the terminal icon stays. The same icon-only treatment already applies in compact mode. The collapse is width-driven (a `.narrow` window class toggled on the breakpoint) so it works regardless of the compact preference, and the narrow minimum-width floor now reserves room for the icon.
- **Auto-compact no longer trips on a normal-height window** — auto-compact engaged when the window was ≤ 800px *tall*, which flipped an ordinary 1080p laptop into compact chrome the moment its window was tiled to half-height, with no action from the user. GNOME HIG adapts primarily on width, so the height trigger is lowered to 600px (a genuine short-screen safety net) and width (≤ 900px) stays the main criterion.
- **The stale "import 0 = success" unit test is fixed** — `format_summary`'s empty-result branch was changed to say "Nothing was imported…" (above), but its unit test still asserted the old "Successfully imported 0 connection(s)" wording and failed. The test now asserts the new message.

### Changed
- **Compact-interface settings polish** — in Settings → Interface, "Compact automatically on small windows" is now greyed out while "Compact interface" (manual) is on, because manual compact already forces it everywhere and the auto toggle could change nothing — a GNOME HIG dependent-row relationship that two independent-looking switches hid. The "Compact interface" subtitle drops the confusing "useful on … macOS and KDE" phrasing for a plain description. The local-shell action also gains a discoverable `Ctrl+Shift+D` default accelerator entry (already in the keybinding registry; surfaced in the Keyboard Shortcuts overlay).
- **Touch-safe compact header targets** — compact-mode header and sidebar-bottom buttons now keep a 24px (HIG minimum) tap target under *auto*-compact (which may be a touch convertible) and only drop to the denser 20px under deliberate `window.compact-manual`, mirroring the existing 34/28px split on the regular header buttons.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: serde_spanned 1.1.1 → 1.1.2, toml 1.1.6 → 1.1.7, toml_datetime 1.1.1 → 1.1.2, toml_edit 0.25.15 → 0.25.16, toml_parser 1.1.3 → 1.1.4, toml_writer 1.1.2 → 1.1.3, zerocopy 0.8.61 → 0.8.62, zerocopy-derive 0.8.61 → 0.8.62. All semver-compatible patch bumps (the TOML parser family and the zerocopy pair); no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). CLI download versions all current (TigerVNC pinned at 1.16.2). picky-krb 0.12.5 remains held back for the same reason as 0.23.1–0.23.4: its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3, which does not match it exhaustively, so picky-krb stays at 0.12.4.

## [0.23.4] - 2026-10-08

### Added
- **A connection can request an Admin/console RDP session (`/admin`)** — a new **Admin/console session (`/admin`)** switch in the RDP options of the connection editor. An RDS-licensing host fails the embedded IronRDP licence exchange (upstream IronRDP #1629: a `SERVER_NEW_LICENSE` `securityHeaderFlags` decode error), and even after the external-client hand-off a plain FreeRDP connect can hit the same wall. A console/admin session (`mstsc /admin`, FreeRDP `/admin`) connects to the server's console/administrative session, which does not consume an RDS CAL, so the licence exchange never happens — the trick the reporter suggested on #366. The flag is a per-connection `admin_session: bool` (`#[serde(default)]`, so profiles written before this load unchanged) threaded from the persisted model through the embedded config into the external `FreeRdpConfig`, which emits `/admin` only when the switch is on. External FreeRDP only for now — the embedded IronRDP path has no admin-session flag yet (IronRDP #1629), noted in a code comment. (#366)
- **A pinned tab stays pinned across a restart** — pinning is live state of the tab's page in the `AdwTabView` ("Pin Tab" / "Unpin Tab" in the tab context menu calls `set_page_pinned` on the page and nothing else), so the session-restore snapshot had no way to carry it and every pinned tab came back ordinary after a restart — closable again, and closing it lost the session the user had deliberately kept. `SessionRestoreData` gains a `pinned: bool` alongside the tab group it already carried, written from a new `TerminalNotebook::is_session_pinned` (which reads the page, the same place the menu writes it) and reapplied through a matching `set_session_pinned`. A local shell restores synchronously, so its pin is set straight after the tab opens; an asynchronous connection start re-applies it through the same `SessionStartObserver` that already reapplies the tab group, rather than a second observer racing the first for one completion. `#[serde(default)]` keeps snapshots written before this field loadable — they restore unpinned. The pin travels with session restore, so it needs Settings → Interface → "Restore sessions on startup" enabled. (#367)

### Fixed
- **Vault root-search now matches a connection's name and host, not only its UUID** — the "search the vault from root" option widened *where* it looked (the whole vault) but still only matched an entry whose title equalled the RustConn connection's internal **UUID**. A user whose existing KeePass/Bitwarden entries are titled by hostname or display name never matched, so the feature read as "doesn't work with keepass/bitwarden" (reported by @EddieSteele73 on #353). Root-search now also matches by the connection's **name** and **host**: a shared `LookupIdentity { key, name, host }` carries the candidates, matched case-insensitively and whitespace-trimmed as an **exact** token (never a substring, so a short name can't surface an unrelated credential), tried in priority order — UUID first for back-compat, then host (more specific than a hand-entered name), then name. All five root-search backends (Bitwarden, KeePass/kdbx, 1Password, pass, Passbolt) honour the wider match through a `retrieve_identity` override; a backend that does not opt in, and any lookup with root-search disabled, is byte-for-byte unchanged (the default delegates to the UUID-keyed path). The `RustConn/`-scoped lookup stays UUID-only — only the whole-vault fallback widens. (#353)

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: async-recursion 1.1.1 → 1.2.0, cc 1.5.1 → 1.6.0, font-types 0.12.5 → 0.12.6, mio 1.2.3 → 1.2.4, objc2 0.6.4 → 0.6.5, powerfmt 0.2.0 → 0.2.1, tokio 1.53.1 → 1.53.2, uuid 1.26.1 → 1.27.0. All semver-compatible patch/minor bumps; no security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). picky-krb 0.12.5 remains held back for the same reason as 0.23.1–0.23.3: its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3, which does not match it exhaustively, so picky-krb stays at 0.12.4.

## [0.23.3] - 2026-10-07

### Fixed
- **Compact mode now actually shrinks the header bar on Linux** — the icon-only header buttons (content header: sidebar-toggle, menu, settings, split-H, split-V; sidebar header: search, menu) carried a hard Rust `set_size_request(44, 44)` as their GNOME-HIG tap-target floor. A GTK size request is a floor CSS cannot cross, and `AdwHeaderBar` takes its height from its tallest child, so on Linux the `window.compact headerbar` rules were dead — the header stayed ~44 px tall in compact (macOS was unaffected, its floor is 28 px). The 44 px floor moved to a `.header-icon-button` CSS class, so it still holds by default but can now be overridden. A new `window.compact-manual` class (added only when the user *deliberately* turns compact on via the toggle/menu/shortcut, not when auto-compact engages on a small window) lowers the floor to 28 px, so a deliberate compact finally shrinks the header. Auto-compact — which may be a tablet or touch convertible — keeps the full 44 px tap target. The now-unused `HEADER_BUTTON_SIZE` constant is removed.
- **Compact mode: welcome rows, bulk-actions bar and the banner note made consistent** — a compact-mode UI audit found three density inconsistencies on top of the sidebar bottom-strip already fixed. (1) The Welcome-tab rows (`list.boxed-list row`) had a `window.macos` slim rule but no `window.compact` counterpart, so the one surface that most needs vertical savings on a small screen stayed at the full ~56 px height while the header, tabs and sidebar shrank — a mirrored `window.compact list.boxed-list row` rule now slims them. (2) The sidebar bulk-actions bar (shown in group-ops mode) kept its full margins and its `.bulk-action` pill metrics in compact; `window.compact .bulk-actions-bar` / `.bulk-action` rules now match compact density (no reserved-height trap — the revealer collapses cleanly when hidden). (3) The CSS note warning against a `min-height` on `banner` referred to "two banners"; there are actually five (config, sync, secret, touch, group-broadcast), so the note is corrected to prevent a future five-fold phantom-band regression. CSS-only; `check-css.sh` green.

- **External FreeRDP no longer exits 255 after an embedded-client hand-off because of a starved version probe** — when the embedded IronRDP client cannot complete a session (RDS licensing on Windows Server 2019, or a TLS-only server such as KDE's KRdp) it hands off to the external FreeRDP client. Since the #351 FreeRDP-3 gate, that launch is only allowed for a client *proven* to be FreeRDP 3, decided by a `--version` probe — and the probe was fragile: its 2 s timeout was too short for a slow `--version` (notably when relayed out of a Flatpak sandbox via `flatpak-spawn --host`), and a timed-out/unparseable probe was *cached as "unknown" for the whole process*. A client whose version stayed unknown was then trusted purely by its `3`-suffixed name, so a FreeRDP **2** behind a Debian/Ubuntu `xfreerdp3` alternative was launched with a `/args-from:` command line it rejects → exit 255. The probe timeout is raised to 5 s and a failed probe is no longer cached (the next attempt re-probes instead of being poisoned), so a healthy FreeRDP 3 is now reliably identified; a launch that still falls back to trusting the name alone logs a warning naming the exit-255 risk. Name-suffix trust is intentionally kept (refusing it would turn a slow-but-healthy FreeRDP 3 into a spurious "install FreeRDP 3" error).

- **Compact mode: the empty strip below the sidebar's bottom toolbar is gone** — in compact interface mode an empty horizontal band sat under the sidebar's bottom icon row (clock / sort / key / file). `AdwToolbarView` wraps its bottom bar in a `GtkRevealer` and libadwaita puts the `.bottom-bar` style class on the inner `GtkWindowHandle`, so the existing `toolbarview > .bottom-bar` compact rules collapsed the handle but never the enclosing revealer — whose own themed `min-height` stayed reserved as the empty strip. A new `window.compact .sidebar toolbarview > revealer { min-height: 0 }` rule collapses that revealer so it sizes to the already-shrunk bar inside it. The icons (inside the handle) and window-dragging (the `GtkWindowHandle` is untouched) are unaffected, and the rule is gated on compact only, so normal density is unchanged. Not platform-specific — fixes every package and the Flatpak alike.
- **Snap build reverted from core26 back to core24** — the 0.23.2 snap migration to `base: core26` (for libadwaita 1.8 parity) never built: the snapcraft `gnome` extension, which provides the whole GTK4 platform, still rejects `base: core26` even on current stable Snapcraft 9.1.3 (`Extension 'gnome' does not support base: 'core26'`). Snapcraft 9.0 added the core26 *base*, but not core26 support in the *gnome extension*, so the published 0.23.2 release notes' "Snap migrated to the core26 base" claim could not hold. The snap returns to `base: core24` + the `gnome-46-2404` platform and drops `--features adw-1-8` (back to the libadwaita 1.5 cfg-gated fallbacks), the last known-good configuration. The Flatpak keeps its GNOME 51 / libadwaita 1.8 build; only the Snap is affected. Revisit once a snapcraft release lists core26 among the gnome extension's supported bases (snapcraft#6185, issue #174).

### Changed
- **CI: informational macOS Homebrew build fixed, and false-positive CI annotations silenced** — the advisory `Build from source` step now installs the formula through a real local tap with the published tarball's real SHA-256 (a bare-path install is rejected by modern Homebrew, and a placeholder hash fails the download check); the advisory semver job clears its stale baseline-checkout cache to stop ~20 spurious `ENOENT` annotations; and `taiki-e/install-action` is bumped to v2.87.25 so `cargo-semver-checks` installs from a prebuilt instead of a slow binstall fallback. All three jobs are non-gating; this only removes red annotations that masked real ones.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: either 1.18.0 → 1.19.0, h2 0.4.19 → 0.4.20, hyper 1.11.1 → 1.12.0, zeroize 1.9.0 → 1.9.1. Flatpak/Flathub `cargo-sources.json` regenerated to match. No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). picky-krb 0.12.5 remains held back for the same reason as 0.23.1/0.23.2: its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3, which does not match it exhaustively.

## [0.23.2] - 2026-10-06

### Fixed
- **The high-contrast tab-bar line is neutralised on GTK < 4.22 as well** — the override that repaints the tab bar's dividing line in the window tone (so it blends into the content the way Nautilus' does) was wrapped in a `@media (prefers-contrast: more)` block, which only GTK 4.22+ parses. On older GTK (4.14 on Ubuntu 24.04, and every distro still shipping it) the whole block was rejected as an unknown `@`-rule, so a high-contrast session kept libadwaita's lighter rule under the tabs. The override now out-specifies libadwaita's own `tabbar .box` rule with a `window tabbar .box` selector instead of a media query, so it wins in both normal and high-contrast modes on every supported GTK (4.14 through 4.24) with no media-query dependency.

### Changed
- **Compact mode density overhaul** — in compact mode the sidebar title ("Connections") is now hidden, header bars shrink from 32 px to 28 px, toolbar buttons from 24 px to 20 px, tab items from 28 px to 24 px (with a proportionally smaller font), sidebar rows from 28 px to 24 px, the search entry from 32 px to 28 px (inner from 26 px to 22 px), popover items from 26 px to 22 px (with a smaller font), the monitoring bar from 22 px to 18 px, and embedded toolbar buttons from 24 px to 20 px. The bottom-bar icon float is fixed by adding explicit box margins and a reduced bar height. Two sidebar header buttons (search, menu) retain their Rust `set_size_request` floor (28 px on macOS, 44 px on Linux) and are not affected by these CSS changes.
- **macOS dialog button placement** — on macOS, action buttons (Save, Test, Create, Rename, Move, Connect, Export, Import, Apply, etc.) in all custom dialog headers are now placed on the right side (`pack_end`) away from the window-close button on the left, reducing the risk of accidentally closing a dialog instead of confirming an action. A new `dialog_pack_action()` helper in `dialogs/widgets.rs` centralises the platform check (`#[cfg(target_os = "macos")]`). The connection editor's two-button header (Test + Create) packs the primary action outermost on both platforms. Cancel-only buttons (credential transfer, SSH agent, portable passphrase change) are intentionally left on the start side. 24 files, 26 call sites updated.

### Dependencies
- **Feature ladder raised** — added `adw-1-10` and `gtk-4-24` rungs to `Cargo.toml` features (pure version-ceiling bumps; the crate does not call any 1.10- or 4.24-specific API). The Homebrew formula, CI release workflow, OBS `debian.rules`, OBS `README.md`, and all three Flatpak manifests are updated to match. Flatpak runtime bumped from GNOME 50 to GNOME 51; bundled VTE raised from 0.80.5 to 0.84.1 (the `< 0.81` ceiling is removed). The `macos-build.sh` feature set now includes `adw-1-10,gtk-4-24`.
- **Snap migrated to the core26 base (libadwaita 1.8 parity)** — the Snap package moves from `base: core24` (GNOME 46 platform, libadwaita 1.5 with fallbacks) to `base: core26` (the `gnome-core26` platform, GTK 4.22 and libadwaita 1.8), so the Snap now builds with `--features adw-1-8` and gains the same AdwSpinner/AdwToggleGroup/AdwShortcutsDialog polish the Flatpak already has (the same libadwaita widget tier as the Flatpak; the Flatpak additionally enables the `adw-1-10`, `vte-0-84` and `gtk-4-24` rungs, which the Snap does not; without `vte-0-78` the Snap keeps hiding the Command monitoring mode, which is a separate follow-up decision). The GTK4-VTE staging and the WebKit-6.0-absence web-embedded drop remain, because `gnome-core26` still ships only GTK3-VTE and `webkit2gtk-4.1`.
- **CI runners pinned to `ubuntu-26.04`** — the 13 Linux jobs in `ci.yml` are pinned from the floating `ubuntu-latest` to `ubuntu-26.04` ahead of GitHub's 2026-10-19 migration, making the runner image explicit instead of letting it flip silently. The release and packaging workflows (`release.yml`, `flatpak.yml`, `obs-update.yml`, …) are intentionally left on their current pins so release artefacts keep their reproducible build base.
- **Updated**: want 0.3.1 → 0.3.2 (transitive, via hyper). Flatpak/Flathub `cargo-sources.json` regenerated to match. No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). picky-krb 0.12.5 remains held back for the same reason as 0.23.1: its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3.

## [0.23.1] - 2026-10-06

### Added
- **A Local Shell tab can be given a name of its own** — right-clicking a Local Shell tab now offers **Rename Tab…**, which titles the tab with whatever is typed (*build logs*, *tails*, *irssi*) instead of leaving every shell titled **Local Shell**. The label names the tab only: no connection is created and the shell itself is untouched. Leaving the field empty restores the default name, because an untitled tab cannot be told apart from a broken one in the tab bar. The label is remembered across restarts with Session Restore, since the snapshot already stores each tab's title and the Local Shell now reopens under it rather than under a hardcoded name; a saved workspace carries it too. The item is offered on Local Shell tabs only — a tab with a saved connection is renamed by editing that connection, which keeps the sidebar, the history and every open tab in step. In the dialog, **Enter** commits the label through the same path as **Apply**, and the field takes the keyboard focus on open.
- **A tab's group now survives Session Restore** — the restore snapshot carried each tab's title but discarded its tab group, so a grouped tab (a Local Shell or a connection) reopened ungrouped after a restart. The snapshot now records the group alongside the title and reapplies it on restore — synchronously for a Local Shell, and through the session-start observer for a connection, whose tab appears asynchronously after credential resolution. The group is carried structurally, never baked into the stored name, so a grouped tab comes back under its base label (even one that itself looks like `[x] y`) with the `[group]` prefix composed once rather than doubled, and a snapshot written by an older version (which has no group field) still loads and simply restores ungrouped. A saved workspace reapplies the group for Local Shell tabs; a connection reopened from a workspace, and a SPICE connection whose pre-connect port check defers the tab, still restore ungrouped — their start paths carry no observer yet.
- **Opt-in directional tab-close items (Settings → Interface → Window)** — the tab context menu's close block stays short by default (**Close Tab**, **Close Others**, **Close All Tabs**), and a new **Show directional tab-close items** switch adds **Close to the Left** and **Close to the Right** for the workflow that closes a run of tabs to one side. Off by default, since shown unconditionally they lengthen the menu for everyone; the toggle takes effect on the next right-click, and a config written before the key existed loads with the items hidden.

### Changed
- **The sidebar's "⋯" menu carries the connection-management actions again** — the 0.23 redesign had moved **New Connection**, **New Group**, the **Tools** managers (Clusters, Workspaces, Snippets) and **Import/Export** out of the sidebar's secondary menu into the content header's primary menu, on a one-menu-per-window reading of the HIG. In practice that put the actions a user reaches for most two clicks farther away — across to the far header and down through a submenu — so they live in the sidebar "⋯" menu again, beside the list they act on, grouped as Create · Tools · List · Select · Sort. The icon stays the three-dot `view-more` (⋯), a panel-scoped secondary menu, not a second hamburger. The content header's primary menu still lists the same entries app-wide, so nothing was lost there — this is a deliberate, convenient duplication of the list-scoped subset. **Select Connections** and the two sort orderings remain.
- **The tab context menu is lighter and its Monitor control is now self-explanatory** — the **Copy** block (Host, Port, Address, Username, Password) was removed from the tab menu: it duplicated the sidebar connection menu's tidy `Copy ▸` submenu, and copying a connection's fields is an operation on the connection object, which belongs where connections are listed rather than on the live-session tab. The tab menu now carries only session actions plus **Edit Connection…**, which stays as the one way to reach the editor from an active tab regardless of the sidebar selection. The **Monitor** control is now a **Monitor ▸** submenu of radio items — **Off**, **Activity**, **Silence**, **Command finished** — with the current mode shown selected, so a mode is chosen explicitly in one click instead of being cycled blindly through a single click-to-advance label.
- **The tab bar reads as part of the window, GNOME-native** — the dividing line under the tabs is repainted in the window's own background tone (and its contrasting shade dropped), so the tab bar blends into the content below it the way Nautilus' does, instead of being fenced off by a lighter rule. Theme-driven, so it follows light/dark and any installed theme pack.

### Documentation
- **User Guide: "Renaming a Local Shell Tab"** — a new subsection under Organization, next to Tab Grouping, covers the **Rename Tab…** item: that it names the tab and nothing else, that an empty field restores the default, that the label survives a restart through Session Restore and a workspace save, and why the item is absent from a tab that has a saved connection. The Tab Grouping and Activity Monitor sections now describe the **Monitor ▸** submenu and note that a tab group is restored across a restart.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: spki 0.8.0 → 0.8.1, zerocopy 0.8.59 → 0.8.60 (with zerocopy-derive). Flatpak/Flathub `cargo-sources.json` regenerated to match. No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). (picky-krb 0.12.5 still held back: its `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3, which does not match it exhaustively.)

## [0.23.0] - 2026-10-05

### Added
- **GNOME HIG sidebar/header redesign** — the window is now built as two real panels (sidebar + content), each with its own headerbar, the way GNOME Files and Settings are. The sidebar header carries a search toggle and a secondary "⋯" menu for the connection list itself (Select Connections, Sort Alphabetically, Sort by Recent Use); creating connections, the managers and import/export stay in the one primary menu. The content header carries the session actions and an optional group-path subtitle for the active connection ("Show hierarchy path in header", off by default). The protocol-filter pills collapse into one Filter button in the search row, which turns accent-coloured while a filter is on; the bottom toolbar is five direct icons. Window controls follow the system button layout and stay at the window edges whether the sidebar is shown or hidden, and fullscreen hides the sidebar together with the header and tab bar.
- **Vault read-only mode and search-from-root for every secret backend (issue [#353](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/353))** — Bitwarden, 1Password, Passbolt, `pass` and KeePass/kdbx each gained a "Read-only mode" and a "Search from vault root" toggle in Preferences → Secrets. Read-only refuses every write — save, delete, rename/move migrations, paste copies and credential transfers, in the GUI and in `rustconn-cli` — before any CLI runs, and the refusal is reported rather than routed around: a read-only vault is never silently replaced by the encrypted-file or keyring fallback. Search-from-root is read-widening and scoped-first, so credentials stored outside RustConn's own group are found without ever changing where writes land; outside that group an entry is matched by name, and when several entries in different folders share the name the first one found is used (the switch subtitle says so).
- **On-demand Cisco OpenH264 download for H.264 RDP GFX (issue [#262](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/262))** — packaged builds compile OpenH264 from source, which the loader refuses by SHA-256, so H.264 GFX silently fell back to a software codec. A new opt-in action (Settings → Connection → Media Codecs, plus `rustconn-cli codec download-h264`) fetches Cisco's official prebuilt blob over HTTPS on explicit consent (the dialog defaults to Cancel and links Cisco's binary licence), verifies its SHA-256, and installs it under the data directory — the Firefox pattern, so no patent-encumbered binary is redistributed. A "Use OpenH264" switch and a Remove button let it be turned off and back on or deleted without re-downloading, and it takes effect on the next RDP connection, no restart needed. The exact Cisco attribution appears in the Media Codecs row and in About.
- **Per-connection command macros and regex cluster auto-membership (asbru-borrow #1/#2)** — the Automation tab adds named single-line commands with a shortcut that types them into the terminal (pressing Enter unless "Run" is off) while that connection's terminal has keyboard focus — in a split tab, the focused pane's connection. `${username}`, `${host}`, `${port}` and your variables are filled in, a variable whose value is `@ask:` is asked for first, and `${password}` uses the password the connection was opened with; when none is available, or any reference is undefined, nothing is sent and a toast says why. Shortcuts need Ctrl, Alt or Super (F1–F24 may be used alone), may not reuse an application shortcut or another macro's, and are checked inline in the editor; commands may not contain line breaks or control characters. Macro shortcuts are inactive for RDP/VNC/SPICE/web sessions and while keyboard passthrough is on. A cluster can also auto-collect members by a regular expression matched anywhere in a connection's name or host (case-sensitive; `(?i)` ignores case), on top of or instead of ticked members: the cluster editor previews the match count as you type (also announced to screen readers), the cluster list and `rustconn-cli cluster list`/`show` count the resolved members (`show` marks pattern matches "(auto)"), and opening a cluster of more than ten members asks first, so an over-broad pattern such as `.` cannot open every connection at once.

### Changed
- **CI/release hardening** — tests run on `cargo-nextest`, a feature-powerset job and an advisory `cargo-semver-checks` job were added, caching moved to `Swatinem/rust-cache` behind a single `ci-success` gate, and every release now ships a `SHA256SUMS` manifest and an embedded dependency SBOM (`cargo auditable`).

### Fixed
- **YubiKey touch cue now fires on KeePass reads, not only writes (issue [#350](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/350))** — the three kdbx readers (connect, password load, root-search) were routed through the same chokepoint as writes, so the "touch your key" cue appears on every challenged read.
- **GFX frame accounting and H.264 fallback visibility (issue [#262](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/262))** — the frame-statistics counter is now fed from the real paint path (it was never recorded, so it read zero), giving the internal drop-rate diagnostics a real signal, and an RDP session now shows a toast (with an Open Settings button) when H.264 falls back to the software path instead of degrading silently.
- **The Welcome tab closes on the first real tab, not only the second** — the placeholder is now removed after the new page is appended and selected in every tab-creation path, so it never lingers beside the first session. It is recognised by a marker on its own page, not by its title, so a session tab that happens to be called "Welcome" (in any language) is never closed in its place.
- **Group Sync follows renames and moves on the Master, and rebuilds the nested hierarchy (issue [#263](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/263))** — a connection or subgroup renamed or moved on the Master is now renamed or moved in place on the Import side, keeping its id, its history and its vault credential, instead of being deleted and recreated. Import still gives every entity it creates an id of its own (the Master's copy can live on the same device, or reach it through Simple Sync), and records the Master's id in a new optional `sync_origin_id` field on connections and groups that it matches on first; trees imported by older versions are matched by name and path once and linked, and files without the field load unchanged. Subgroups are created at their real depth under existing groups — adding `Web/Prod` no longer creates a second `Web`, and a connection added to an existing subgroup no longer lands at the Import root — created groups keep the Master's description, icon and inherited settings, and a group name containing `/` stays one group. Everything is moved before anything is deleted, so a connection whose old group goes away is no longer stranded outside the Import group and recreated on the next sync, and KeePass and keyring entries follow sync-driven renames and moves of connections and groups (a read-only vault is logged and skipped). The GUI and `rustconn-cli sync import` now apply one shared plan from `rustconn-core` (`GroupSyncPlan`), so the CLI creates subgroups and applies renames and moves too, `rustconn-cli sync now` applies its imports instead of only reporting them, and sync reports count renamed or moved groups, so a sync that only renamed a group no longer prints "Already up to date".
- **A deleted or self-referencing jump host is detected instead of silently bypassed (issue [#345](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/345))** — when a Jump Host (the connection's own, a group's or the global one) names a connection that no longer exists, or the connection itself, the GUI now stops and asks before connecting without it ("Connect Directly", or "Connect Anyway" when a ProxyJump or the rest of the chain still applies; Cancel is the default), once per connection per session, and a cluster asks once for all its members. Only SSH, SFTP, RDP, VNC and SPICE are checked — the protocols that route through a jump host. The connection editor marks such a Jump Host row for SSH, RDP, VNC and SPICE until another is picked, and `rustconn-cli connect` prints a warning that the hop is skipped.
- **Import/export round-trip fixes** — CLI `export --format native` keeps templates/clusters/variables/snippets; Ásbrú import preserves the RDP domain and resolution and skips protocols that cannot round-trip; `.rdp` import preserves the three-way audio mode.
- **RDP: Multipath TCP and Kerberos NLA** — the MPTCP toggle now actually reaches the embedded client, `rustconn-cli add`/`update` can set Kerberos NLA + KDC address, and a Kerberos KDC proxy now reaches the external FreeRDP client too (as FreeRDP 3 `/kerberos:kdc-url:` for an MS-KKDCP `https://…/KdcProxy`), not just the embedded one (issue [#351](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/351)).
- **The stored password is sent to keyboard-interactive logins such as ESXi, not only to OpenSSH's own password prompt (issue [#364](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/364))** — the `SSH_ASKPASS` helper that releases a connection's (or jump host's) password matched only OpenSSH's `user@host's password:` form, so a server that authenticates through PAM keyboard-interactive — ESXi's `(root@host) Password:`, a plain `Password:` from a Dropbear or appliance — never received the password and the login fell through to `Permission denied`. The helper's matcher now mirrors the terminal-side one (`looks_like_password_prompt`): it accepts any account-password prompt, in the fifteen UI languages, while still staying silent for the prompts it must never answer — a key passphrase, a host-key confirmation, an OTP or verification code, a `sudo` prompt, and every stage of a password *change* (`old`/`current`/`new`/`retype`/`confirm`, including the Ukrainian and Russian forms), so the hardening behind issues [#191](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/191), [#254](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/254) and [#321](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/321) is preserved.
- **Config forward-compat marker now covers every config file** — history, tombstones, trash, snippets, clusters, templates and workspace profiles stamp the writing version and are backed up before an older version overwrites a newer file, including a save made without a prior load (such as `rustconn-cli history clear`). Downgrading to 0.22.15 drops a cluster's new `auto_membership` regex from `clusters.toml`; the file is backed up first, so re-upgrading restores it from the `.bak`.
- **Smaller fixes** — a split-pane scrollbar that could not be dragged (issue [#358](https://github.com/totoshko88/RustConn/issues/358)), a duplicated favorite on duplicate, a shared CLI-download checksum policy, the RDP/VNC Ctrl+Alt+Del toolbar button restyled from a filled block to a flat accent button to match the Shell button, and a session already shown in one split view is no longer offered in another's "Select Tab" (which could leave it visible in neither), with the move made atomic so a failed placement never strands it.

### Removed
- **"Color tabs by protocol" and "Show protocol filters" settings** — every tab already shows its protocol's symbolic icon, so the tab indicator only repeated it, and the protocol filters now live behind the sidebar's Filter button with nothing to keep shown. Both switches are gone from Settings → Interface; values saved by older versions are still read and kept, and simply have no effect.

### Dependencies
- **Updated**: async-recursion 1.1.1 → 1.2.0, cc 1.5.1 → 1.6.0, font-types 0.12.5 → 0.12.6, mio 1.2.3 → 1.2.4, objc2 0.6.4 → 0.6.5, powerfmt 0.2.0 → 0.2.1, tokio 1.53.1 → 1.53.2, uuid 1.26.1 → 1.27.0. Flatpak/Flathub `cargo-sources.json` regenerated to match. No security advisories affected (`cargo deny check advisories` clean). (picky-krb 0.12.5 held back: its new `GssApiMessageError` variant breaks the pinned sspi 0.21.3.)

## Older releases

Releases **0.22.15 and earlier** have been moved to a separate archive to keep
this file small. They are preserved verbatim (every byte of content; this was a
move, not a trim):

- [`docs/CHANGELOG-archive-0.1.0-to-0.22.15.md`](docs/CHANGELOG-archive-0.1.0-to-0.22.15.md)
  — 0.22.15 (2026-10-03) down to 0.1.0 (2025-12-01).
